## Extracting the shared text animation engine (AnimatedTextEngine.cs) from TextSlideRenderer

- **Feature/area**: `Musio.Core/Processing/TextSlideRenderer.cs` (existing) + new `Musio.Core/Processing/AnimatedTextEngine.cs`, on `feature/animated-text-overlays`. Goal: behaviour-preserving extraction of the tuned cinematic text-animation engine so a future `TextOverlayRenderer` can reuse it for animated text overlays drawn on top of video.
- **Approaches tried / what worked**: Moved verbatim (only accessibility changed) — `EntranceSeconds`/`ExitSeconds`/`TypewriterCharsPerSecond`/`TypewriterEraseCharsPerSecond`/`WaveHz`/`WaveFadeSeconds` consts, `ComputeInOutProgress` (now `public static`), `DrawAnimatedText` (now `public` instance method — the intended future extension point), `DrawPerCharacter`/`CharParams`/`ComputeCharParams`/`IsPerCharacter` (`IsPerCharacter` made `public static`), `ComputeWholeState` (`public static`), `DrawBlurredText`, `DrawCaret`, `TypewriterText` (`public static`), `CreateFormat`/`ParseColor` (made `internal static` because `TextSlideRenderer`'s background/measurement code still needs them cross-class), `WithAlpha` and all easings (kept `private static`, only used inside the engine). `TextSlideRenderer` now holds `private readonly AnimatedTextEngine _textEngine` built from its own `_device` and delegates via `_textEngine.DrawAnimatedText(...)`; static engine helpers are called as `AnimatedTextEngine.CreateFormat(...)` / `AnimatedTextEngine.ParseColor(...)`. Left in `TextSlideRenderer`: `RenderSlide`, `ComputeTextRect`, `MeasureTextHeight`, `ToCanvasAlignment`, background drawing (`DrawSlideBackground`/`DrawGradientBackground`/`DrawImageBackground`/`EnsureBackgroundLoadedAsync`/`DrawKenBurns`), the image/gradient caches, and `Dispose`.
- **New `ComputeEnvelope(anim, progress, durationSeconds, width, height) -> (Scale, Tx, Ty, Opacity)`**: lets a caller (e.g. an overlay's background box) move/fade in lockstep with the text without a full per-character transform. Rules baked in: `TypeWriter`/`TypewriterCaret` always report opacity `1` (the text itself is drawn fully opaque the whole duration in `DrawAnimatedText`, so a fading box behind it would look wrong); per-character animations report identity transform `(1,0,0,fade)` since there's no single whole-text transform, where `fade` is the standard `EaseOutCubic(inP)*(1-EaseInCubic(outP))` **except** for the continuous `Wave` animation, which instead mirrors `ComputeCharParams`'s own fade math (`Min(elapsed/WaveFadeSeconds, 1-outP)`) since Wave has no discrete entrance; all other (whole-text) animations delegate to `ComputeWholeState` and simply drop the blur term.
- **Confirmed dead code removed**: `TextSlideRenderer.RenderOverlay(CanvasDrawingSession, TextOverlay, ...)` had zero callers anywhere in `src/` (verified via repo-wide grep) — deleted entirely as instructed, since the `TextOverlay` record it depended on was being removed by a concurrently-running agent editing `TimelineSegment.cs`/`TimelineModel.cs`, and a proper `TextOverlayRenderer` is being written separately.
- **Unused-using cleanup**: Removing `RenderOverlay`/`CreateFormat`/`ParseColor`/easings from `TextSlideRenderer.cs` left `using Windows.UI.Text;` unused (no more `FontStyle`/`FontWeight` references there) — removed it; `Microsoft.Graphics.Canvas.Effects`/`Brushes` stayed because the gradient background (`BorderEffect`, `TurbulenceEffect`, `Transform2DEffect`, `ArithmeticCompositeEffect`, `DisplacementMapEffect`, `CanvasLinearGradientBrush`) still lives in `TextSlideRenderer`.
- **Verification**: `dotnet build` fails in this repo (WinAppSDK PriGen MSB4062, pre-existing/unrelated) — use VS MSBuild directly instead, e.g. `& 'C:\Program Files (x86)\Microsoft Visual Studio\2022\BuildTools\MSBuild\Current\Bin\amd64\MSBuild.exe' 'C:\Users\prmistri\source\repos\musio\src\Musio.Core\Musio.Core.csproj' /restore /t:Build /p:Configuration=Debug /p:Platform=x64 /v:minimal` (note: `C:\Program Files\...\2022\Community\...` does not exist on this machine — only the `(x86)\...\BuildTools` edition is installed). Build succeeded cleanly with zero errors/warnings referencing either file, even while the concurrent agent's `TimelineSegment.cs`/`TimelineModel.cs` edits were in flight — confirming the extraction is self-contained and does not depend on the in-progress `TextOverlay` removal.
- **What didn't work**: `dotnet build` failed with the known PriGen MSB4062 environment issue; the build playbook already requires VS MSBuild. Several old findings were rejected because current code or learnings show they are fixed or intentional, including editor preview generation races, UI-thread decoder disposal, per-frame export surface ownership, timeline geometry disposal, and the bounded app-instance redirect.


## Top stability and performance risks implemented

- **Feature/area**: XAML exception policy, recording capture/write lifecycle, webcam teardown, Direct3D interop ownership, timeline playback rendering, and image-background loading.
- **Approaches tried**: Split non-overlapping implementation areas across Opus 5 agents, integrated the shared worktree, ran two adversarial combined-diff reviews, corrected truthful frame-preservation reporting and retryable wallpaper failures, then built/tested with the ARM64 playbook.
- **What worked**:
  - Unknown XAML exceptions are logged but left unhandled; only a narrow allow-list of cancellation/shutdown faults is recoverable.
  - Capture callbacks copy into independently owned render targets and enqueue into a bounded writer; one worker serializes JPEG/gap writes, preserves CFR via duplicate slots, and exposes the first write failure.
  - Webcam stop has a watchdog and abandons a wedged driver call without blocking the rest of recording finalization.
  - Recording faults only advertise preserved JPEGs after finalization decides whether the directory actually remains.
  - CsWinRT `FromAbi` does not consume the caller's COM reference; release the returned ABI pointer exactly once in `finally`.
  - The playhead is a XAML overlay, so playhead changes move it without invalidating static timeline canvases.
  - Wallpaper loads are asynchronously prewarmed, device/path keyed, nonblocking during render, cancellation-safe, and retried with bounded exponential backoff.
- **What didn't work**: A first integration pass attached the frames directory to degraded faults before successful finalization deleted it. The first wallpaper cache also permanently latched cancellation/transient failures. Both designs were replaced and covered by regression tests.
- **Verified**: ARM64 VS MSBuild succeeded; full test assembly passed 568, skipped 2, failed 0.

## Preview playback and rapid-seek lag investigation

- **Feature/area**: Editor timeline scrubbing, preview render scheduling, MP4 frame decoding, and audio scrub feedback.
- **Approaches tried**: Traced pointer movement through `TimelineControl.PlayheadPosition`, `EditorPage.UpdatePreviewFrameAsync`, `VideoFrameReader`, `Mp4FrameSource`, and `AudioPlaybackEngine`; compared the current behavior with the prior MP4 preview-stutter findings.
- **What worked**: The root cause is an uncancellable single-decoder pipeline. Every pointer move updates the playhead immediately, but an in-flight obsolete MP4 seek must finish before the coalesced latest position can render. A stalled random seek can consume multiple 600 ms retries, grace periods, wake-step recovery, and drain delays; meanwhile playback advances far enough to miss the eight-frame step window and require another full GOP seek. The last presented frame remains visible throughout, which appears as a freeze. Synchronous `ScrubTo` stop/seek/play work on every pointer move adds UI-thread latency, and playback schedules preview updates both through the timeline property callback and the playback-tick handler.
- **What didn't work**: Treating this as playhead drawing/layout cost or raw compositor cost does not explain the multi-second freezes; the playhead is already an overlay and render requests are coalesced. Coalescing limits queue growth but cannot preempt the obsolete decode already holding `Mp4FrameSource._gate`.

## Preview seek fix design

- **Feature/area**: Behavior-preserving preview playback and scrubbing responsiveness.
- **Approaches tried**: Compared decode-quality reductions, longer caches, pure debouncing, additional decoder instances, and cancellation-aware latest-position scheduling.
- **What worked**: Keep exact decoding and composition, but make preview requests supersedable. Add preview-only cancellation through `VideoFrameReader`/`Mp4FrameSource`, safely abandon the current seek without allowing its late frame to satisfy the next request, render scrub positions at a bounded cadence, and force the exact final frame on pointer release. Suppress the timeline callback during playback-driven playhead updates so each tick schedules one render. Move audio scrub transport onto a latest-only throttled worker so pointer handling remains immediate.
- **What didn't work**: Reducing preview quality, changing GOP/frame accuracy, or removing audio feedback changes expected behavior. Coalescing without decoder cancellation still waits behind obsolete work; cancellation without stale-frame isolation risks presenting or exporting the wrong frame.

## Preview cache and reduced-resolution direction

- **Feature/area**: Revised preview-performance design after product preference clarification.
- **Approaches tried**: Evaluated enlarging the existing 12-frame source-resolution GPU cache, caching final composed frames, scaling decoded frames, and using a dedicated low-resolution preview proxy.
- **What worked**: Use a byte-budgeted LRU of low-resolution decoded source frames, preserving live composition of cursor, zoom, webcam, transitions, and styles. A 960x540 BGRA frame is about 2 MiB versus about 8 MiB at 1080p, so a 128-256 MiB budget can retain roughly 64-128 frames. For materially cheaper H.264 seeks, decode a low-resolution, short-GOP preview proxy; scaling only after full-resolution decode reduces composition and cache cost but not the GOP decode work. Keep cancellation/latest-request handling because cache misses and first visits still occur.
- **What didn't work**: A larger cache alone cannot accelerate frames never visited, and rapid long-distance movement has poor cache locality. Caching final composed frames requires broad invalidation and risks stale effects; an unlimited full-resolution cache scales to hundreds of MiB or GiB, especially with 4K and appended recordings.

## Reduced-resolution preview cache implementation

- **Feature/area**: Editor preview decoding, frame caching, playback scheduling, compositor sampling, and audio scrub transport on `feature/preview-proxy-cache`.
- **Approaches tried**: Considered a persisted short-GOP proxy file, then implemented a lower-risk preview-only decoder surface because it requires no project/package schema change or proxy-generation delay. Added a real MP4 integration test rather than relying only on dimension math.
- **What worked**: `Mp4FrameSource` now accepts preview options and copies frames directly into a bounded 960x540 GPU surface; `FrameCompositor` maps full-resolution zoom/crop coordinates into the reduced bitmap. `VideoFrameReader` uses a 192 MiB byte-budgeted LRU for preview while export keeps the existing full-resolution/count-bounded reader. Preview seeks use one bounded attempt without export's expensive recovery sequence, playback no longer schedules the same frame twice, and scrub audio is latest-only on a worker thread. A real 1280x720 MP4 decoded successfully into a 960x540 frame.
- **What didn't work**: Direct VS MSBuild invocation was intermittently denied by the shell; the documented `ProcessStartInfo` wrapper worked. The existing sequential/random MP4 frame-identity tests remain ignored for pre-existing decoder correctness defects and were not changed.
- **Verified**: ARM64 app and test builds succeeded; preview tests passed 7/7; full suite passed 518 with 2 existing ignored tests.

## Adaptive preview resolution

- **Feature/area**: Runtime preview quality selection and live reader swaps on `feature/preview-proxy-cache`.
- **Approaches tried**: Replaced the fixed 960x540 cap with 540p/720p/900p/1080p tiers. Initial quality uses source size plus logical processor count; runtime quality uses an EMA of user-visible playback frame time with asymmetric promotion/demotion thresholds and cooldown.
- **What worked**: Machines with 8+ logical processors start at up to 1080p, lower-capability machines start lower, sustained headroom promotes after 120 samples, and overload demotes after 12. Resolution changes open the replacement reader before disposing the current one, clear appended-reader caches only after success, and use generation/project guards across awaits. The controller proposes a tier and commits only after the reader swap succeeds.
- **What didn't work**: The first controller version committed its tier before the reader opened, leaving controller and actual resolution divergent after an open failure; changed to propose/commit/reject. Review also found a disposal race that could recreate an audio scrub timer after shutdown; the timer is now guarded and disposed under the scrub lock.
- **Verified**: ARM64 app/test builds succeeded; 12 focused preview tests passed before review fixes; final full suite passed 524 with 2 existing ignored MP4 identity tests.

## Shy preview playback chrome

- **Feature/area**: `PreviewCanvas` playback controls and adaptive-quality badge placement.
- **Approaches tried**: Moved the badge beside Play/Pause inside one bottom chrome and used a Composition opacity animation driven by preview pointer enter/exit plus keyboard focus.
- **What worked**: The full chrome (Play/Pause, adaptive tier, and timecode) fades in over 140 ms on hover and out over 280 ms after leaving. Keyboard focus keeps it visible. Hidden chrome disables pointer hit-testing, preventing invisible buttons or tooltips from intercepting clicks, while keyboard focus can still reveal it.
- **What didn't work**: The first version changed only opacity; transparent XAML elements still hit-test, so the invisible Play/Pause button could be clicked. Review caught it and `IsHitTestVisible` now follows the chrome state.
- **Verified**: ARM64 app build succeeded; full suite passed 581 with 2 existing ignored MP4 identity tests.

## Shy preview chrome Release deployment

- **Feature/area**: Clean ARM64 Release build, loose package registration, and local launch.
- **Approaches tried**: Removed the existing package before cleaning the exact Release outputs, rebuilt with VS BuildTools MSBuild, registered the loose manifest, and launched through AppsFolder.
- **What worked**: `Add-AppxPackage -Path <AppxManifest.xml> -Register` registered the fresh output with status `Ok`; the launched process was responsive with window title `Musio`.
- **What didn't work**: Adding `-DisableDevelopmentMode` rejected the loose manifest with `0x80073CF9` ("manifest is not in the package root"); omit that switch for this development registration.

## Rolling transitions in export (Task 2 of feature/segment-transitions): `SegmentFrameComposer.cs`, new `SegmentFrameComposerTransitionTests.cs`

- **Feature/area**: `src\Musio.Core\Export\SegmentFrameComposer.cs` (single-file scope; sibling agents concurrently owned `TransitionRenderer.cs` (T4), `TimelineControl.xaml.cs`/`EditorPage.xaml.cs` (T3/T5/T6), and other timeline files). Goal: replace the legacy `SlideTransitions`-driven crossfade block in `ComposeFrameAsync` with `TransitionResolver.Resolve` (from T1, already committed at `ea02aab`) and make the outgoing side of a dissolve ROLL past its own cut point (per `TransitionResolution.OutgoingLocalOffset`) instead of freezing, exactly as T1's doc comments specify.
- **Approaches tried**: Extracted the `switch (segment) { TextSlideSegment / VideoSegment / null / default }` body out of `ComposeSegmentFrameAsync` into a new `private async Task<CanvasRenderTarget> ComposeSegmentAtOffsetAsync(TimelineSegment segment, TimeSpan localOffset, CancellationToken ct)` that composes a NAMED segment at a local offset that may exceed that segment's own duration. `ComposeSegmentFrameAsync` now only resolves `(segment, localOffset)` from the timeline, throws for a null segment (that check cannot move into the shared method since the rolling/transition caller never passes null), then delegates. `ComposeFrameAsync`'s transition block now calls `TransitionResolver.Resolve(_timeline, outputTime)` and, when active, calls `ComposeSegmentAtOffsetAsync(resolution.OutgoingSegment!, resolution.OutgoingLocalOffset, ct)` directly — no frame-index round-trip, so the old "Floor not Round" comment (which existed only because the legacy path converted a frozen instant back into an output frame index) is now obsolete; replaced it with a short note pointing out that the offset-based path supersedes that concern entirely, so a future reader doesn't think it was overlooked. Passes `resolution.Type` and `resolution.EasedProgress` (not hardcoded `CrossFade`/`RawProgress`) into `TransitionRenderer.Render`, whose signature T4 leaves unchanged.
- **What worked (clamping bound)**: For the `VideoSegment` branch, extracted the pure arithmetic into `internal static (double SourceTimeSeconds, bool Held) ClampSourceTime(double sourceStartSeconds, double localOffsetSeconds, double speed, double availableDurationSeconds)` — computes `raw = sourceStartSeconds + localOffsetSeconds * speed` (same formula as the pre-existing inline code), floors at 0, and clamps to `availableDurationSeconds` (reporting `Held = true`) only when that bound is `> 0` and exceeded; `<= 0` means "unknown bound", so only the zero-floor applies. `Musio.Core.csproj` already declares `InternalsVisibleTo("Musio.Tests")` (confirmed by reading the `.csproj` directly), so no assembly-attribute change was needed to make the helper testable — kept it `internal` rather than `public` per the task's own stated preference. For the readable-footage bound itself, added `private static double ResolveAvailableSourceDuration(SourceContext context, VideoSegment video)`: prefers `context.Reader.Duration` (a pre-existing property on `VideoFrameReader` — `TimeSpan.FromSeconds((double)_source.FrameCount / _fps)`, i.e. the reader's ACTUAL captured-JPEG-or-decoded-MP4 frame count, independent of how the timeline trimmed the segment) when `context.Reader` is non-null and its `Duration > TimeSpan.Zero`; falls back to `video.SourceStart + video.SourceDuration` (the segment's own trimmed cut point, reproducing today's frozen-last-frame behaviour) only in the rare case `SourceContext.Reader` is null (the `SourceComposition`-only fallback path in `BuildContextAsync`, used when neither captured frames nor an openable MP4 decoder exist) — a `MediaComposition` has no cheap total-duration query without an extra async call this per-frame hot path can't afford.
- **Frame-ownership contract preserved**: kept the existing `try { ... } catch { frame.Dispose(); throw; }` around the transition-composition block in `ComposeFrameAsync` unchanged in structure (still disposes the un-blended `frame` on any failure, still returns ownership of the final `blended`/`frame` result to the caller), and kept `using var outgoing = await ComposeSegmentAtOffsetAsync(...)` so the outgoing frame is always disposed regardless of path.
- **`SlideTransitions` reference removed**: confirmed via grep that `SegmentFrameComposer.cs` had exactly one call site (`SlideTransitions.Resolve` in the old `ComposeFrameAsync` block) before this change; after the rewrite, `grep SlideTransitions` in this file returns zero matches, so the file's CS0618 warning is gone (verified: `Musio.Core.csproj` build produced zero warnings after the change).
- **What didn't work / traps avoided**: Initially considered exposing a richer clamp-result type (e.g. an enum) instead of a `(double, bool)` tuple, but a plain tuple was simpler to test and matched the task's own suggested `internal static ClampSourceTime(...)` shape closely enough; did not add complexity beyond what's needed to report "held" for tests.
- **Tests added**: new `src\Musio.Tests\SegmentFrameComposerTransitionTests.cs` (MSTest, `[TestClass]`/`[TestMethod]`, matching `TransitionResolverTests.cs`'s style) — 8 tests covering `ClampSourceTime` directly: normal in-segment mapping, past-cut-but-within-footage mapping (not held), past-available-footage clamping (held), unknown bound (`<= 0`) only floors at zero, speed factor applied (2x and 3x, including composing with the footage clamp), negative raw time floored at zero, and the exact-boundary case (`localOffset` landing precisely on `availableDurationSeconds`) correctly reporting NOT held. Did NOT attempt to unit-test `ComposeFrameAsync`/`ComposeSegmentAtOffsetAsync`/`ResolveAvailableSourceDuration` end-to-end — `SegmentFrameComposer` requires a GPU `CanvasDevice` and real video/JPEG media with no fake/mock substitute anywhere in the codebase, so actual rolling-dissolve rendering and reader-duration resolution need manual/GPU verification, explicitly called out in the test file's remarks rather than writing a hollow test.
- **Verification**: `MSBuild.exe src\Musio.Core\Musio.Core.csproj ... /p:Platform=x64` → 0 errors, 0 warnings. `MSBuild.exe src\Musio.Tests\Musio.Tests.csproj ...` → 0 errors (pre-existing CS0618 warnings only from `SlideTransitionsTests.cs`/`TransitionResolverTests.cs`, unrelated to this file). `dotnet test src\Musio.Tests\Musio.Tests.csproj --no-build -c Debug -p:Platform=x64` (with `$env:DOTNET_ROLL_FORWARD="Major"`): **741 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped (743 total)** — baseline 733 + 8 new `SegmentFrameComposerTransitionTests` tests, confirming no regressions from the refactor.

## T2 review fixes: same-source contiguous-boundary collapse + composition-duration fallback (`SegmentFrameComposer.cs`)

- **Feature/area**: Same file as the T2 entry above. A GPT-5.6 Sol code review found two medium-severity bugs in the initial T2 landing, both fixed in this pass without touching any other agent's files.
- **Bug 1 (the important one) — invisible transition on a contiguous same-source split**: an untrimmed split sets `incoming.SourceStart == outgoing.SourceStart + outgoing.SourceDuration`, so the outgoing side's rolled `OutgoingLocalOffset` maps to EXACTLY the same source-file instant the incoming side is already showing for the whole transition window (not just at the boundary) — the two "rolling" and "frozen" interpretations coincide, so `TransitionRenderer` blends a frame with itself and the dissolve is a silent no-op (CrossFade looks static; Push/Wipe show identical content sliding past itself).
  - **Fix — explicit same-source boundary policy**: added `internal static double CollapseContiguousSourceBoundary(double outgoingSourceTimeSeconds, string outgoingVideoFilePath, double? incomingSourceTimeSeconds, string? incomingVideoFilePath, int fps)`. No-ops (returns the outgoing time unchanged) when `incomingSourceTimeSeconds` is null (incoming isn't a `VideoSegment`, e.g. a text slide), when the two `VideoFilePath`s differ (`OrdinalIgnoreCase`, matching the file's existing path-comparison convention), or when the outgoing time is STRICTLY EARLIER than the incoming's (a genuine trimmed gap — rolling stays legitimate). Otherwise (outgoing meets or overtakes incoming) collapses to `incomingTime - 1/fps` (falling back to a 30fps step when `fps <= 0`, since `VideoSegment.Fps` defaults to 30 but is guarded defensively), floored at zero.
  - **Wiring**: `ComposeSegmentAtOffsetAsync` gained two new optional parameters (`string? incomingVideoFilePath = null, double? incomingSourceTimeSeconds = null`, default null so the normal per-frame path is unaffected) and now calls `CollapseContiguousSourceBoundary` on the `VideoSegment` branch's already footage-clamped `sourceTime` before composing. `ComposeFrameAsync`'s transition block computes the incoming side's mapped source time itself (only when `resolution.IncomingSegment is VideoSegment`) by re-deriving the transition's elapsed local time as `resolution.OutgoingLocalOffset - resolution.OutgoingSegment!.Duration` (guaranteed to agree with `TransitionResolver`'s own `local`, per `OutgoingLocalOffset`'s documented `outgoing.Duration + local` contract) and mapping it via the same speed formula, then passes both new args into `ComposeSegmentAtOffsetAsync`.
  - **New shared helper**: extracted `internal static double MapLocalOffsetToSourceTime(double sourceStartSeconds, double localOffsetSeconds, double speed) => sourceStartSeconds + localOffsetSeconds * speed` — used by both `ClampSourceTime` (outgoing side) and `ComposeFrameAsync`'s new incoming-side calculation, so the mapping formula is defined exactly once.
- **Bug 2 — null-`Reader` path silently disabled rolling**: `ResolveAvailableSourceDuration` fell back straight to the segment's trimmed cut point whenever `context.Reader` was null, ignoring `context.SourceComposition.Duration` (synchronously available, already used at ~line 896 in `ExtractFrameFromCompositionAsync`) even though it's a real, cost-free bound.
  - **Fix**: extracted the precedence rule into `internal static double SelectAvailableSourceDuration(TimeSpan? readerDuration, TimeSpan? compositionDuration, TimeSpan cutPoint, int fps)` — prefers `readerDuration` (no back-off needed: `VideoFrameReader.LoadFrameAtTimeAsync` already clamps its frame index to `[0, FrameCount-1]`), then `compositionDuration` backed off by one source frame (`1/fps`, or 30fps step) because `MediaComposition.Duration` is an EXCLUSIVE endpoint with no clamping of its own on the `GetThumbnailAsync` call path, then finally `cutPoint`. `ResolveAvailableSourceDuration(SourceContext, VideoSegment)` is now a thin wrapper passing `context.Reader?.Duration`, `context.SourceComposition?.Duration`, `video.SourceStart + video.SourceDuration`, `video.Fps`.
  - **Why extracted as a separate pure method rather than tested via `SourceContext` directly**: `SourceContext` is a `private sealed class` requiring a live `FrameCompositor`/`VideoFrameReader`/`MediaComposition` (GPU + real media) to construct, so the precedence rule itself needed to be pulled out to nullable-`TimeSpan` inputs to be unit-testable at all — matching the task's own suggested escape hatch ("extract that precedence into a pure helper if it currently requires a live SourceContext").
- **Tests added** (bringing `SegmentFrameComposerTransitionTests.cs` from 8 to 20 tests): 8 new `CollapseContiguousSourceBoundary` tests (exact-equal contiguous split collapses strictly earlier — the Bug 1 regression; outgoing overtaking incoming still collapses; a genuine trimmed gap preserves rolling; different source files are unaffected; no incoming-video-context is a no-op; differing `SpeedFactor`s on both sides still collapse correctly when their mapped times coincide; non-positive `fps` falls back to a 30fps step instead of corrupting the math; result never goes negative near source time zero) and 4 new `SelectAvailableSourceDuration` tests (reader wins over composition; composition wins over cut point, backed off by exactly `1/fps`; cut point is the final fallback; a zero — not just null — reader duration correctly falls through to the next preference).
- **Verification**: `MSBuild.exe src\Musio.Core\Musio.Core.csproj ... /p:Platform=x64` → 0 errors, 0 warnings. `MSBuild.exe src\Musio.Tests\Musio.Tests.csproj ...` → 0 errors (pre-existing CS0618 warnings only, unrelated). `dotnet test src\Musio.Tests\Musio.Tests.csproj --no-build -c Debug -p:Platform=x64` (with `$env:DOTNET_ROLL_FORWARD="Major"`): **813 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped (815 total)** — the total rose since T2 landed because sibling agents (T3-T6) landed their own tests concurrently; filtering to just `SegmentFrameComposerTransitionTests` shows all 20 passing, confirming both fixes and zero regressions.

## Floating rounded preview chrome

- **Feature/area**: `PreviewCanvas` playback chrome placement and shape.
- **Approaches tried**: Added a 12 px bottom margin and changed the container from top-only corners to a uniform pill radius.
- **What worked**: A 25 px radius matches half the chrome's rendered height, producing semicircular ends with straight horizontal sides while keeping the existing padding, controls, and shy behavior unchanged.
- **What didn't work**: An oversized radius such as 999 is normalized against both container dimensions by WinUI and turns the entire wide chrome into an oval instead of a stadium-shaped pill.
- **Verified**: ARM64 Release builds compile the corrected XAML successfully.

## Circular preview play button

- **Feature/area**: `PreviewCanvas` Play/Pause button shape.
- **Approaches tried**: Replaced the content-sized default button geometry with an explicit square hit target and half-size radius.
- **What worked**: A 40 x 40 button with a 20 px corner radius produces a true circular hover/pressed surface while preserving the centered 18 px icon.
- **What didn't work**: Padding alone allowed the default button template to render a rounded rectangle.
- **Verified**: The ARM64 Debug app build compiled the updated XAML successfully.

## Circular preview control Release deployment

- **Feature/area**: Clean ARM64 Release deployment after preview control geometry updates.
- **Approaches tried**: Pushed the UI changes, removed the prior loose package registration, cleaned the exact Release outputs, rebuilt, registered the new manifest, and launched through AppsFolder.
- **What worked**: The package registered with status `Ok` and launched a responsive Musio process from the fresh ARM64 Release output.
- **What didn't work**: Nothing.

## PR 66 review fixes

- **Feature/area**: Timeline playhead clipping, D3D11/WinRT device interop, and background-image cache invalidation.
- **Approaches tried**: Validated all three review threads against current code, fixed each at its geometry or ownership boundary, and added deterministic in-flight invalidation tests.
- **What worked**: Visible playhead offsets are clamped inside the track viewport; the D3D11 device is queried for `IDXGIDevice` before WinRT projection; cache loads carry generation IDs so invalidated or superseded completions cannot republish stale bitmaps.
- **What didn't work**: Clearing only the in-flight path/device is insufficient when a replacement load immediately uses the same key, because the older completion can match the replacement key.
- **Verified**: ARM64 Debug solution build succeeded; 20 focused tests and the full suite of 583 passing tests with 2 existing skips completed successfully.

## Editor graphics-device recovery

- **Feature/area**: Preview, filmstrip, timeline, appended-segment rendering, and adaptive preview resource lifecycle.
- **Approaches tried**: Added editor-level shared-device loss handling, coordinated GPU-resource teardown/reinitialization, exception-safe disposal for dead-device resources, bounded per-reader cache budgets, appended-preview LRU eviction, and adaptive reader validation before swaps.
- **What worked**: Recovery pauses rendering, invalidates all async generations, drops old-device frames/thumbnails/readers/compositors, rebuilds from the replacement shared device, regenerates filmstrips, and rerenders the playhead. A deterministic recovery lifecycle probe completed in about 500 ms while the editor remained responsive.
- **What didn't work**: Calling Win2D `RaiseDeviceLost()` directly on this packaged ARM64 setup returned `E_INVALIDARG`; invoking the same recovery lifecycle directly provided deterministic end-to-end validation. A single queued flag also dropped a second loss during recovery, so recovery now records additional requests and loops until stable.
- **Verified**: ARM64 Debug solution build succeeded; the full suite passed 583 tests with 2 existing MP4 identity skips. A clean ARM64 Release package registered with status `Ok` and opened a responsive project editor.

## PR 66 frame-callback fault retention

- **Feature/area**: `RecordingSession.OnFrameCaptured` error handling.
- **Approaches tried**: Validated the review thread against the free-threaded callback and existing `RecordFault`/`ReportFault` contract.
- **What worked**: Frame callback exceptions now flow through `RecordFault`, retaining the degraded session state and insulating the capture callback from throwing `Error` subscribers.
- **What didn't work**: Direct `Error` invocation bypassed fault retention and allowed subscriber exceptions to escape the capture callback.
- **Verified**: ARM64 Debug solution build succeeded; recording fault tests passed 4/4 and the full suite passed 583 tests with 2 existing skips.

## Preview reduced-surface CI reliability

- **Feature/area**: Reduced-resolution MP4 preview decoding test.
- **Approaches tried**: Inspected the failed GitHub Actions job and separated output-scaling validation from the production preview seek budget.
- **What worked**: The reduced-surface test uses normal recovery settings while still requesting 960x540 output; the existing options test independently verifies the one-attempt, 400 ms preview policy.
- **What didn't work**: Requiring a cold CI decoder to return its first frame within the production best-effort preview budget made the scaling test timing-dependent.
- **Verified**: The exact AnyCPU Release build succeeded, the formerly failing test passed five consecutive runs, and the full suite passed 583 tests with 2 existing skips.

## VideoWriter shutdown drain race and package version

- **Feature/area**: `VideoWriter` bounded shutdown queue ownership and app package versioning.
- **Approaches tried**: Validated the review finding against both timeout drain sites, retained bounded caller-side cleanup, and changed the channel contract instead of removing cleanup or allowing unbounded shutdown waits.
- **What worked**: The frame channel now declares multi-reader support because `AbortWriterAsync` and `Dispose` may drain pending frames while the writer releases its in-flight frame. A regression test locks that contract, and the manifest/settings version advanced past the published 1.2.0 packages to 1.2.1.
- **What didn't work**: Direct `dotnet test` attempted to build through the known unsupported PriGen path; VS MSBuild followed by `dotnet test --no-build` worked.
- **Verified**: ARM64 Debug solution build succeeded; focused queue tests passed 11/11 and the full suite passed 581 with 2 existing skips.

- **What didn't work**: Screen capture APIs omitted Mini mode because of `WDA_EXCLUDEFROMCAPTURE`, and `GetWindowRect` from a DPI-unaware PowerShell process returned undersized logical dimensions.

## Safe editor shortcuts and global hotkey removal

- **What worked**: Typing `S`, using Delete/Ctrl+X, and native Ctrl+Z/Ctrl+Y inside text and property controls no longer invoke timeline commands; timeline-focused shortcuts remain familiar. The app no longer reserves Ctrl+Shift+R/P/S globally before configurable hotkeys exist.

An ARM64-qualified `dotnet test --no-build` looked for a RID-specific test DLL that the solution build does not produce, so the verified no-build test command omits `--arch`.

## Global hotkey default recommendation

- **Feature/area**: Default system-wide recording shortcut.
- **Approaches tried**: Compared mnemonic letter combinations, Windows-key shortcuts, AltGr-sensitive Ctrl+Alt combinations, and modified function keys.
- **What worked**: `Ctrl+Shift+F9` is a reasonable opt-in start/stop preset because Windows does not reserve it, it cannot fire during ordinary typing, and `RegisterHotKey` supports it.
- **What didn't work**: No global combination is universally conflict-free; Win-key combinations are often OS-reserved, Ctrl+Shift+letter combinations are common app commands, Ctrl+Alt can collide with AltGr layouts, and F12 is reserved for debuggers.

## Win+Shift+X Mini-mode hotkey

- **Feature/area**: System-wide shortcut for revealing Mini mode without using the tray icon.
- **Approaches tried**: Reused `GlobalHotkeyService`, added `MOD_NOREPEAT`, registered Win+Shift+X only in the primary app instance, routed it through `ShellCoordinator.ActivateFromTray`, and exposed it in Settings.
- **What worked**: Win+Shift+X is not assigned in Microsoft's current Windows shortcut list and registered successfully on this PC. An end-to-end probe hid Mini mode with Esc, sent Win+Shift+X, and confirmed the same window became visible and responsive without starting or stopping recording.
- **What didn't work**: Microsoft reserves the Win-key namespace in principle, so this combination could be claimed by a future Windows release even though it is available today; registration failure remains non-fatal and is logged.
- **Verified**: ARM64 Debug solution build and 584 active tests passed; a clean ARM64 Release deployed with package status `Ok`, and the live hotkey visibility probe passed.

## Mini-mode reveal animation

- **Feature/area**: Mini window appearance when launched, restored from the tray, collapsed from the full app, or summoned by Win+Shift+X.
- **Approaches tried**: Added a root-content fade from 0 to 1 and a 12-DIP upward translate over 180 ms with cubic ease-out, gated by the Windows animation preference.
- **What worked**: The animation is prepared before `SW_SHOW`, only runs when transitioning from hidden to visible, resets cleanly after completion, and repeated hide/hotkey-reveal cycles leave the app responsive. Hiding the HWND before resetting animation values avoids a one-frame flash.
- **What didn't work**: The first automated Esc probe immediately after external process launch did not hide the pill because foreground activation had not settled; a subsequent hide/reveal cycle passed.
- **Verified**: ARM64 Debug solution build and 584 active tests passed; a clean ARM64 Release deployed with package status `Ok`, and two live Win+Shift+X reveal attempts completed with the final cycle fully verified.

- **What worked**: Text-input guards, primary-instance hotkey ownership, no-repeat registration, recording-state suppression, reduced-motion handling, and shutdown disposal are coherent.
- **What didn't work**: Save accelerators bypass the disabled Save button and can start overlapping writes to the same `.saving` path; the root fade/translate leaves the acrylic HWND shell static and immediately visible; Settings advertises Win+Shift+X even when registration fails and only a log is written.

## Hotkeys branch review fixes

- **Feature/area**: Save shortcut concurrency, Mini-mode reveal animation, and global-hotkey status reporting.

- **What worked**: Repeated save commands can no longer overlap or open multiple pickers; Settings warns when Win+Shift+X is unavailable; the complete acrylic window now fades from 0 to 255 and moves upward 12 DIPs over 180 ms, then restores its original extended style and final position.
- **What didn't work**: A root-element storyboard could not animate the separate system backdrop. The first external Escape probe did not hide Mini mode until the pill received a real pointer click, after which hide and hotkey reveal passed.

## Initial Mini-mode horizontal centering

- **Feature/area**: First-launch Mini window placement during its reveal animation.
- **Approaches tried**: Deferred reveal-target rebasing until visible layout, ignored initialization position events until placement completed, centered against the requested resize dimensions, and updated the active reveal target when the final AppWindow size event arrived.
- **What worked**: The final size-change event now re-centers the window and replaces the animation's stale target, so first launch finishes at the exact horizontal center while later reveals preserve a user-moved position.
- **What didn't work**: Rebasing immediately after `AppWindow.Resize` still used the old width because `AppWindow.Size` updates asynchronously; the later reveal tick then restored the stale X coordinate.
- **Verified**: ARM64 Debug build passed; the first-launch HWND measured a 0 px center delta, and a moved Mini window returned to the same X after Escape plus Win+Shift+X.

## Motion design fundamentals — camera motion blur + continuous zoom drift (research, pre-implementation)

- **Feature/area**: Design research for (1) motion blur on cursor + zoom/pan, (2) always-moving scene during zoom segments. No code changed yet.
- **Motion blur fundamentals learned**:
  - Physical model is the **shutter angle**. 180 deg = shutter open half a frame, so `blurLength(px) = velocity(px/frame) * 0.5`. At 30fps that is 16.7ms of smear; at 60fps half that.
  - Two techniques: **temporal supersampling** (render N sub-frame samples across the shutter interval and average - exact, covers scale/pan/rotate in one mechanism) vs **directional/vector blur** (one render + a convolution along the velocity vector - cheaper, but wrong for radial/zoom motion and prone to edge artifacts).
  - Sample spacing should stay **under ~1-2px** or the blur bands. Production sample counts are 8-16; 4 is a game-grade minimum.
  - Screen Studio splits blur into exactly three user-facing channels: **cursor movement**, **screen zooming in/out**, **screen moving while zoomed**. Worth mirroring.
- **Continuous drift (Ken Burns / living camera) fundamentals learned**:
  - Amplitudes: **2-8% scale** and **under ~10% pan** across a whole shot. "If the viewer consciously notices the drift, it is too much."
  - Organic float = **2-4 stacked sines per axis with different frequencies and random phases**, or Perlin with 2-3 octaves (lacunarity 2, persistence 0.5). Low band 0.05-0.15 Hz is the "float"; 0.2-0.5 Hz is "breath".
  - Use **incommensurate frequencies** on X vs Y so the path never visibly repeats.
  - Always ease; linear starts/stops read as robotic.
- **Codebase facts that shape the design** (verified by exploration):
  - Compositing is **100% Win2D (Microsoft.Graphics.Win2D 1.3.0) on the GPU**. `GaussianBlurEffect`, `Transform2DEffect`, `ShadowEffect` are already used in `BackgroundCompositor`/`TextSlideRenderer`. `DirectionalBlurEffect` is available but unused.
  - **`AutoZoomEngine.GetZoomState(t)` is a pure function of time** with no accumulated state. This means camera velocity can be derived analytically by sampling at `t` and `t - dt` (or at N sub-frame offsets) with **no frame history**, and it behaves identically for scrubbing, preview and export.
  - Two zoom draw paths: `FrameCompositor.ComposeFramePostCompositeZoom` (ZoomScope.Frame, crops the composited 1x `_compositeBuffer`, cursor is INSIDE it) and `ComposeFrameDirect`/`CropSourceFrame` (crops the source bitmap, cursor drawn after in output space). Any screen-blur work must handle both or it silently no-ops in one scope.
  - **Cursor motion blur already exists**: `CursorStyle.MotionBlurEnabled` (default false, no UI toggle) drawing 4 trailing ghost sprites in `CursorRenderer.RenderMotionBlur`. Velocity is precomputed per frame in `SmoothedPosition` and already scaled to output space in `RenderCursorOverlay`.
  - Settings records use `init` properties with defaults, and `MusioManifest` is plain System.Text.Json, so **new bool/record properties are additive and backwards compatible with no schema bump**.
  - `SegmentFrameComposer.SourceKey` is `(videoPath, BackgroundStyle, CursorStyle)`. Global-only settings on `CompositionConfig` need no cache-key change; a future per-segment motion override WOULD require extending `SourceKey`.
- **Constraints carried over from earlier work**: motion must be driven by **playhead time only** (a paused playhead must not animate - a repaint loop was explicitly rejected for text slides); pan amplitude must be **pre-bounded to the available slack** rather than relying on viewport clamping, otherwise the motion visibly stalls at the edges; and `EditorPage.SyncZoomStateToRenderer()` must be called after any preview-renderer rebuild.
- **What worked**: Splitting exploration into three parallel explore agents (zoom/compositor, scene-settings plumbing, cursor pipeline) produced a complete end-to-end map quickly.
- **What did not work**: N/A - research only. Custom HLSL `PixelShaderEffect` was ruled out up front because an earlier text-slide task already found the Win2D shader-compiler tooling fragile in this build environment.

## CursorRenderer.cs: shutter-model camera motion blur for the cursor (recovered partial entry — see recovery note below)

- **Feature/area**: `src\Musio.Core\Processing\CursorRenderer.cs` — replaced the old comet-tail cursor blur with a shutter-model implementation matching `MotionBlurSettings`, working concurrently with other agents editing `FrameCompositor.cs`/UI/tests in the same PR.
- **Approach taken**: `RenderFrame` now branches: if `motionBlur is not null && motionBlur.Enabled && motionBlur.CursorStrength > 0f`, calls new `RenderShutterMotionBlur` (relative velocity = own velocity − `cameraVelocity`, `travel = |relVel| * ShutterFraction / OutputFps * clamp(CursorStrength,0,4)`, `n = settings.ResolveSampleCount(travel)`, samples centred on the actual position spanning `-travel/2..+travel/2`). Otherwise falls back to the byte-for-byte original ghost-trail path when `motionBlur is null` (legacy/tests). Added public `CursorRenderer.OutputFps` (default 30) — **`FrameCompositor` must set this**; it wasn't wired in this task since `FrameCompositor.cs` was owned by a parallel agent.
- **Compositing fix**: N cursor copies are rendered into a cached/grow-only `CanvasRenderTarget` scratch buffer (quantized to 64px, capped at 2048px/side or ~4M px area) using **progressive averaging** — copy `i` at opacity `1/(i+1)` on a transparent target gives the exact mean via ordinary source-over compositing, avoiding the `1-(1-1/n)^n` washed-out look of naive equal-opacity overlapping draws. Falls back to direct `1/n`-opacity draws onto the session if the scratch target can't be allocated or the swept bounding box is too large; never throws.
- **What worked**: Deriving frame duration from `OutputFps` (new property, same pattern as existing `StartTimestampTicks`/`TickFrequency`) rather than threading fps through the `RenderFrame` signature, since that signature was already finalized by another agent. Matching `Color.FromArgb(0,0,0,0)` for transparent-clear (not `Colors.Transparent`, which doesn't resolve under the `Windows.UI` using in this file — build failed once with `CS0103` until this was fixed).
- **What didn't work**: N/A — single build error (see above) was the only compile issue, fixed immediately. No changes needed to any file outside `CursorRenderer.cs`; build succeeded (0 errors) via `MSBuild.exe ... /p:Platform=x64` with no `FrameCompositor.cs` interference at the time of this run.

## Recovery note: accidental `git checkout -- learnings.md` during the motion-blur-improvements branch work

- **What happened**: While updating this file with a PowerShell here-string (`@"..."@`), unescaped backticks (used for Markdown code spans, e.g. `` `CameraDrift.cs` ``) were interpreted by PowerShell as escape characters (e.g. `` `t `` became a literal tab), corrupting the append. The fix attempt used `git checkout -- learnings.md`, which — because this file had **uncommitted** changes from several concurrently-running agents on this same branch (this whole feature was being developed without intermediate commits) — silently discarded all of those agents' log entries back to the last commit, not just the corrupted append.
- **What was recovered**: The "Motion blur feature branch" and "Motion design fundamentals" sections were reconstructed verbatim from this session's own earlier tool output (they had been read before the accident). The CursorRenderer.cs entry above was reconstructed from a partial `Get-Content -Tail 5` capture (body bullets only — the original heading text is not recoverable and was replaced with a best-effort equivalent).
- **What was lost and could not be recovered**: Any learnings entries the `CameraDrift.cs`/`MotionSettings.cs`/test-writing agents may have added between the research section and the CursorRenderer entry were not captured in this session's tool output and are gone. `git fsck --unreachable --dangling` found no matching blobs (the content was never `git add`ed, so git has nothing to recover). No source files were affected — this data loss is limited to this Markdown log.
- **Lesson for future agents**: Never run `git checkout -- <path>` (or any other destructive git command) on a file that may hold concurrent agents' uncommitted work, even to fix your own mistake — it silently discards everyone else's changes, not just yours. If a shared file gets corrupted, fix it with a text edit (restore just the bad hunk) instead of reverting the whole file. When appending Markdown containing backticks via PowerShell, use a **single-quoted** here-string (`@'...'@`) or the `edit`/`create` tool, never a double-quoted here-string (`@"..."@`), since PowerShell treats a lone backtick as its escape character even inside markdown code spans.

## FrameCompositor.cs: camera drift + camera shutter motion blur (Feature 1 & 2 of the motion-blur branch)

- **Feature/area**: `src\Musio.Core\Processing\FrameCompositor.cs` only (exclusive ownership for this task; `CursorRenderer.cs`/`Musio.App`/`Musio.Tests` were owned by parallel agents in the same PR). Consumed the already-landed `MotionSettings.cs` (`MotionBlurSettings`, `CameraDriftSettings`) and `CameraDrift.cs` (pure `Evaluate`/`ApplyZoom`/`Window`).
- **Approach taken**:
  1. Extracted the inline zoom-state resolution in `ComposeFrame` into a pure `ResolveZoomState(double timeSeconds)` (cursor-index lookup + `GetZoomState`/cursor-center override, unchanged logic) plus a new `ApplyCameraDrift(zoomState, timeSeconds)` step that computes slack-to-source-edge on each axis, calls `CameraDrift.Evaluate` (phaseSeed fixed at 0 — `ZoomState` carries no segment identity), and applies the result via `CameraDrift.ApplyZoom` (never `zoom * factor`) while preserving `IsManualOverride`. Early-outs when `CameraDrift.Enabled` is false or `zoomState.ZoomLevel <= 1f` (drift's own `Window` is 0 there anyway, so this is a pure perf short-circuit, not a behavior change).
  2. Factored the post-composite-zoom crop math into `ComputeCompositeCropRect(ZoomState, viewport1x)` so it can be re-evaluated at arbitrary sub-frame times.
  3. Shutter accumulation (`DrawCompositeWithMotionBlur` for `ZoomScope.Frame`, `CropSourceFrameWithMotionBlur`/`DrawSourceCropSample` for `ZoomScope.Source`): evaluate the crop/viewport at the two shutter endpoints (`ResolveZoomState(t +/- shutter/2)`), map 4 corners plus center through each endpoint via small `MapCompositeToOutput`/`MapSourcePointToOutput` helpers, split the max corner displacement into a pan component (center delta) and a zoom component (`maxCorner - pan`), blend `PanStrength`/`ZoomStrength` into a `channelScale` that scales both the effective travel (fed to `ResolveSampleCount`) and the shutter interval itself — so e.g. `ZoomStrength=0` genuinely suppresses zoom blur rather than just reducing the sample count. If the resolved sample count is `<= 1` (the overwhelmingly common still-camera case), falls through to the exact original single `DrawImage`/`CropSourceFrame` call at zero extra cost. Otherwise draws N samples with progressive opacity `1/(i+1)` (exact running mean via ordinary source-over compositing, avoiding the washed-out look of a naive uniform `1/N`).
  4. Camera velocity for the cursor's relative-blur (`ComputeCameraVelocityComposite`/`ComputeCameraVelocityDirect`): finite-differences the crop/viewport one frame back (`ResolveZoomState(t - 1/fps)`), maps a fixed content point (the frame-time crop's own centre) through both the current and previous mappings, multiplies the delta by `OutputFps`. Returns `Vector2.Zero` when `timeSeconds <= 0`. Threaded through new optional trailing parameters on `RenderCursorOverlay` (`MotionBlurSettings? motionBlur`, `Vector2 cameraVelocity`) into `_cursorRenderer.RenderFrame(...)`, passed at both call sites (`ComposeFrameDirect`, `ComposeFramePostCompositeZoom`).
  5. **Cross-agent wiring**: the (recovered) `CursorRenderer.cs` learnings entry above flagged `CursorRenderer.OutputFps` (new public settable property, default 30) as needing to be set by `FrameCompositor` — added `_cursorRenderer.OutputFps = _config.OutputFps;` in `InitializeAsync` next to the existing `StartTimestampTicks`/`TickFrequency` wiring. Always re-check other concurrently-edited files' learnings entries for "needs wiring from FrameCompositor" notes like this — a parallel agent may leave a property unset that only your file can set.
- **What worked**: Building `EstimateShutterTravel`/`ResolveShutterSamples` as small `static` helpers shared between the Frame-zoom and Source-zoom code paths (parameterized via `Func<Vector2,Vector2>` mapping closures) avoided duplicating the pan/zoom-split + channel-blend math twice. Refactoring `CropSourceFrame` into `EnsureCroppedBuffer`/`ResolveCropInterpolation`/`DrawSourceCropSample`/`ToBitmapViewport` sub-methods let the single-draw and N-sample-accumulation paths share the exact same per-sample draw logic, which is what makes the `N<=1` fallback provably byte-identical to the pre-feature code. Rebuilding with `/t:Rebuild /v:normal` (not just `/t:Build /v:minimal`) after every substantive edit confirmed `0 Warning(s), 0 Error(s)` throughout.
- **What didn't work / traps avoided**: Almost implemented `ResolveZoomState` as a stateful/mutating helper by instinct (mirroring `AutoZoomEngine`'s internal spring state) — re-reading `CameraDrift.cs`'s doc comments up front clarified it must stay a pure function of time, since that purity is exactly what lets the shutter-blur accumulation sample `t +/- shutter/2` and `t - 1/fps` safely with no per-frame ordering dependency. Separately: corrupted this same `learnings.md` file mid-task via an unsafe PowerShell here-string, then made it worse with `git checkout --` (see the "Recovery note" entry immediately above for the full incident and the fix — use the `edit`/`create` tool for Markdown appends, not PowerShell here-strings).

## Motion blur + camera drift — integration review, two real bugs, and the compositing rule that matters

- **Feature/area**: Wrap-up for the `feature/motion-blur-improvements` work across `MotionSettings.cs`, `CameraDrift.cs`, `FrameCompositor.cs`, `CursorRenderer.cs`, the Scene pane UI, and two new test files. Written after an independent review pass; **corrects a claim made in the CursorRenderer entry above**.

### CORRECTION to the CursorRenderer entry above: progressive averaging is WRONG for sprites
- That entry states the cursor's N samples are composited with **progressive averaging** (sample `i` at opacity `1/(i+1)`, source-over, on a transparent target) and calls it "the exact mean". **That is false for sprites and has been fixed** to `CanvasBlend.Add` with a flat `1/N` opacity per sample.
- **The rule**: progressive `1/(i+1)` source-over only computes a running mean when **every draw fully covers every pixel already in the buffer**. Source-over leaves a pixel untouched wherever the incoming sample has zero alpha, so a pixel covered only by an *early* sample keeps that sample's **full** opacity forever — no later draw can fade it. For a cursor sweeping further than its own footprint (exactly the fast motion this feature exists for) that renders a hard, fully-opaque cursor at the leading end of what should be a smooth symmetric streak.
- **Correct technique for sparse/partial-coverage sprites**: additive blending (`CanvasDrawingSession.Blend = CanvasBlend.Add`) at a flat `1/N`, which accumulates `(1/N) * sum(sample_i)` in premultiplied space — a true per-pixel mean, bounded to `[0,1]` because each sample contributes at most `1/N` alpha. Composite the scratch target onto the session once afterwards with normal source-over.
- **Progressive `1/(i+1)` remains correct in `FrameCompositor`** (`DrawCompositeWithMotionBlur`, `CropSourceFrameWithMotionBlur`) because there every sample draws the *entire* opaque canvas over the *entire* destination rect, so the full-coverage premise genuinely holds. Both techniques are in the codebase on purpose; the deciding question is always "does every sample cover every pixel?".

### Second real bug: double-counted camera velocity in the Frame-zoom path
- The cursor's blur must use velocity **relative to the frame**, so the first implementation subtracted camera velocity in both zoom paths. That is wrong for `ZoomScope.Frame`: there the cursor is drawn **into** the 1x `_compositeBuffer`, which the shutter accumulation then smears wholesale — so the cursor *already* receives the camera's smear, and subtracting again double-counts it. It was also dimensionally inconsistent: `RenderCursorOverlay` leaves the cursor's velocity in *composite-buffer* px/s there (scaled by the 1x viewport), while the computed camera velocity was in *output* px/s — off by exactly the zoom factor.
- **Fixed** by passing `Vector2.Zero` in `ComposeFramePostCompositeZoom` and deleting the now-dead `ComputeCameraVelocityComposite`. `ComposeFrameDirect` still subtracts `ComputeCameraVelocityDirect`, correctly: there the cursor is drawn *after* the crop in output space, both quantities are output px/s, and nothing else smears it.
- **General rule**: before subtracting a "relative" velocity, ask both (a) what coordinate space is each term in, and (b) is some later stage of the pipeline already applying this term?

### Scene pane UI (entry lost in the `git checkout` incident above, reconstructed)
- Two global toggles + strength sliders live on `CompositionConfig` (`MotionBlur`, `CameraDrift`), **not** on `BackgroundStyle`. This matters: the Scene pane's usual `StyleToggle_Toggled -> ScheduleStyleUpdate -> ApplyBackgroundStyle` path routes into the selected segment's `FrameStyleOverride`, which would silently make these per-segment. Global scene settings must instead follow the `ApplyZoomScope` pattern (write `ProjectService.Instance.CurrentComposition`, then `InvalidateSegmentPreviews()` + `RebuildPreviewRendererAsync(config)`); `AspectRatio`/`FitMode`/`CropAnchor`/`ZoomScope` all do the same.
- Rebuild the sub-record with `config.MotionBlur with { ... }`, never `new MotionBlurSettings { ... }` — a `new` silently resets shutter angle, per-channel strengths, drift frequencies, etc. back to defaults.
- Controls need their own debounce timer (`_motionDebounceTimer`), separate from `_styleDebounceTimer`, and must honour `_suppressStyleEvents` so the programmatic sync during project load does not fire a spurious model write.

### Other things worth keeping
- **Blur cost is opt-out by construction**: `MotionBlurSettings.ResolveSampleCount` returns `1` below `MinBlurPixels`, and every call site falls through to the original single `DrawImage` on `1`. A still or gently drifting camera therefore costs nothing extra — and camera drift itself moves at roughly 0.2px/frame, so drift never trips the blur threshold. The two features compose for free.
- **Windowing drift by zoom depth is what makes it safe**: `CameraDrift.Window` is exactly 0 at 1x and `ApplyZoom` scales the zoom *depth* (`1 + (zoom-1) * factor`), never the zoom level. A plain multiply would leave the scene a few percent magnified forever after a segment released.
- **Preview vs export blur differ by design**: shutter length is `ShutterFraction / OutputFps`, and preview runs at a lower fps than export, so a preview frame represents more time and gets more smear. That is physically correct, but it does mean tuning blur in preview is only approximate.
- **Parallelising agents over one C# project**: give each agent an exclusive file, and have them build with *different* `/p:Platform` values (ARM64 vs x64) — output goes to `bin\<Platform>` / `obj\<Platform>`, so concurrent builds do not clobber each other. Also lock any cross-file API contract (here, the new `RenderFrame` parameters) *before* dispatching, so neither agent blocks on the other.
- **Verified**: Musio.Core builds ARM64 + x64, Musio.App builds ARM64 (only pre-existing MVVMTK0045 warnings), and all 628 tests pass (2 skipped), including ~45 new tests in `CameraDriftTests` / `MotionBlurSettingsTests`.

## Motion blur + camera drift — fixing three reported regressions (stagnation, double cursor, stutter)

- **Feature/area**: Follow-up on `feature/motion-blur-improvements` after the user reported (1) the camera stagnating at a point, (2) double/faded mouse pointers, (3) stuttering playback.

### 1. Camera stagnation — absolute-time oscillators are wrong for short segments
- **Root cause**: drift was driven by ABSOLUTE timeline time with a 0.08Hz pan (12.5s period) and 0.05Hz breathe (20s period). A default zoom segment is only **~3.9s** (pre 1.0 + hold 1.33 + post 1.56), and the drift window is near full amplitude only during the ~1.3s hold. A segment could therefore land on a **stationary point of its own sine** and the camera parked. Worse, whether it parked depended on where the segment happened to fall in absolute time, so it looked random.
- **Fix**: drive drift from **segment progress**, not absolute time. `ZoomState` gained `HasSegment`, `SegmentProgress`, and `SegmentHeadingX/Y`, populated by `AutoZoomEngine`. Drift is now a **monotonic smoothstep push-in** plus a **circular arc** pan (`sin θ`, `1 − cos θ`) traversing `PanSweepCycles = 0.3` turns per segment — both always advancing, never retracing. `ZoomFrequencyHz`/`PanFrequencyHz` were replaced by `PanSweepCycles`.
- **Lesson**: when tuning motion design against real content, check the oscillator period against the actual duration of the thing being animated. Ken Burns figures assume a 10-20s shot; a 4s zoom segment needs segment-relative parameterisation or it can sit on a stationary point for its entire visible life.

### 2. Two bugs the rework introduced, both caught by review rather than by tests
- **Silent drift kill**: `FrameCompositor.ResolveZoomState`'s cursor-centre override calls `AutoZoomEngine.ComputeViewportForCenter`, which returns a **fresh** `ZoomState` — dropping `HasSegment`/`SegmentProgress`/`SegmentHeading`. Since auto-zoom is the dominant path, camera drift was dead for essentially every zoom, with **no error and no failing test**. Now copied across explicitly, and `ZoomSegmentProgressTests` covers it. **Any struct field added for a downstream consumer must be audited against every site that rebuilds that struct.**
- **Arc magnitude broke the slack bound**: `(sin θ, 1 − cos θ)` is NOT a unit vector — its magnitude is `2·sin(θ/2)`, reaching 1.618 at the default sweep and 2.0 at half a turn. `BoundToSlack` clamped the per-axis *amplitude* as if it were the final displacement, so the offset could reach ~1.13x the available slack and drive `ComputeViewportForCenter` into its edge clamp — stalling the exact motion the bound exists to protect. Now the arc is normalised by its own peak (`2·sin(min(sweep, π)/2)`) so `|unit| <= 1`. **My own tests had baked in the 2x overshoot (`limit = 2f * slack * MaxSlackFraction`) instead of asserting the real invariant — a test that encodes the bug is worse than no test.** Replaced with `Evaluate_PanNeverExceedsTheAvailableSlack`, swept over headings.

### 3. Double / faded cursor — sample banding, not a double draw
- **Root cause**: `ResolveSampleCount` caps at `MaxSamples`. When travel exceeded what those samples could cover at the target spacing, the samples spread apart — and on a small, high-contrast, hard-edged sprite like a pointer, widely-spaced samples read as **a second faded cursor behind the real one** rather than as blur. Dense content hides this; sprites do not.
- **Fix**: never emit a smear that cannot be sampled smoothly. Both `FrameCompositor.ResolveShutterSamples` and `CursorRenderer.RenderShutterMotionBlur` now clamp travel/shutter to `(sampleCount - 1) * SampleSpacingPixels`. A slightly shorter blur is invisible; banding is not.

### 4. Stutter — interpolation quality was being spent on pixels about to be averaged away
- The accumulation drew each of up to 8 full-canvas samples with `HighQualityCubic`. That is a bicubic tap per sample on a 1080p+ canvas, ~4x the cost of bilinear, multiplied by the sample count, every frame of every zoom.
- **Fix**: accumulation samples now use `CanvasImageInterpolation.Linear` (the sharp single-draw path keeps `HighQualityCubic`); `MinBlurPixels` 1.5→2.5 so imperceptible smears cost nothing; `SampleSpacingPixels` 1.5→2.0; `MaxSamples` 8→6. Together roughly a 5x cut in blur cost.
- **Lesson**: match interpolation quality to what the pixel is *for*. Anything feeding a blur/average does not need high-quality resampling.

### Overlapping segments
- Auto-zoom segments overlap often (clicks can be 0.5s apart while a segment spans ~3.9s). `AutoZoomEngine` already weight-blends the focal point to avoid focal snaps; drift progress and heading now blend by the **same** activation weights. Heading is carried as a **vector, not an angle**, because averaging angles breaks across the ±π wrap. Fallback values are seeded from the first in-range segment, not the first to beat the zoom floor — at a segment edge every weight is 0, and the fallback would otherwise report a default heading.
- Seeds stay `(int)Math.Round(startSeconds * 1000)`. **Never use `string.GetHashCode` for anything that must match between preview and export** — .NET randomises it per process.

- **Verified**: Musio.Core ARM64 + x64, Musio.App Release ARM64, **643 tests pass** (2 skipped), deployed and launched cleanly with no new `crash.log` entries.

## PR #70 review: unused `timeSeconds` parameter on `ApplyCameraDrift`

- **Feature/area**: `src/Musio.Core/Processing/FrameCompositor.cs` — camera drift layering.

- **What worked**: Dropped the parameter and updated the single call site in `ResolveZoomState`. Purely mechanical; no behaviour change. `ResolveZoomState` remains a pure function of `timeSeconds`, so the shutter-blur sub-frame sampling contract is untouched.

## Implementing `TextOverlayRenderer` — clipped-to-text backgrounds for animated text overlays drawn on top of the video

- **Feature/area**: new `src\Musio.Core\Processing\TextOverlayRenderer.cs` (feature/animated-text-overlays, renderer-only scope; concurrent agents owned `TimelineSegment.cs`/`TimelineModel.cs`, `EditOperation.cs`, `MusioPathRewriter.cs`, `TimelineControl.*`, property panes). Public API: `TextOverlayRenderer(CanvasDevice? device = null)` and `void Render(CanvasRenderTarget target, IReadOnlyList<TextOverlaySegment> overlays, TimeSpan sourceTime, int width, int height)` — takes the render target (not an open drawing session) because the `Blur` background must read `target`'s current pixels.
- **Approaches tried**: Reused `AnimatedTextEngine.CreateFormat`/`ParseColor`/`ComputeEnvelope`/`DrawAnimatedText` (all shared with `TextSlideRenderer`) instead of duplicating text-animation math. All overlay pixel-valued properties (font size, corner radius, outline width, accent thickness, blur amount) are scaled by `height / 1080.0` so an overlay authored at one resolution looks proportionally identical at another. Text box size hugs the actual measured `CanvasTextLayout.LayoutBounds` (constrained to `WidthFraction * width`) plus `PaddingScale * scaledFontSize` padding, not the full available width — this is what makes the background "clipped to the text content" as required. `TextOverlaySegment.ResolveCenter(...)` resolves anchor → normalized center using the box's own normalized size, then the box is clamped fully inside the frame.
- **Blur background (frosted glass)**: **must** copy `target`'s pixels into a cached scratch `CanvasRenderTarget` *before* opening a drawing session on `target` for that overlay — Win2D forbids reading from and drawing into the same render target within one pass. Solved by doing the per-overlay `target.CreateDrawingSession()` *after* the scratch copy + `BorderEffect{ExtendX/Y=Clamp}` + `GaussianBlurEffect` graph is built (each overlay gets its own short-lived drawing session on `target`, opened only once its blur inputs — if any — are ready); this also means a later overlay's blur pass correctly samples any earlier overlay already drawn onto `target` this frame, since no session is open on `target` between overlays. The scratch target itself uses allocate-then-swap (`EnsureBlurScratch`, keyed on `(width, height)` + device) and is only allocated the first time an active overlay actually has `Background == Blur`.
- **Envelope transform placement**: the `(scale, tx, ty, opacity)` envelope from `AnimatedTextEngine.ComputeEnvelope` is applied as `ds.Transform` (saved/restored via try/finally) only around the manual background draws (Solid/Blur/GradientScrim/AccentBar) — never around `OutlineShadow`, because that mode has no box and instead calls `AnimatedTextEngine.DrawAnimatedText` directly for its shadow/outline passes, which already applies the identical envelope internally from the same `progress`/`durationSeconds`. This keeps the outline/shadow passes pixel-in-sync with the fill pass without double-transforming.
- **What worked**: Matched `TextSlideRenderer`'s conventions closely — `ObjectDisposedException.ThrowIf(_disposed, this)` on the public method, `using` on every `CanvasGeometry`/`CanvasTextLayout`/`CanvasTextFormat`/effect/layer, `# ─────────── Section ───────────` comment banners, and a plain `Dispose()` disposing only the cached scratch target (the `AnimatedTextEngine` instance itself owns no disposable state).
- **What didn't work**: N/A — no failed approaches this round; the design in the task prompt (per-overlay session opened after the blur copy) avoided ever hitting the read/write-same-target Win2D restriction in the first place, so no rework was needed there.
- **Build/test (verified)**: `& 'C:\Program Files (x86)\Microsoft Visual Studio\2022\BuildTools\MSBuild\Current\Bin\amd64\MSBuild.exe' src\Musio.Core\Musio.Core.csproj /restore /t:Build /p:Configuration=Debug /p:Platform=x64 /v:minimal` → 0 Warning(s), 0 Error(s), including a full `/v:normal` pass filtered for `TextOverlayRenderer|error|warning` showing zero hits — the new file compiles cleanly on its own even while other agents' concurrent edits were in flight.

## Wiring `TextOverlayRenderer` into the compositor/export/preview pipeline (`FrameCompositor.cs`, `SegmentFrameComposer.cs`, `PreviewRenderer.cs`)

- **Feature/area**: `src\Musio.Core\Processing\FrameCompositor.cs`, `src\Musio.Core\Export\SegmentFrameComposer.cs`, `src\Musio.Core\Processing\PreviewRenderer.cs` (feature/animated-text-overlays; model and `TextOverlayRenderer` already existed from prior concurrent agents). Goal: make previously-written animated text overlays actually draw in live preview, MP4 export, and GIF export, including on appended/imported (non-primary) recordings.
- **Approaches tried**: Added `FrameCompositor._textOverlayRenderer`/`_textOverlays` fields and a public `SyncTextOverlays(IReadOnlyList<TextOverlaySegment>)` modeled exactly on `SyncManualZoomKeyframes`/`SyncSuppressedClickTicks`, lazily constructing the renderer only when a non-empty list is set. Added a shared private `RenderTextOverlays(CanvasRenderTarget output, double timeSeconds)` helper (no-ops immediately when nothing synced) called from **both** `ComposeFrameDirect` and `ComposeFramePostCompositeZoom`, in each case placed *after* the `using (var ds = output.CreateDrawingSession()) { ... }` block closes (not inside it) and *after* any zoom is applied, with an inline comment at each call site explaining both reasons (frosted-blur must sample the finished/flushed frame; overlays must stay fixed on screen like the webcam/keyboard/subtitle overlays, never scaled by zoom). Disposed `_textOverlayRenderer` in `Dispose()` next to `_webcamCompositor?.Dispose()`.
- **Confirmed `timeSeconds` in `ComposeFrameDirect`/`ComposeFramePostCompositeZoom` is source-video time**: `FrameCompositor.ComposeFrame(CanvasBitmap, double sourceTimeSeconds)` does `double timeSeconds = sourceTimeSeconds;` and passes it straight through to both compose paths — it is never the *output*-timeline time. This matches `TextOverlaySegment.Start`/`Duration`, which are also documented as source-video time, so no conversion is needed before calling `TextOverlayRenderer.Render`.
- In `SegmentFrameComposer.cs`, extracted `BelongsToSource(ZoomKeyframe, string, string?)`'s body into a new private `BelongsToSource(string? sourceVideoFilePath, string videoFilePath, string? primaryVideoFilePath)` overload (the public `ZoomKeyframe` overload now just forwards `keyframe.SourceVideoFilePath` to it), then added `public static List<TextOverlaySegment> SelectTextOverlays(TimelineModel timeline, string videoFilePath)` — modeled on `SelectManualZoomKeyframes` — that filters `timeline.TextOverlays` through the same shared rule, so the ownership convention is defined exactly once. Both `SelectManualZoomKeyframes`/`BelongsToSource(ZoomKeyframe,...)`'s existing public signatures were preserved unchanged (verified no test breakage: `Musio.Tests/ExportZoomKeyframeSelectionTests.cs` calls both by their original signatures).
- Wired the actual sync call inside the existing private `SyncZoomState(FrameCompositor, TimelineModel?, string videoFilePath)` helper (adding `compositor.SyncTextOverlays(SelectTextOverlays(timeline, videoFilePath))` right after the existing `SyncManualZoomKeyframes` call, updating its XML doc since it now syncs more than zoom state) rather than adding a new call site — `SyncZoomState` is already called from `BuildContextAsync`, which is itself invoked both by `CreateAsync` (builds `_primaryContext`, covering the **legacy no-segments path** since it only guards on `timeline is null`, not `timeline.Segments.Count`) and by `GetOrCreateContextAsync` (builds every appended/imported recording's `SourceContext`) — so one edit covers primary, non-primary, and legacy projects identically, mirroring exactly how zoom keyframes are already handled.
- **Confirmed GIF export shares the same composer as MP4**: `ExportEngine.ExportGifAsync` (~line 137) builds a `SegmentFrameComposer` via `CreateAsync` and drives `GifEncoder.EncodeComposedFramesAsync(frameIndex => composer.ComposeFrameAsync(...), ...)` — the identical `SegmentFrameComposer`/`ComposeFrameAsync` path the MP4 exporter (`VideoEncoder`) uses. Since the text-overlay sync lives inside `SegmentFrameComposer`'s shared `SyncZoomState`/`BuildContextAsync`, both export formats get overlays automatically with no format-specific wiring needed.
- **Preview path**: `PreviewRenderer` wraps exactly one `FrameCompositor` for one source recording at a time. Added `public void UpdateTextOverlays(IReadOnlyList<TextOverlaySegment> overlays)` mirroring the existing `UpdateZoomKeyframes(IReadOnlyList<Timeline.ZoomKeyframe>)` exactly (`_compositor?.SyncTextOverlays(overlays)`), with its doc comment telling callers to select overlays via `SegmentFrameComposer.SelectTextOverlays(timeline, videoFilePath)` so preview uses the identical per-source ownership rule as export (a hard "preview and export must be pixel-identical" requirement in this codebase). This method's caller lives in `Musio.App` (out of this task's owned-files scope) — the `EditorPage`/preview-wiring agent must call `previewRenderer.UpdateTextOverlays(SegmentFrameComposer.SelectTextOverlays(timeline, currentSourceVideoFilePath))` (both public, in `Musio.Core.Processing`/`Musio.Core.Export` respectively) whenever the active preview recording changes and whenever a text overlay is added/edited/removed/reordered/undone, the same trigger set that already drives `UpdateZoomKeyframes`.
- **What worked**: Referencing a sibling namespace (`Export.SegmentFrameComposer`) from an XML doc `<see cref>` inside `Musio.Core.Processing` compiles cleanly with zero warnings — C#'s namespace lookup checks members of enclosing namespaces (here `Musio.Core`, which both `Processing` and `Export` nest under), the same pattern already used unqualified for `Timeline.ZoomKeyframe` in `FrameCompositor.SyncManualZoomKeyframes`'s parameter type. No new `using` directives were needed anywhere.
- **What didn't work**: N/A — the existing `SyncManualZoomKeyframes`/`SelectManualZoomKeyframes`/`UpdateZoomKeyframes` triad was a complete, directly-reusable template for every piece of this task, so no rework was needed.
- **Build/test (verified)**: `& 'C:\Program Files (x86)\Microsoft Visual Studio\2022\BuildTools\MSBuild\Current\Bin\amd64\MSBuild.exe' src\Musio.Core\Musio.Core.csproj /restore /t:Build /p:Configuration=Debug /p:Platform=x64 /v:minimal` and a full `/t:Rebuild /v:normal` pass both reported **0 Warning(s), 0 Error(s)**.
