## Adding `TextOverlayPresets` — one-click style presets for animated text overlays

- **Feature/area**: new `src\Musio.Core\Timeline\TextOverlayPresets.cs` (feature/animated-text-overlays, single-file scope; other agents concurrently owned `EditorPage.xaml.cs`, `FrameCompositor.cs`/`SegmentFrameComposer.cs`, and the test project). Public API: `sealed record TextOverlayPreset(string Name, string Glyph, Action<TextOverlaySegment> Apply)` + `static class TextOverlayPresets` with `IReadOnlyList<TextOverlayPreset> All` and `TextOverlayPreset? ByName(string? name)` (case-insensitive via `StringComparison.OrdinalIgnoreCase`, null-safe).
- **Approaches tried**: Modeled the file shape on `Musio.Core/Settings/DefaultBrandPresets.cs` (named `public static X { get; }` per preset + an `All` array built from those names) rather than inlining everything into one array literal — keeps each preset independently referenceable and readable. Colours use plain `"#RRGGBB"` (matching `TextOverlaySegment`'s own defaults), since `AnimatedTextEngine.ParseColor` supports 3/6/8-digit hex (`#AARRGGBB` for 8-digit) but every opacity-bearing property (`BackgroundOpacity`, `BlurTintOpacity`, `ScrimStrength`, `ShadowStrength`) already has its own dedicated field, so alpha-in-hex was never needed.
- **Critical constraint honored**: every preset's `Apply` sets **all** style fields (`Animation`, `Anchor`, `WidthFraction`, `MarginFraction`, `FontFamily`, `FontSize`, `IsBold`, `IsItalic`, `TextColor`, `TextAlignment`, `Background`, and all 14 background-parameter fields `BackgroundColor/BackgroundOpacity/CornerRadius/PaddingScale/BlurAmount/BlurTintOpacity/ScrimDirection/ScrimStrength/OutlineWidth/OutlineColor/ShadowStrength/AccentColor/AccentThickness/AccentSide`), even fields the chosen `Background` mode ignores — e.g. `Big Title` (OutlineShadow) and `Minimal Outline` (OutlineShadow) still explicitly set `BackgroundColor`/`BackgroundOpacity`/`BlurAmount`/etc. to the model's own defaults, so switching between presets never leaves stale state (e.g. an accent colour lingering from Callout Pill after switching to Frosted Card). Never touched `Id`/`Start`/`Duration`/`Enabled`/`Text`/`SourceVideoFilePath`/`X`/`Y`.
- **Six presets**: `Lower Third` (BottomLeft, GradientScrim/Bottom, SlideUp, FontSize 40 bold) · `Caption Bar` (BottomCenter, Solid `#000000`@0.65, FadeIn, FontSize 30) · `Callout Pill` (TopRight, Solid accent `#0078D4`@1.0, `CornerRadius=80` to force a true pill — confirmed via `TextOverlayRenderer.DrawSolidBackground`'s `CanvasGeometry.CreateRoundedRectangle`, which Win2D clamps to fit the box — ScalePop, narrow `WidthFraction=0.26`) · `Big Title` (MiddleCenter, OutlineShadow no box, ZoomBlurIn, FontSize 88 bold) · `Frosted Card` (BottomCenter, Blur `#FFFFFF` tint @0.28/BlurAmount 18, FadeIn, `CornerRadius=28`) · `Minimal Outline` (MiddleCenter, OutlineShadow thin `OutlineWidth=1.5`/soft `ShadowStrength=0.35`, TrackingIn).
- **Glyphs**: looked up real Segoe Fluent Icons codepoints from the official Microsoft Learn glyph table (`https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/iconography/segoe-fluent-icons-font`) rather than guessing — `AlignLeft` E8E4, `CC` E7F0, `Tag` E8EC, `FontIncrease` E8E8, `Effects` E794, `ReadingMode` E736. Deliberately avoided `E8D2` (Font) for any preset since the existing `PropertiesPane`'s `TextSlideTab` already uses that glyph (see the `TextOverlayPropertiesView` learnings entry above) — different UI surface (pane tab vs. preset tile) but avoided reusing it anyway to reduce visual-glyph collisions across the feature.
- **What worked**: Reading `TextOverlayRenderer.cs`'s `DrawSolidBackground`/`DrawBlurBackground`/`DrawGradientScrim`/`DrawAccentBarBackground`/`DrawOutlineShadow` methods before authoring values — confirmed exactly which overlay fields each background mode actually reads (e.g. `Blur` reads `BlurAmount`/`BlurTintOpacity`/`BackgroundColor` as tint but never `BackgroundOpacity`; `OutlineShadow` reads `OutlineWidth`/`OutlineColor`/`ShadowStrength` but never any box/blur/scrim/accent field) so "unused" fields could be set to sensible, harmless values (the model's own defaults) rather than guessed ones.
- **What didn't work**: N/A — no failed approaches; the task was purely additive/data-only (`Action<TextOverlaySegment>` lambdas, no Win2D/UI/I-O), and the contract signature was followed verbatim on the first attempt.

## Wiring text overlays into `EditorPage` (selection → pane, pane → undo/redo, preview refresh)

- **Feature/area**: `src\Musio.App\Pages\EditorPage.xaml.cs` and `EditorPage.xaml` only (feature/animated-text-overlays; model, edit operations, timeline track, properties pane, presets, and the compositor sync APIs were already built by concurrent agents). Connected the Text track to the properties pane and undo/redo: selection sync, create/move/resize/remove, all 27 pane control handlers, preset-tile application, the Insert-menu "Text Overlay" entry, and Delete-key support.
- **Approaches tried**: Modeled the whole "Text overlay track handlers" section directly on the existing "Camera track handlers" block (`OnCameraSegmentSelected`/`Created`/`Moved`/`Resized`/`RemoveRequested`, `DeleteCameraSegment`) rather than the `TextSlideSegment` pane handlers, because the task required every edit to go through `UndoRedoManager.Execute(new UpdateTextOverlayPropertiesOperation(...))` for undo correctness — unlike `SlideTextBox_TextChanged` etc., which mutate the slide model directly with no undo support. Reused the existing static `ParseHexColor`/`ColorToHex`/`UpdateSlideColorSwatch` helpers verbatim for all four overlay colour pickers (text/background/outline/accent) instead of writing new ones.
- **Preview/compositor sync**: `FrameCompositor.SyncTextOverlays` stores the overlay list *by reference*, and `SegmentFrameComposer.SelectTextOverlays` returns a new `List<T>` containing the *same* segment instances as `TimelineModel.TextOverlays` — so in-place property edits (every pane handler) are picked up by an already-synced renderer on the very next frame with no re-sync call needed; only Add/Remove need a fresh `SyncTextOverlays` call, which already happens automatically because `UndoRedoManager.StateChanged` → `InvalidatePreview()` → `SyncZoomStateToRenderer()`/`SyncSegmentZoomStateToRenderers()` (both extended with a new `SyncTextOverlaysToRenderer(renderer, sourceVideoFilePath)` helper) run on every `Execute(...)` call. Also added the same sync call at the per-segment renderer build site (`GetOrBuildSegmentPreviewAsync`) so freshly-built segment renderers start pre-synced. `SegmentFrameComposer.SelectTextOverlays` requires the *real* primary video path (not null, unlike the editor's own `ManualKeyframesForSource(null)` convention) — the helper resolves `sourceVideoFilePath ?? PrimaryVideoPath` and no-ops if still empty.
- **`TextOverlaySegment.ResolveCenter` ignores stored X/Y for every anchor except `Custom`** — it derives the centre purely from `margin`/box-fraction. `OverlayAnchor_Checked` still resets `X`/`Y` to an approximate resolved position when leaving `Custom` (per task spec, "so switching away from Custom actually snaps"), estimating box-height fraction from `FontSize`/`PaddingScale` since the editor has no access to real Win2D glyph measurement — this approximation is cosmetic-only, the live renderer recomputes the true position at render time for all non-Custom anchors regardless of stored X/Y.
- **Text box high-frequency edits**: `OverlayTextBox_TextChanged` commits `o.Text` through `UndoRedoManager.Execute` on every keystroke (required for undo/export correctness), but only debounces the *preview repaint* via a new 200ms `ScheduleOverlayPreviewRefresh()` timer, mirroring `ScheduleMotionPreviewRebuild`'s split between immediate model commit and debounced expensive rebuild — deliberately not copying `SlideTextBox_TextChanged`'s weaker non-undoable pattern.
- **Preset application re-entrancy**: `OverlayPreset_SelectionChanged` applies `preset.Apply` via `UpdateTextOverlayPropertiesOperation`, then calls `SyncTextOverlayUI(id)` (which clears `OverlayPresets.SelectedItem` to reset the one-shot tile) — that re-raises `SelectionChanged`, so `_suppressOverlayEvents` must be (and is) checked at the very top of the handler, set for the whole `SyncTextOverlayUI` body.
- **Insert-menu playhead mapping**: `AddTextOverlay_Click` → new `GetPlayheadSourceTimeForOverlay()` helper, which for segment-based projects maps the playhead through `TimelineModel.GetSegmentAtTime` (only creates an overlay when the playhead sits over a `VideoSegment`, doing nothing for e.g. a text-slide segment) computing `videoSeg.SourceStart + localOffset * videoSeg.SpeedFactor`, and returns `null` (primary) vs. the segment's own `VideoFilePath` for the overlay's `SourceVideoFilePath` depending on whether that segment *is* the primary recording (compared against `PrimaryVideoPath`); falls back to the legacy no-segments `MapToSourceTime` path, and returns `null` gracefully (no-op) when there's no primary video at all.
- **What worked**: Extending the two existing `SyncZoomStateToRenderer`/`SyncSegmentZoomStateToRenderers` methods (rather than adding new call sites scattered around) was sufficient to guarantee every renderer (main preview + all per-segment renderers) stays synced automatically through the existing `InvalidatePreview()` chain that every `UndoRedoManager.Execute(...)` already triggers.
- **What didn't work / blocker (not in my scope)**: `Musio.App.csproj` build shows exactly **one** remaining error — `TimelineControl.xaml.cs(3065,36): CS0234 'CanvasTextTrimmingSign' does not exist in Microsoft.Graphics.Canvas.Text` — a pre-existing issue in a file explicitly out of my scope (owned by a concurrent agent; also already flagged as a known pre-existing issue in this file's own earlier learnings entry above, from the `TextOverlayPropertiesView` pane task). Confirmed via three separate rebuild attempts (with waits in between) that no error touches `EditorPage.xaml.cs`/`EditorPage.xaml` or any of the 27 new pane handlers — all `CS0103`/enum/Tag-mismatch risks resolved cleanly on the first attempt, and no missing-symbol errors for `SyncTextOverlays`/`SelectTextOverlays`/`TextOverlayPresets` (those concurrent-agent APIs had already landed correctly).
- **Build/test (verified)**: `& 'C:\Program Files (x86)\Microsoft Visual Studio\2022\BuildTools\MSBuild\Current\Bin\amd64\MSBuild.exe' src\Musio.App\Musio.App.csproj /restore /t:Build /p:Configuration=Debug /p:Platform=x64 /v:minimal` → **only** the one pre-existing `TimelineControl.xaml.cs` `CS0234` error described above; zero errors anywhere in `EditorPage.xaml.cs`/`EditorPage.xaml`.
## Follow-up correction: preview API name + build-blocker fix (`RefreshOverlayPreview`, `TimelineControl.xaml.cs`)

- **Feature/area**: `src\Musio.App\Pages\EditorPage.xaml.cs` (continuing the editor-wiring task above) plus a single one-token fix in `src\Musio.App\Controls\TimelineControl.xaml.cs` (normally out of this task's scope, but the user explicitly asked to get the whole `Musio.App` project building clean and confirmed it was the only remaining error).
- **Correction received**: initial assumption about the preview-sync API name was wrong; the landed, correct API is `Musio.Core.Processing.PreviewRenderer.UpdateTextOverlays(IReadOnlyList<TextOverlaySegment>)` fed by `Musio.Core.Export.SegmentFrameComposer.SelectTextOverlays(TimelineModel, string videoFilePath)` — which is exactly what the already-written `SyncTextOverlaysToRenderer` helper called, so no change was needed there. Strengthened `RefreshOverlayPreview()` to call `SyncTextOverlaysToRenderer` explicitly (primary renderer + every per-segment renderer) before repainting, rather than relying solely on the `UndoRedoManager.StateChanged` → `InvalidatePreview()` chain, so overlay add/edit/remove/undo always repaints with the freshest overlay list even in edge cases where `_compositorReady` is false or a segment renderer isn't built yet.
- **Build blocker fix (outside normal scope, done only because explicitly instructed)**: `TimelineControl.xaml.cs`'s new text-track label rendering had `TrimmingSign = CanvasTextTrimmingSign.Ellipsis` — `CanvasTextTrimmingSign` does not exist in Win2D; the correct type is `Microsoft.Graphics.Canvas.Text.CanvasTrimmingSign` (verified against the official Win2D API docs). Fixed as a single-token rename; did not touch anything else in that file.
- **What worked**: A targeted web search against the official Win2D docs (`microsoft.github.io/Win2D`) confirmed the exact correct enum type name before editing, avoiding a guess-and-check cycle.
- **Build/test (verified)**: `& 'C:\Program Files (x86)\Microsoft Visual Studio\2022\BuildTools\MSBuild\Current\Bin\amd64\MSBuild.exe' src\Musio.App\Musio.App.csproj /restore /t:Build /p:Configuration=Debug /p:Platform=x64 /v:minimal` → **exit code 0, zero `error CS*` diagnostics** (only pre-existing `MVVMTK0045` AOT-compatibility warnings in unrelated ViewModel files remain). `Musio.App` now builds clean end-to-end.

## Runtime UIA validation of animated text overlays (Editor, live app) — partial success, blocked by concurrent-agent contention

- **Feature/area**: Runtime/UI validation only (no source edits) of the animated-text-overlays feature via PowerShell UI Automation against a live, freshly-built `Musio.App.exe`. Host is **ARM64**, not x64 — build with `MSBuild\Current\Bin\arm64\MSBuild.exe /p:Platform=ARM64`; `dotnet build` fails with PriGen `MSB4062` here. Developer Mode is off and the shell is not elevated, so `Add-AppxPackage -Register` fails `0x80073CFF` — launch the self-contained unpackaged exe directly instead: `src\Musio.App\bin\ARM64\Debug\net9.0-windows10.0.26100.0\win-arm64\Musio.App.exe`.
- **What worked (solid, repeatable evidence gathered against a clean, known-good build)**: App launches in **Mini mode** first (window title "Musio Mini"); click the `"Expand to full app"` button (`InvokePattern.Invoke()` works fine here) to reach the full window. The **Gallery** `NavigationViewItem` is `ControlType.ListItem` with `SelectionItemPattern`, **not** `InvokePattern` — calling `Invoke()` on it silently no-ops; use `SelectionItemPattern.Select()`, or a real mouse click on its bounding-rect center (also works, and matches `OpenProjectsPage`'s `GridView.ItemClick`). Confirmed the **"Text" track row** renders between Camera and Audio in the timeline (row heights: Ruler 30 / Video 80 / Cursor 40 / Zoom 50 / Camera 44 / **Text 44** / Audio 40 / Mic 40). `Insert ▸ Insert text overlay` (via a **real mouse click** on the Insert `DropDownButton`'s bounding-rect center, then a real click on the `"Insert text overlay"` `MenuItem` — background `InvokePattern`/`ExpandCollapsePattern` on this `MenuFlyout` is unreliable, confirming the earlier-documented gotcha) reliably creates a new overlay, auto-selected, with the **"Text Overlay"** rail tab/pane populated (presets, `"Overlay text"` textbox, animation/font combos, duration/font-size, background section, etc.). Editing `"Overlay text"` (click, Ctrl+A, type, Tab) reliably updates the Text-track block's on-canvas label to match — confirmed via multiple screenshots. `Ctrl+Z`/`Ctrl+Y` both genuinely mutate app state (observed Ctrl+Z restore a previously-deleted overlay onto the Text track, and Ctrl+Y re-apply a text/animation/font-size edit) without any crash.
- **New gotcha found**: the pane's **"Scene"** tab has its own, unrelated **"Background"** section (canvas background behind the video, Solid Color swatch, used for aspect-ratio letterboxing) — don't confuse this with the **overlay's own** `"Overlay background type"` combo under the **"Text Overlay"** tab; both are labeled "Background" in the UI but are completely different models. Also: when no overlay exists/is selected, the **"Text Overlay"** rail tab itself disappears from the UIA tree (consistent with the documented `Visibility.Collapsed`-removes-from-tree behavior) and the pane falls back to "Scene".
- **CRITICAL environment finding — do not skip this check next time**: partway through this validation session, `Musio.App`'s process was **repeatedly and silently terminated by an external, concurrent agent session** sharing this same machine/working directory (not by my script, not a crash — `crash.log` never grew from its baseline line count across the *entire* session despite ~4 process disappearances). Direct evidence: (1) `git status --short` on the shared working tree showed uncommitted modifications to `EditorPage.xaml`, then later to `TimelineControl.xaml`/`.xaml.cs`/`EditorPage.xaml.cs`, that I did not make; (2) the `Musio.App.exe` `LastWriteTime` changed twice mid-session (i.e., it was rebuilt by someone else) even though I never re-ran MSBuild; (3) two screenshot captures accidentally bled through content from another foreground window (a Copilot CLI terminal actively discussing "Review caught three HIGH bugs... I cancelled the automated UIA validation as you asked" and rebuilding after an `EditorPage.xaml` timeline-row-height fix) — both such screenshots were deleted immediately without further analysis, per privacy/isolation policy. **Lesson: before trusting any capture in a shared/multi-agent environment, verify `GetForegroundWindow() == target hwnd` and sanity-check the window rect (non-zero, reasonable size) — a "successful" `CopyFromScreen` call can silently capture a different, unrelated window if the target window lost focus/z-order to a concurrent session.** Also: `EnumWindows`+`GetWindowThreadProcessId` (bypassing UIA's `RootElement.FindFirst(Children, ProcessIdProperty)`) is a more reliable way to enumerate a process's real top-level HWNDs when UIA's window lookup starts intermittently returning null/stale results, and can reveal a window is genuinely hidden (`IsWindowVisible=false`, e.g. after "Hide to system tray") rather than just slow to enumerate.
- **What didn't work / was left UNVERIFIED**: because of the repeated external process kills and at least one mid-session rebuild from unrelated concurrent changes, the remaining checklist items (background-type cycling through all 6 values + visible-difference proof, the "background clipped to text box, video still shows through" critical assertion, the 3×3 anchor-grid movement proof, and the Remove-overlay button) could not be completed against a build I could confidently attribute to my own clean baseline, and are reported as **UNVERIFIED** rather than guessed at as PASS — per the task's explicit instruction that a false pass is worse than an honest "couldn't verify." A background-fill-not-rendering-while-selected symptom was observed once (Solid/Gradient Scrim combo value changed correctly but no visible box fill appeared in the preview even after real clicks and multi-second settle waits) but was **not conclusively distinguished** from the documented "stale by one action" render-timing artifact before the session's process churn made further isolation impossible — flag this as worth a focused, uncontended re-test rather than a confirmed bug.
- **What worked for recovery attempts (for next time)**: `SW_RESTORE`(9) must be conditioned on `IsIconic(hwnd)` — calling it unconditionally un-maximizes an already-maximized window (a real bug in my own harness, not the app). Forcing a hidden/backgrounded window forward with `SetForegroundWindow` alone is unreliable against an actively-competing foreground app; neither an `AttachThreadInput` dance nor a `keybd_event(VK_MENU)` "foreground-lock bypass" trick reliably won focus back in this specific contended scenario — `SetWindowPos(HWND_TOPMOST, ...)` immediately before capture (and only removing topmost afterward) is the most robust option when foreground cannot be reliably won, though it still requires the window to actually be alive/visible first.
## Animated text overlays — review fixes and timeline track layout

- **Feature/area**: `feature/animated-text-overlays` end-game — GPT-5.6 Sol review pass over the whole branch, then two timeline-layout bugs the user hit at runtime. Files: `TextOverlayRenderer.cs`, `AnimatedTextEngine.cs`, `TextSlideRenderer.cs`, `TimelineControl.xaml(.cs)`, `EditorPage.xaml(.cs)`.
- **Review findings worth remembering (all fixed)**:
  - **Preview vs export divergence is easy to reintroduce.** Overlays were synced in the preview-renderer *rebuild* path but not where the renderer is first created, so opening a project rendered overlays in the export and not the preview. When adding anything renderer-scoped, grep for *every* site that constructs a `PreviewRenderer`/`FrameCompositor`, not just the rebuild one.
  - **Source-time tracks are not interchangeable.** The new text track copied the *camera* track, which is deliberately primary-only (`XToPrimarySourceTime`). Overlays support per-recording ownership, so it needed the *zoom* track's pattern instead (`OwningSegmentForKeyframe`/`ZoomKeyframeTimeToX` → `OwningSegmentForTextOverlay`/`TextOverlayTimeToX`). Copying the wrong neighbour silently corrupted overlays on appended clips.
  - **Anchored placement must be resolved before a drag.** `ResolveCenter` ignores stored `X`/`Y` for every anchor except `Custom`, so reading raw `X`/`Y` as the drag origin made a plain click teleport the overlay. Resolve the effective centre, flip to `Custom` at that same centre, and only commit past a movement threshold.
  - **A gesture needs a finalizer, not just a `PointerReleased`.** Capture loss left live model mutations persisted with no undo entry. Route release, capture-lost and cancel through one idempotent finalizer.
  - **Effects built inside a `using` initializer leak on the exception path** — the object exists before the `using` local is assigned. Prefer renderer-owned cached fields disposed in `Dispose()`.
  - Frosted blur must sample with an **identity** transform (transform only the clip geometry), or an animating overlay drags a blurred copy of its starting position.
- **Timeline layout (two real bugs found by using the app, not by tests)**:
  - `EditorPage.xaml` pinned `TimelineControl` to `Height="280"` while its rows already totalled 324px — the mic track was **already** clipped before this feature, and the new 44px text row pushed system audio off too. The host row is `Auto`, so the fixed height was pure downside: removed it and let the control size to its tracks.
  - Added adaptive track visibility: cursor, camera, system audio and mic collapse when the recording has no data for them (`RowDefinition.Height = 0` **and** label/canvas `Collapsed`, so nothing hit-tests or draws a sliver). Video, zoom and text always stay — they are authoring surfaces, and hiding them when empty hides the feature.
  - Gotcha: waveforms are generated on a background pass **after** the timeline first draws, so anything that gates visibility on waveform presence must refresh when the samples land, or the track stays collapsed despite having data.
- **What didn't work**: deploying with `/p:DeployOnBuild=true` silently left a *stale* registered package (an ARM64 Release build from the previous day) registered, so manual testing was against old bits and the feature appeared missing. Reliable sequence on this machine: stop the running process by PID, `Get-AppxPackage *Musio* | Remove-AppxPackage`, build `/p:Platform=ARM64`, then `Add-AppxPackage -Register <win-arm64>\AppxManifest.xml -ForceUpdateFromAnyVersion`. **Always verify the running process's loaded `Musio.App.dll` timestamp** before trusting any manual or automated UI validation. Note the host is ARM64 — building x64 and deploying it is another way to end up testing the wrong thing.
- **Build/test (verified)**: `MSBuild src\Musio.App\Musio.App.csproj /restore /t:Build /p:Configuration=Debug /p:Platform=ARM64` (app, this machine) and `/p:Platform=x64` (Core + Tests) → 0 errors; `dotnet test src\Musio.Tests\Musio.Tests.csproj --no-build -c Debug -p:Platform=x64` with `DOTNET_ROLL_FORWARD=Major` → 715 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped.

## Animated text overlays — second review pass

- **Feature/area**: second GPT-5.6 Sol review of `feature/animated-text-overlays`, covering the fixes from the first review plus the later timeline-layout, adaptive-track-visibility, insert-seek and icon commits. 6 findings: 5 valid and fixed, 1 declined.
- **Highest-value finding was a regression introduced by an earlier fix in the same branch** — reviewing only the original feature would have missed it. When a branch grows past its first review, re-review the *newer* commits, not just the original diff.
  - **Adaptive track visibility hid imported audio.** Waveform generation only classifies the recorder's own `system_*`/`mic_*` files (`EditorPage.xaml.cs`), but `VideoImportService` extracts imported audio to `audio.wav` — so an imported clip has audible audio, no waveform, and a visibility rule keyed on the waveform collapsed the track *and its mute button*. **Presence of media and presence of a visualisation of that media are different predicates**; gate UI on the former.
- **Ordering bugs around in-flight gestures (both real, both subtle)**:
  - Syncing a new selection into the properties pane *before* finalizing the previous gesture is wrong: the commit path re-syncs the pane for the overlay it belongs to, so the pane ends up showing the old overlay while the new one is selected, and the next edit writes stale values onto the new overlay. **Finalize, then adopt the new selection.**
  - Deleting an object out from under a live gesture strands the gesture's pre-gesture snapshot — the "restore-then-execute" undo trick can no longer find the object, while the remove operation captures the already-mutated one, so undoing the delete restores state the user never committed. **Finalize before removing.**
- **A fix can reintroduce the bug it fixed.** `SeekPastOverlayEntrance` exists because an overlay is fully transparent at progress 0; but adding the midpoint offset in raw output time could overshoot the clip and land on the next segment, where the overlay is inactive and again renders nothing. Clamp such seeks to the owning segment's range, not just the timeline end.
- **Declined finding, recorded so it isn't "fixed" later by mistake**: `XToKeyframeFileTime` clamps to the nearest edge of the *overlay's own* recording when the pointer strays over another clip, rather than returning null. That is intentional and shared with the zoom track — the clamped time stays in the correct source domain, so it pins to the clip boundary instead of writing a foreign timestamp. Making the text track reject instead would diverge from zoom for no correctness gain. The misleading code comment that claimed it rejected was corrected.

## W2-2 — Shared test infrastructure (`src/Musio.Tests/TestSupport/`)

- **Feature/area**: DRY-ing up copy-pasted test fixtures across `src/Musio.Tests` (66 files, zero
  shared infrastructure beforehand). Created `TestSupport/TestTimelineBuilder.cs`,
  `TestMouseRecordingBuilder.cs`, `TempDirectoryFixture.cs`, `CanvasBitmapTestHelpers.cs`. Touched
  24 existing test files to delegate into them; left `Musio.App`/`Musio.Core` untouched (build
  coordination with a concurrent sibling agent working on `Musio.App`/ARM64).
- **Approaches tried / what worked**:
  - **Keep the local private method with the SAME signature, and swap only its body for a
    one-line delegation to the shared helper.** This meant zero changes at any call site (20+
    call sites per file in some cases) — only the ~5-20 line implementation moved. Far lower risk
    than rewriting every call site to reference `TestTimelineBuilder.X(...)` directly.
  - **`ModelWith(params TimelineSegment[])` had EXACTLY two variants, never three** — variant A
    (`PrimaryVideoFilePath = "primary.mp4"` always, byte-identical across all 4 files) and variant
    B (no primary path set, byte-identical across all 4 files). Confirmed this before unifying by
    diffing every call site; had there been a third divergent literal, the correct move would have
    been a third named method, not a parameter default guessed to match a majority.
  - **`Video(double durSec, ...)` in the 4 transition-boundary test files disagreed on the literal
    VideoFilePath** (`"C:\clip.mp4"` in `TransitionEditOperationsTests`, `"C:\primary.mp4"`
    elsewhere) — made `videoFilePath` a REQUIRED parameter (no default) on the shared
    `TestTimelineBuilder.TransitionVideo`, forcing every call site to state its own literal
    explicitly rather than silently inheriting a default that would erase the distinction.
  - **Two "duplicate" mouse-recording builders were verified byte-identical before merging**
    (`AutoZoomEngineTests.BuildRecordingWithClicks` vs `ZoomSegmentProgressTests.BuildRecordingWithClicks`)
    by diffing line-by-line, not by eyeballing similarity — safe to fully unify into
    `TestMouseRecordingBuilder.WithClicks`.
  - **`TempDirectoryFixture` narrows the old bare `catch { }` in `Directory.Delete` teardown to
    `catch (IOException) {} catch (UnauthorizedAccessException) {}`** — this IS a sanctioned,
    intentional behavior change (the work item's own design note asked for it, to avoid
    swallowing e.g. `NullReferenceException`), not an accidental one. Verified
    `DirectoryNotFoundException : IOException`, so the narrower catch still covers "directory
    already gone" cleanly.
  - Files with an exotic/incompatible teardown shape were deliberately left unmigrated and
    reported rather than forced into the fixture: `SessionCleanupServiceTests` (its
    `[TestCleanup]` has NO catch at all — propagates delete failures on purpose, since it is
    itself testing a cleanup service) and `BackgroundCompositorPreloadTests` (static
    `AppContext.BaseDirectory`-based dir + `[ClassCleanup]`, not per-instance/per-test — forcing
    it into a per-instance Guid-suffixed fixture would change its shared-across-methods semantics).
  - Nested-folder temp dirs (`AudioFileDurationProbeTests` used
    `Path.Combine(GetTempPath(), "MusioAudioProbeTests", guid)`, not the flat
    `"prefix_" + guid` most files used) were preserved bit-for-bit by passing
    `$"MusioAudioProbeTests{Path.DirectorySeparatorChar}"` as the fixture's `prefix` argument,
    rather than normalizing to the flat pattern.
  - Files using a *local variable* + inline `try/finally` instead of `[TestInitialize]`/
    `[TestCleanup]` (`PreviewFrameReaderTests`, `SubtitleGeneratorTests`) were migrated by
    constructing `TempDirectoryFixture` locally inside the test method and calling `.Dispose()`
    once from the existing `finally` — this changes 2-3 lines without restructuring the test
    method itself (no attribute shape changed).
  - **Self-verification that mattered**: after every batch, ran
    `git diff --unified=0 -- src/Musio.Tests | Select-String -Pattern "^[+-].*Assert\."` and the
    equivalent for `[TestMethod]`/`[TestClass]`/`[TestInitialize]`/`[TestCleanup]` — zero hits in
    the final diff confirms no assertion or test-structure line was ever touched, only fixture
    bodies.
- **What didn't work**: none of the migration approaches needed reverting — every batch (8
  `ModelWith`/`Video` files, mouse-recording builders, `CanvasBitmapTestHelpers`, then 3 waves of
  temp-dir files) stayed green at 953 total / 951 passed / 2 skipped / 0 failed on the first try,
  because each batch was verified by full build + full suite run before moving to the next.
- **Build/test (verified)**: `MSBuild src\Musio.Tests\Musio.Tests.csproj /restore /t:Build
  /p:Configuration=Debug /p:Platform=x64` → 0 errors, only pre-existing `CS0618` warnings in files
  untouched by this change; `dotnet test src\Musio.Tests\Musio.Tests.csproj --no-build -c Debug
  -p:Platform=x64` with `DOTNET_ROLL_FORWARD=Major` → 953 total, 951 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed
  (matches baseline exactly).
- **Win2D**: object-initializer construction of a multi-effect graph still leaks if the *second* effect throws — the first is already constructed but not yet owned by a field. Build both into locals inside `try`, dispose both on failure, then swap into the fields.
- **Build/test (verified)**: app x64 + ARM64 build clean; `dotnet test src\Musio.Tests\Musio.Tests.csproj --no-build -c Debug -p:Platform=x64` with `DOTNET_ROLL_FORWARD=Major` → 715 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped.

## Text overlay box resizing on the preview

- **Feature/area**: `EditorPage.xaml(.cs)` — drag handles on the vertical edges of the text overlay box so the user defines the wrap width directly instead of guessing with the pane slider. Extended `ITextEditTarget` with `CanResizeWidth`/`WidthFraction`/`CommitWidth`/`AbortResize`; text slides return `CanResizeWidth = false` (their box is a fixed fraction of the slide).
- **Symmetric resize, not single-edge.** The renderer positions the box from its anchor via `ResolveCenter`, so moving one edge would make an edge-anchored overlay drift off the edge it is meant to hug. Growing symmetrically about the resolved centre keeps the anchor meaningful — a `BottomLeft` overlay grows rightward and stays pinned left.
- **Trap worth remembering (same class as an earlier review finding): `ITextEditTarget.Center` returns the raw stored `X`/`Y`, which is NOT where an anchored overlay renders.** Any gesture that needs the on-screen position must derive it from `ComputeRect(...)` — the same path the renderer and hit region use — not from `Center`. This bit the drag gesture once and would have bitten resize identically.
- **Capture the reference point once per gesture.** The rendered centre of an edge-anchored box moves as the box grows, so recomputing it every pointer tick feeds back into the width being computed. Capturing it at pointer-press keeps the drag stable.
- **Floor the width** (8% of frame here): below that the text wraps to about one character per line and the two handles overlap, leaving no way to drag back out.
- Resize joins the existing gesture-finalization discipline: committed or aborted on release, capture loss, cancellation, selection change, delete, and on entering in-place text edit — one undo entry per gesture, none for a click that never moved.
- **Build/test (verified)**: app x64 + ARM64 build clean; 715 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped.

## Text overlay box: single source of truth + 8-point rect grabbers (feature/animated-text-overlays)

- **Feature/area**: `Musio.App`-side follow-up after `Musio.Core`'s `TextOverlaySegment` gained an explicit `HeightFraction` and a shared `ComputeBox(frameWidth, frameHeight)` (replacing the old measured-text box). Scope was `EditorPage.xaml(.cs)` and `TextOverlayPropertiesView.xaml` only.
- **Approaches tried**: (1) Deleted `EditorPage`'s duplicate `ComputeOverlayTextRect` (its own `CanvasTextFormat`/`CanvasTextLayout` re-measurement) and routed both call sites (`OverlayTextEditTarget.ComputeRect`, `PrepareForDrag`) through `overlay.ComputeBox(...)` instead — this was the actual bug fix (drag region and rendered box could drift apart because two independent measurements existed). (2) Replaced the two width-only handles (`TextEditLeftHandle`/`TextEditRightHandle`, symmetric-about-centre resize) with 8 point handles (`TextEditHandleTL/T/TR/L/R/BL/B/BR`), modeled directly on `RegionSelectorOverlay`'s handle convention: same Tag values (`TL,T,TR,L,R,BL,B,BR`), same `ApplyResize`-style delta switch (`ApplyBoxResize`), same cursor mapping (`GetCursorForBoxHandle`). (3) Generalized `ITextEditTarget` from `CanResizeWidth/WidthFraction/CommitWidth` to `CanResizeBox/Box (X,Y,W,H)/CommitBox`, keeping `AbortResize`. (4) Added `OverlayHeightSlider` to `TextOverlayPropertiesView.xaml` and wired its `ValueChanged` directly in `EditorPage`'s constructor (via `PropertiesPanel.TextOverlay.OverlayHeightSlider.ValueChanged += ...`) rather than in `EditorPage.PropertyPanels.cs`'s `WirePropertyPanels()`, since that file was out of scope for this task.
- **What worked**: A single edge/corner drag now moves that edge while the opposite edge stays put — achieved by maintaining a frame-pixel-space working rect (`_resizeLeft/Top/Width/Height`) mutated incrementally per tick (canvas-space pointer delta ÷ `layout.Width/_previewFrameW` scale, mirroring `RegionSelectorOverlay.Grid_PointerMoved`'s incremental `_resizeStart = pos` pattern), then converting to normalized center+size and writing through `target.Box`. Because the renderer positions the box from its centre (`ResolveCenter`), a hand-resize also calls `target.PrepareForDrag()` at gesture start (reusing the drag gesture's "snap anchor to Custom at current on-screen centre without moving" step) so the box doesn't get pulled back toward an anchor-resolved centre mid-resize; `CommitBox`/`AbortResize` use the same restore-then-execute pattern as `CommitPosition`/`CommitWidth` (restore all pre-gesture fields including `WidthFraction`/`HeightFraction`, then `Execute` one `UpdateTextOverlayPropertiesOperation` setting `Anchor=Custom` + final `X/Y/WidthFraction/HeightFraction` — one undo entry per gesture). Min box size enforced per-axis at `0.04` of the frame (mirrors old `MinTextWidthFraction=0.08`, but per-axis and smaller since corners can now shrink both dimensions at once). `SlideTextEditTarget.CanResizeBox` stays `false` with no-op `Box`/`CommitBox`, so slides show no handles and are unaffected. `OverlayHeightSlider` reads/writes `overlay.HeightFraction` exactly like the existing width slider's `WidthFraction` pattern, guarded by `_suppressOverlayEvents`.
- **What didn't work / traps avoided**: Reusing `PrepareForDrag` for resize (instead of adding a near-duplicate method) turned out fine since its contract ("snap anchor to Custom at current rendered centre, live, non-undoable") is exactly what a resize also needs before the box's edges can move independently of an anchor's auto-centring. Positioning writes in `PositionTextBoxHandles`/`PositionTextEditControls` (called every drawn frame from the Win2D callback) are guarded (`SetHandleVisibility`/`SetHandlePosition` compare-before-write) to avoid dirtying layout every frame, per the task's no-new-per-frame-allocations constraint — no per-frame array/tuple allocations were introduced (handle refs are the generated x:Name fields, not stored in a collection).
- **Build/test (verified)**: `Musio.App` x64 Debug build succeeded (0 errors); `Musio.Tests` x64 Debug build succeeded; `dotnet test` passed 715, failed 0, skipped 2 — matching the pre-change baseline exactly, so no test asserted the old measured-text geometry.

## Version 1.3.0 Store packages

- **Feature/area**: Microsoft Store package generation for version 1.3.0.
- **Approaches tried**: Bumped `Package.appxmanifest` and the `SettingsPage.xaml` version string from 1.2.1 to 1.3.0, then built unsigned non-bundled Release MSIX packages per architecture with VS BuildTools MSBuild, deleting App/Core `bin`/`obj` between x64 and ARM64.
- **What worked**: Both builds exited 0 and produced `1.3.0.0` packages; embedded `AppxManifest.xml` confirmed `PratikMistri.Musio` with matching `x64` / `arm64` ProcessorArchitecture.
- **What didn't work**: Nothing; the pre-existing missing `mspdbcmf.exe` warning only skips symbol-package generation and does not affect the MSIX.
- **Note**: The Store listing showed 1.2.1.0 already published, which equals the in-repo manifest version — always compare the manifest against the live Store version before packaging, since a matching version is rejected.
- **Artifact handoff**: Both validated MSIX files copied to `Packages\` and the user's `Downloads` folder for Store submission.

## Transition foundation (Task 1 of feature/segment-transitions): `TransitionResolver.cs`, extended `TransitionType`/`TransitionConfig`, `SlideTransitions` shim
- **Feature/area**: `src\Musio.Core\Timeline\TimelineSegment.cs` (enum/record extension only), new `src\Musio.Core\Timeline\TransitionResolver.cs`, `src\Musio.Core\Timeline\SlideTransitions.cs` (rewritten as a delegating shim), new `src\Musio.Tests\TransitionResolverTests.cs`. Model/pure-logic-only scope; explicitly NOT touching `SegmentFrameComposer.cs` (T2), `EditorPage.xaml.cs` (T3), `TransitionRenderer.cs` (T4), or `TimelineControl.*`/properties panes (T5/T6) — those are later tasks in the same feature.
- **Approaches tried**: Read `SlideTransitions.Resolve` first (its exact clamping/geometry: `Half()` truncates ticks, `Min` of three durations, frozen `OutgoingTime = current.Start - 1 tick` floored at `previous.Start`) before generalising it into `TransitionResolver.Resolve`, which resolves ANY boundary (not just slide-touching ones) using `TimelineSegment.InTransition` when present, falling back to the legacy 500ms Linear CrossFade only when `InTransition` is null AND the boundary touches a `TextSlideSegment`. An explicit `TransitionConfig { Type = None }` short-circuits before the fallback check runs, so it beats the legacy behavior even on slide boundaries.
- **What worked**: Appending new `TransitionType` members (`WipeRight`, `WipeUp`, `WipeDown`, `DipToWhite`, `ZoomBlur`, `WhipPanLeft`, `WhipPanRight`, `PushLeft/Right/Up/Down`, `Glitch`) strictly after the original 8 (keeping `Wipe` as the legacy left-to-right name, not renamed to `WipeLeft`) — enum values serialize by name via `MusioPackage.JsonOptions`' `JsonStringEnumConverter`, so reordering/renaming would corrupt saved `.musio` files. Added `TransitionEasing {Linear, EaseIn, EaseOut, EaseInOut}` (default `EaseInOut`) and `TransitionConfig.Easing` init property mapped onto the pre-existing `CubicBezierEasing.EaseIn/EaseOut/EaseInOut(float)` (all already clamp t<=0→0, t>=1→1, so `TransitionResolver.Ease` just clamps input and forwards). New `TransitionResolution` readonly record struct carries `OutgoingLocalOffset = outgoing.Duration + local` — the key new contract: the outgoing segment keeps ROLLING PAST its cut point (unlike the legacy frozen-instant semantics) so downstream renderers (T2/T3) can dip into footage past the edit point, clamping against available source range and holding the last frame when it runs out. Rewrote `SlideTransitions.Resolve` to delegate to `TransitionResolver.Resolve(timeline, outputTime, maxDuration)` and adapt back: filters explicitly for `incoming is TextSlideSegment || outgoing is TextSlideSegment` (so it can never surface a video→video transition even once other code starts setting `InTransition` there), and reconstructs the frozen `OutgoingTime` itself (not read from `TransitionResolution`, since that struct only exposes the new rolling `OutgoingLocalOffset`). Marked `SlideTransitions` `[Obsolete("Use TransitionResolver...")]` — confirmed safe (no `TreatWarningsAsErrors`/`WarningsAsErrors` anywhere in any `.csproj` or `.props` in the repo), so it only produces CS0618 warnings (in `SegmentFrameComposer.cs`, `EditorPage.xaml.cs`, `SlideTransitionsTests.cs`, and one intentional internal reference suppressed with `#pragma warning disable/restore CS0618` in `TransitionResolver.cs` itself) rather than build breaks.
- **What didn't work / traps avoided**: Initially referenced `SlideTransitions.DefaultDuration` from `TransitionResolver`'s legacy-fallback branch without suppressing the resulting self-inflicted CS0618 warning inside the new "canonical" resolver — added a scoped `#pragma warning disable CS0618` around just that one assignment since the dependency there is permanent by design (not a leftover of an unfinished migration), rather than hardcoding a duplicate `500ms` literal that could drift from `SlideTransitions.DefaultDuration`.
- **Verification**: `& 'C:\Program Files (x86)\Microsoft Visual Studio\2022\BuildTools\MSBuild\Current\Bin\amd64\MSBuild.exe' src\Musio.Core\Musio.Core.csproj /restore /t:Build /p:Configuration=Debug /p:Platform=x64 /v:minimal /nologo` and same for `Musio.Tests.csproj` and `Musio.App.csproj` — all built with 0 errors (CS0618 warnings only, as expected). `dotnet test src\Musio.Tests\Musio.Tests.csproj --no-build -c Debug -p:Platform=x64` (with `$env:DOTNET_ROLL_FORWARD="Major"`): **730 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped (732 total)** — baseline 717 + 13 new `TransitionResolverTests` tests, confirming full behaviour neutrality (all 7 pre-existing `SlideTransitionsTests` still pass unmodified).

## Transition rendering (Task 4 of feature/segment-transitions): `TransitionRenderer.cs` — 12 new effects, removed internal double-easing
- **Feature/area**: `src\Musio.Core\Processing\TransitionRenderer.cs` only, plus new `src\Musio.Tests\TransitionRendererTests.cs`. Scope explicitly excluded `SegmentFrameComposer.cs`, `TimelineControl.xaml.cs`, `EditorPage.xaml.cs`, `TransitionResolver.cs`, `TimelineSegment.cs`, `ExportAudioPlan.cs`, `VideoEncoder.cs`, `AudioPlaybackEngine.cs` (owned by sibling T1/T2/T3/T5/T6 agents running concurrently).
- **Coverage — all 20 `TransitionType` values, no silent fallthrough except `None`**: `Fade`/`DipToWhite` → shared `RenderFade(..., Color through)` (refactored to take a colour instead of duplicating black/white variants). `CrossFade` unchanged. `SlideLeft/Right/Up/Down` → `RenderSlide` (unchanged formula, `SmoothStep` call removed). `Wipe`/`WipeRight`/`WipeUp`/`WipeDown` → generalized `RenderWipe(..., dirX, dirY)` calling a new pure `WipeRevealRect(t, w, h, dirX, dirY)` — direction convention (verified against T1's own XML-doc wording on the enum members in `TimelineSegment.cs`): `(1,0)`=legacy left→right, `(-1,0)`=WipeRight "right-to-left", `(0,-1)`=WipeUp "bottom-to-top", `(0,1)`=WipeDown "top-to-bottom". `PushLeft/Right/Up/Down` → new `RenderPush`, using new pure `PushPairOffsets(t, dir, size)` = `(-dir*size*t, dir*size*(1-t))` (same dirX/dirY sign convention as Slide). `ZoomBlur` → new `RenderZoomBlur` (Transform2DEffect scale-around-own-center + GaussianBlurEffect, blur peaks mid-transition via `PeakEnvelope`). `WhipPanLeft/Right` → new `RenderWhipPan` (Push arithmetic + `DirectionalBlurEffect{Angle=0}`, strength via `PeakEnvelope`). `Glitch` → new `RenderGlitch` (crossfade base + `ColorMatrixEffect`-isolated R/B channels offset oppositely + additive-composited, plus a handful of horizontal-band slices displaced via `GlitchSliceOffset`), all peaking mid-transition and zero at both ends. `None` still falls through `default:` to a hard cut (`ds.DrawImage(incoming)`), same as before.
- **Double-easing resolved by removing `SmoothStep` from the shared motion path, not adding a second curve**: `Render`'s `progress` parameter is documented as already being `TransitionResolver.EasedProgress` (T1's contract — the resolver applies the segment's configured `TransitionEasing` via `CubicBezierEasing` before calling `Render`). Verified *before* deleting anything: (1) grepped every call site of `TransitionRenderer.Render` in the whole repo (`SegmentFrameComposer.cs:182`, `EditorPage.xaml.cs:1118`) — both hardcode `TransitionType.CrossFade` today, and `RenderCrossFade` never called `SmoothStep`, so CrossFade truly is unaffected. (2) Confirmed `Slide*`/`Wipe` were never reachable from either call site (only CrossFade is ever passed), so removing their internal `SmoothStep(progress)` application cannot regress any existing/saved project's rendered output — there is no saved project whose output depends on the old smoothstepped Slide/Wipe path, because nothing has ever requested it. Removed the now-fully-unused private `SmoothStep` helper entirely (nothing else needed it) rather than keeping dead code. New effects (Push/ZoomBlur/WhipPan/Glitch) treat `progress` as final throughout — no monotonic re-easing anywhere.
- **Effect-identity intensity curves are NOT the same thing as progress easing**: added `PeakEnvelope(t) = 4t(1-t)` (unimodal, 0 at t=0/1, 1 at t=0.5) as an `internal static` helper used by `ZoomBlur`'s blur radius, `WhipPan`'s directional-blur strength, and `Glitch`'s channel-offset/slice-displacement magnitude — this shapes each effect's own look, distinct from (and layered on top of) the already-eased overall `progress` used for position/opacity. Documented this distinction explicitly in each method's XML doc and in `Render`'s doc comment so a future editor doesn't reintroduce double-easing by "fixing" what looks like a missing ease call.
- **Glitch determinism**: `GlitchSliceOffset(sliceIndex, progress)` is a pure function — Knuth multiplicative hash (`sliceIndex * 2654435761u`) gives a stable per-slice phase in `[0,1)`, combined with `Math.Sin((progress*3 + phase) * 2π)` and a per-slice sign flip — **no `System.Random`, no wall-clock/instance seed**. Same `(sliceIndex, progress)` always produces the same displacement, so export (which renders every frame independently, potentially out of order) matches live preview exactly for the same instant, and re-exporting the same project never flickers differently. Verified with a dedicated `GlitchSliceOffset_IsDeterministic_ForSameInputs` test (repeated calls, same inputs, exact equality) plus a range check (`[-1,1]`) and a "not all slices identical" sanity check.
- **Win2D resource disposal**: every effect graph (`Transform2DEffect`, `GaussianBlurEffect`, `DirectionalBlurEffect`, `ColorMatrixEffect`) is constructed in a `using`/`using (...)` block scoped to a single `Render` call and disposed before the method returns — matches the existing convention in `BackgroundCompositor.DrawBlurBackground`/`AnimatedTextEngine.DrawBlurredText`. Avoided allocating any *extra* intermediate `CanvasRenderTarget` for cross-resolution scaling (outgoing/incoming can be different pixel sizes, e.g. a text slide vs. a video frame) by keeping `Transform2DEffect`/`DirectionalBlurEffect` scale/blur math in the *source bitmap's own pixel space* (e.g. `Transform2DEffect` scale-center = `bitmap.SizeInPixels/2`) and relying on `CanvasDrawingSession.DrawImage(ICanvasImage, Rect destRect, Rect srcRect, float opacity, [interpolation, composite])` to do the final resolution-independent stretch — confirmed this overload accepts any `ICanvasImage` (not just `CanvasBitmap`) via Win2D docs before relying on it, since none of this file's pre-existing code had drawn an *effect* (rather than a bitmap) through a dest/src Rect before.
- **Testing route taken — GPU smoke tests, not skipped**: checked `BackgroundCompositorPreloadTests.cs`/`CursorShapeTests.cs` first — both establish `new CanvasDevice(forceSoftwareRenderer: true)` + `Assert.Inconclusive("Win2D CanvasDevice unavailable.")` fallback as the house pattern, and `CanvasBitmap.CreateFromColors(device, colors, w, h)` as the way to fabricate test bitmaps. Followed both. `new TransitionRendererTests.cs` runs a `[DynamicData]`-driven smoke test over `Enum.GetValues<TransitionType>()` (all 20 values × progress {0, 0.5, 1} × outgoing {present, null}) asserting `Render` returns exactly the requested width/height and never throws, plus a mismatched-input-resolution test (1920×1080 outgoing vs. incoming... swapped, 320×240 vs 1920×1080) proving the scale-to-output-size guarantee, and a disposed-renderer throw test. *Additionally* extracted the pure arithmetic (`WipeRevealRect`, `PushPairOffsets`, `PeakEnvelope`, `GlitchSliceOffset`) as `internal static` (visible to `Musio.Tests` via the existing `InternalsVisibleTo` in `Musio.Core.csproj`) and unit-tested those directly with no GPU device at all, since the task's edge-to-edge/envelope-shape/determinism claims are precise numeric claims best proven analytically rather than by eyeballing rendered pixels.
- **Interesting/non-obvious finding, documented in code comments rather than "fixed"**: `RenderPush`'s spec formula (`outgoing = -dir*size*t`, `incoming = dir*size*(1-t)`) is *arithmetically identical* to what `RenderSlide` already computed before this task (`RenderSlide` already moved both frames by the same amounts, edge-to-edge — it was never a "static outgoing, incoming slides over it" style overlay transition). Verified this by working through the algebra by hand (outgoing's leading edge = incoming's trailing edge = `size*(1-t)` for both). Rather than silently duplicating a "different-looking" effect or unifying them (risking scope creep into `RenderSlide`, which pre-existed and is explicitly not mine to redesign), kept them as two separate named helpers with a doc comment on `RenderPush` explaining they currently share the same math but are intentionally decoupled so either can diverge later (e.g. Slide could later gain an overshoot/spring while Push stays perfectly rigid).
- **Build & test**: `Musio.Core.csproj` and `Musio.Tests.csproj` both built with the verified MSBuild command, 0 errors (pre-existing CS0618 warnings only, unrelated to this file). `dotnet test` (`$env:DOTNET_ROLL_FORWARD="Major"`): full suite **790 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped (792 total)**; filtering to just `FullyQualifiedName~TransitionRendererTests` showed **49 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped** — confirming the GPU smoke tests actually executed on this host (no silent `Inconclusive`s) rather than merely compiling.

## Transition audio crossfade (Task 7 of feature/segment-transitions): `ExportAudioPlan.cs`, `VideoEncoder.cs` (audio/mux region only), `AudioPlaybackEngine.cs`, new `EqualPowerCrossfade.cs`
- **Feature/area**: Audio side of the transitions feature. Scope strictly limited to `src\Musio.Core\Export\ExportAudioPlan.cs`, `src\Musio.Core\Export\VideoEncoder.cs` (the `ApplyPlacement`/`MuxAudioAsync`/`ResolveAvailableAudioAsync` audio region only — not video code in the same file), `src\Musio.Core\Audio\AudioPlaybackEngine.cs`, a new `src\Musio.Core\Audio\EqualPowerCrossfade.cs`, and a new `src\Musio.Tests\ExportAudioPlanTransitionTests.cs` — did NOT touch `SegmentFrameComposer.cs`, `TransitionRenderer.cs`, `TimelineControl.xaml(.cs)`, `EditorPage.xaml.cs`, `TransitionResolver.cs`, or `TimelineSegment.cs` (owned by sibling agents T1-T6 working in parallel; `git status` at the end confirmed only the intended files + new files changed by this task).
- **Approaches tried**: (1) Investigated whether a custom `Windows.Media.Effects.IBasicAudioEffect` registered via `BackgroundAudioTrack.AudioEffectDefinitions` could ramp gain on export. Confirmed via docs/web research that `AudioEffectDefinitions` is a real, populated `IList<IAudioEffectDefinition>` on `BackgroundAudioTrack` (so the property itself isn't the blocker) — but activating a custom effect by class-id string requires **(a)** the effect class live in a genuine Windows Runtime Component project producing a `.winmd` (a plain `Microsoft.NET.Sdk` class library like `Musio.Core` cannot host a WinRT-activatable class no matter how `IBasicAudioEffect` is implemented in C#), and **(b)** registering it as an in-process server via a `windows.activatableClass.inProcessServer` extension in `Musio.App\Package.appxmanifest` (confirmed `Musio.App` is `WindowsPackageType=MSIX` with a real `Package.appxmanifest`). Both are structural, cross-project changes outside this task's file scope, and (b) is a single shared file every other concurrent agent could also need — took the pre-approved fallback rather than burning time on this. (2) Designed `ExportAudioPlan.BuildFromSegments` to resolve `TransitionResolver.Resolve(timeline, segment.Start)` for every segment up front (confirming resolving at `incoming.Start` always lands `local==0`, inside the window, whenever one is active — read `TransitionResolver.cs` first per instructions rather than assuming), then threaded a `trailingExtension` parameter into the existing `BuildPlacement` so the SAME `available`/`outputRoom` clamping logic that already caps speed-adjusted segments also bounds the transition extension — proved algebraically that adding the same `extension` to both `available` and `outputRoom` (both terms of the existing `min()`) means the realized extension always equals the requested one exactly (`min(a+E,b+E) == min(a,b)+E`), so `ExportAudioPlan` has no separate "true EOF" cap of its own (impossible without I/O — deferred to the caller, matching the class's existing "pure, no I/O" contract) and instead reuses `TransitionResolver`'s own half-of-neighbouring-segment-duration clamp as the one meaningful, purely-computable bound.
- **What worked**: `AudioPlacement` extended with `FadeOutDuration`/`FadeInDuration` (both `TimeSpan, default`) — fully source-compatible, confirmed the pre-existing `ExportAudioPlanTests.cs` (untouched) still passes unmodified. `BuildFromSegments` now does a first pass resolving `TransitionResolution[]` per segment, computes each segment's `trailingExtension` (only when the NEXT segment's resolved transition's `OutgoingSegment` is `ReferenceEquals` to THIS segment — correctly excludes the legacy slide-adjacent case, since there the outgoing side on the full timeline is the `TextSlideSegment`, not the preceding video in the filtered video-only list), then a second pass (`ApplyTransitionFadeMetadata`) sets `FadeInDuration` on every incoming placement (even at a slide-adjacent boundary — the following clip still fades up from the slide's silence) and `FadeOutDuration` on the outgoing placement only when its `VideoSegment` reference matches. New `EqualPowerCrossfade` (`Musio.Core.Audio`, `OutGain(t)=cos(t·π/2)`, `InGain(t)=sin(t·π/2)`, both clamp `t` to `[0,1]`) — verified `out²+in²==1` for `t` in `0..1` step `0.01` in a dedicated test, plus endpoint and clamping tests. **Export**: `VideoEncoder.ApplyPlacement` cannot ramp gain (per the rejected-effect investigation above), so it calls a new `ExportTakeDuration` helper that subtracts `FadeOutDuration` back out of `TakeDuration` before computing `TrimTimeFromEnd` — proved this exactly reconstructs the pre-feature hard cut because both the extension and `FadeOutDuration` are derived from the identical clamped transition duration — **except** for `PlaysAtNativeRateOnSpeedAdjustedSegment` placements, which are excluded from the subtraction since their `TakeDuration` was never extended in the first place (subtracting there would wrongly shorten an already-correct take). Added `WarnAboutTransitionFadeAudio` (logged via `Debug.WriteLine`, same pattern as the pre-existing `WarnAboutSpeedAdjustedAudio`) so the export-side divergence is never silently swallowed. **Preview**: `AudioPlaybackEngine` gained a new `AudioFadeWindow` record + an optional `Load(paths, fadeWindowsByPath)` overload (existing single-arg `Load` calls, e.g. in `EditorPage.xaml.cs`, stay byte-for-byte unchanged and apply no ramp, exactly as before) and a private `EqualPowerFadeSampleProvider : ISampleProvider` that multiplies samples by `EqualPowerCrossfade` gain based on the ORIGINAL `AudioFileReader.CurrentTime` (not an internally-tracked sample counter, which would desync across `Seek`/`ScrubTo` since those mutate `reader.Position` directly) — correct even when wrapping a resampled provider downstream of that reader, since elapsed file-time is invariant to resampling.
- **What didn't work / important discovery — preview is NOT segment-aware today**: Before wiring anything into `AudioPlaybackEngine`, traced `EditorPage.xaml.cs`'s `AudioPositionForVideo(videoPosition) => videoPosition + offset` (a **direct, constant-offset** mapping from `Preview.PlayheadPosition`, which is OUTPUT/authoring time) and confirmed it is genuinely NOT the same as `MapToSourceTime` (which DOES account for segments/cuts and is used for video frame mapping elsewhere in the same file). This means preview audio today plays each raw source file continuously regardless of splits/reorders/deletes — a pre-existing limitation predating this feature, out of scope to fix (would require making `AudioPlaybackEngine` placement-aware and rewiring its call sites in `EditorPage.xaml.cs`, which is off-limits for this task). Given that architecture, `AudioPlaybackEngine`'s new fade-window capability is real, tested-by-construction, reusable code — but nothing currently calls the new overload with actual windows (since doing so requires computing them from `TimelineModel`/`TransitionResolver` inside `EditorPage.xaml.cs`), so today it is a capability ready for a future task to wire up, not a wired end-to-end preview crossfade. Documented this precisely in `AudioPlaybackEngine`'s class remarks and in `ExportAudioPlan`'s class remarks (matching the existing speed-adjustment limitation's voice/depth) rather than overclaiming "preview crossfades".
- **Build & test**: `Musio.Core.csproj` and `Musio.Tests.csproj` both built via the verified MSBuild command, 0 errors (only pre-existing CS0618 warnings in unrelated files). `dotnet test` (`$env:DOTNET_ROLL_FORWARD="Major"`): full suite **801 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped (803 total)** — higher than this task's stated 733-passed baseline because sibling T1-T6 agents concurrently added their own tests to the same shared test project (confirmed via `git status`: `SegmentFrameComposerTransitionTests.cs`/`TransitionRendererTests.cs` are new, untouched-by-me files); filtering to `FullyQualifiedName~ExportAudioPlanTransitionTests` showed **11 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped**, confirming all new tests actually ran and passed.

## Rolling transitions in the LIVE PREVIEW (Task 3 of feature/segment-transitions): `EditorPage.xaml.cs` only

- **Feature/area**: `src\Musio.App\Pages\EditorPage.xaml.cs` only (single-file scope enforced throughout; `git status`/`git diff --stat` confirmed at the end that no other file was touched). Goal: replace the `[Obsolete]` `SlideTransitions.Resolve`-driven crossfade block in `RenderFrameAtAsync` with `TransitionResolver.Resolve` (T1) and mirror T2's `SegmentFrameComposer` rolling-outgoing-side model exactly, so the preview and exporter dissolve identically instead of diverging (the repo's single most-recorded failure mode).
- **Important timing note**: T2's file (`SegmentFrameComposer.cs`) was NOT static during this task — partway through, a T2 review-fix pass landed `CollapseContiguousSourceBoundary`/`SelectAvailableSourceDuration`/`MapLocalOffsetToSourceTime` on top of the initial T2 handoff this task's instructions were written against. Re-diffed `SegmentFrameComposer.cs` mid-task (not just once at the start) and found the new same-source-contiguous-split fix, which had real implications for this file too (see below) — **lesson: when a sibling's file is the thing you're told to mirror, re-check it again before finishing, not just at hand-off time**.
- **The cache — retired, not rekeyed**: `GetCrossfadeOutgoingAsync`/`ReleaseCrossfadeOutgoing`/`_crossfadeOutgoing`/`_crossfadeOutgoingKey`/`_crossfadeGeneration` were deleted entirely, along with all 5 `ReleaseCrossfadeOutgoing()` call sites (`Unloaded` lambda, `RecoverGraphicsDeviceAsync`, `InitializePreviewCoreAsync`, `InvalidatePreview`, `RebuildPreviewRendererCoreAsync`, `RefreshSlidePreview` — found via `grep -n ReleaseCrossfadeOutgoing`, confirmed all 7 occurrences removed and none left dangling by rebuilding clean). Rationale: the cache existed ONLY because the legacy model froze the outgoing side at a fixed instant (`current.Start - 1 tick`), so composing it repeated identical, expensive work (~15x per 500ms dissolve). The rolling model (`OutgoingLocalOffset = OutgoingSegment.Duration + local`) makes the outgoing side compose a DIFFERENT image on nearly every tick, so a single-slot cache keyed on the offset would almost always miss — not worth the invalidation-site bookkeeping the previous entries in this file document as hard-won (use-after-dispose, publish-after-invalidate). Instead relies on two things that already existed: `VideoFrameReader`'s own internal per-frame-index decode LRU (hits automatically once the offset holds at the reader's last frame, which now happens more often since transitions are user-configurable and can run longer than 500ms) and `TextSlideRenderer.RenderSlide`'s own cost (~1.5ms/frame, measured and documented earlier in this file) being cheap enough not to need caching. Net effect: `outgoing` is now a plain method-local disposed in the same `finally` as `incoming`, eliminating the entire "page-owned cache aliases a method-local" hazard class the earlier crossfade-cache entries in this file warn about — there is no longer a shared field for `HideTextEditOverlay()`'s `RefreshSlidePreview` → (formerly) `ReleaseCrossfadeOutgoing` chain to race with. `HideTextEditOverlay()` still runs BEFORE composing the outgoing frame (unchanged ordering) because committing an in-place slide edit still changes which slide content the outgoing side composes from — that ordering requirement is independent of caching and was never only about avoiding use-after-dispose.
- **`ClampSourceTime` (T2) — reused indirectly, not copy-pasted, and not actually needed**: confirmed `Musio.Core.csproj` only declares `InternalsVisibleTo("Musio.Tests")`, not `Musio.App`, so the `internal` `ClampSourceTime`/`ResolveAvailableSourceDuration` are invisible here and can't be called or thinly wrapped. Investigated whether duplicating the arithmetic was actually necessary and found it ISN'T: `VideoFrameReader.LoadFrameAtTimeAsync` → `GetFrameIndex` already does `Math.Clamp(index, 0, FrameCount-1)` against the reader's OWN frame count — the exact same "available footage" bound T2's `ResolveAvailableSourceDuration` prefers (`VideoFrameReader.Duration`, i.e. `FrameCount/fps`). Since both the primary preview (`_frameReader`) and appended-segment preview (`ctx.Reader`) are genuine `VideoFrameReader`s, feeding an uncapped, past-cut `sourceTime` straight into `LoadFrameAtTimeAsync` already holds at the reader's last decodable frame for free — no separate clamp needed, and nothing to duplicate or flag as a gap for `ClampSourceTime` specifically.
- **`CollapseContiguousSourceBoundary` (T2 review-fix) — DID need re-implementing, and this is the one genuine, deliberate duplication left behind**: unlike `ClampSourceTime`, there is no existing preview-side mechanism that would prevent a same-source contiguous split (plain split of one recording, transition added at the existing cut, no footage trimmed) from blending a frame with itself — a silent no-op dissolve that would exist in preview but NOT in export, exactly the divergence this whole feature exists to eliminate. Re-implemented a `private static double CollapseContiguousSourceBoundary(...)` in `EditorPage.xaml.cs` with identical parameter shape and semantics to T2's version (compare source file paths case-insensitively, no-op if incoming isn't a `VideoSegment`, no-op on a genuine trimmed gap, otherwise collapse to `incomingTime - 1/fps` floored at 0). `ComposePreviewFrameAtOffsetAsync` gained matching optional `incomingVideoFilePath`/`incomingSourceTimeSeconds` parameters, and `RenderFrameAtAsync` computes the incoming side's own mapped source time the same way T2's `ComposeFrameAsync` does (`incoming.SourceStart + (OutgoingLocalOffset - OutgoingSegment.Duration) * incomingSpeed`) before composing the outgoing side. This is flagged in code comments as a deliberate, documented duplication (not an oversight) with a pointer for a future task (T8, matching the `ClampSourceTime` convention already set) to promote both helpers to `public` so this file can delegate instead.
- **Preserved exactly**: canvas-size blend (`GetPreviewCanvasSize()`, not raw capture size), `_lastRenderedFrameIndex=-1`/`_lastRenderedSegmentId=null` forcing a full redraw once the dissolve ends, the whole transition block staying inside try/catch/finally so any failure falls back to the normal render, and the "incoming frame missing → fall through, don't strand the previous frame" retry logic. Passed `resolution.Type`/`resolution.EasedProgress` into `TransitionRenderer.Render` instead of the hardcoded `TransitionType.CrossFade`/raw `crossfade.Progress` — signature unchanged (owned by T4), unimplemented effect types degrade safely inside it.
- **Performance**: did NOT add a new outgoing-specific downscale/quality-tier mechanism. Traced the existing feedback loop instead: `UpdatePreviewFrameAsync` times `RenderFrameAtAsync` wall-clock (now inclusive of the rolling outgoing compose) and feeds it to `_adaptivePreviewQuality.ObservePlaybackFrame`, gated on `IsVideoPosition(currentPos)` — which is exactly true during a video-to-video transition (the new case this feature enables; previously only slide-adjacent transitions existed, which are cheap). A sustained downgrade calls `ApplyPreviewResolutionAsync`, which reopens `_frameReader` at a smaller decode resolution AND (via the pre-existing `DisposeSegmentPreviews()` call inside it) forces appended-segment contexts to rebuild at the new tier too on next use — so both the incoming and outgoing sides of a transition already get cheaper together under sustained load, with zero new code. Considered composing the outgoing side at a separately-capped resolution, but `FrameCompositor.OutputWidth/Height` (the GPU compositor's actual render-target size) is fixed at construction from source/project dimensions independent of the adaptive tier — the tier only shrinks the decode+upscale step — so a preview-only cap would need changes in `FrameCompositor.cs`, which is out of this task's file scope. Could not measure real-world impact (no GPU-driven UI test harness exists for `EditorPage`, and inventing one is explicitly disallowed by the task) — this is reported honestly rather than claimed as verified.
- **Build & test**: ARM64 `Musio.App.csproj` build → 0 errors, 0 warnings from `EditorPage.xaml.cs`, and zero `CS0618` (confirming the file no longer references the obsolete `SlideTransitions`). x64 `Musio.Tests.csproj` build → 0 errors. `dotnet test` (`$env:DOTNET_ROLL_FORWARD="Major"`): **813 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped (815 total)**, unchanged from the pre-existing count at the time of this task (this file has no unit tests of its own — `EditorPage` needs a XAML host + GPU and is verified by build + careful reasoning, the established convention already documented for this file).

## Transition properties pane — the user-facing surface (Task 6 of feature/segment-transitions): new `TransitionPropertiesView.xaml(.cs)`, `PropertiesPane.xaml(.cs)`, `EditorPage.PropertyPanels.cs`, `EditorPage.xaml.cs`, `EditOperation.cs`, new `TransitionEditOperationsTests.cs`

- **Feature/area**: Makes T1-T5's plumbing user-facing. Scope: the new pane + its two integration points (`PropertiesPane`, `EditorPage.PropertyPanels.cs`), `EditorPage.xaml.cs` (new subscriptions/handlers only — read T3's diff first since it retired the crossfade cache and rewired the preview transition path), two new `IEditOperation`s appended to `EditOperation.cs` (did not touch any existing operation), and a new test file. Did not touch `TransitionRenderer.cs`, `ExportAudioPlan.cs`/`VideoEncoder.cs`/`AudioPlaybackEngine.cs` (T4/T7, confirmed mid-edit by siblings during this session via `git status`/file timestamps), `TimelineControl.xaml.cs` (T5, read-only), `SegmentFrameComposer.cs` (T2), or `TransitionResolver.cs` (T1).
- **Approach tried and kept — two-tier Type picker instead of one 20-item ComboBox**: considered faking WinUI ComboBox grouping with disabled `IsEnabled="False"` header items interleaved among 20 flat entries, but the built-in `Disabled` visual state in the default `ComboBoxItem` template overrides custom foreground/opacity Setters at the state-precedence level, and a half-greyed "header" reads as a broken item, not a section label. Switched to a **Family ComboBox** (7 entries: Automatic, Hard Cut/None, Dissolve, Slide, Push, Wipe, Stylised — matching T5's chip-colour grouping except Slide/Push are split, since the pane has room to be more specific than a 20px chip glyph) plus a **Variant ComboBox** populated in code (`PopulateTransitionVariantCombo`) only for the 5 families with more than one member, shown/hidden exactly like `OverlayBgTypeCombo` already toggles its own sub-panels. Fully readable at every state, no template fighting.
- **Preserving `null` (unconfigured) vs explicit `Type=None` (hard cut) through the UI — the sharpest edge in this task**: added a literal third state to the Family combo, `Tag="Unset"`, distinct from `Tag="None"`. Selecting "Automatic (Default)" pushes `InTransition = null` via `UpdateTransitionOperation`; selecting "Hard Cut (None)" pushes an explicit `TransitionConfig { Type = None }`. Critically, **`SyncTransitionUI` (the pane-populate function driven by `TimelineControl.TransitionSelected`) never calls `UndoRedoManager.Execute`** — it only reads state onto controls under a `_suppressTransitionEvents` guard, exactly mirroring `SyncTextOverlayUI`. This is the load-bearing invariant: merely selecting an unconfigured ("+") chip and viewing the pane must never itself write a config to the model, or every boundary a user ever inspected would silently become an explicit hard cut the instant the pane redrew. Verified by construction (Sync never executes an operation) rather than by a UI test (impossible per the Musio.Tests/Musio.Core-only constraint), and pinned the same null↔value round trip at the `EditOperation` level in the new test file instead.
- **`UpdateTransitionOperation`**: models on `UpdateTextOverlayPropertiesOperation` but snapshots the **whole nullable `TransitionConfig`** up front (`_previous = segment.InTransition` before mutating) rather than diffing individual properties — the same shape as `RemoveTextOverlayOperation`/`AddTextOverlayOperation` needing a full snapshot, except here the "removed" state IS `null` itself, which is a legitimate value, not "operation didn't run". Used a separate `_found` bool (segment existed at Execute-time) rather than `_previous is null` as the not-run guard, since the latter is ambiguous — a boundary that was already `null` before Execute must still Undo back to `null`, which is indistinguishable from "never executed" if you only check `_previous is null`.
- **"Apply to all boundaries" as ONE undo entry**: `ApplyTransitionToAllBoundariesOperation` snapshots `(segment.Id, segment.InTransition)` for every boundary (`model.Segments[1..]`, explicitly skipping index 0 — mirrors `TransitionResolver`'s own `index <= 0 -> None` rule) into a single `List<(string, TransitionConfig?)>` before overwriting, so `Undo` restores each boundary to its own individual prior value — including previously-`null` boundaries and boundaries that had a *different* config from each other — rather than collapsing everything to one shared "previous". `EditorPage`'s button handler reads the *currently selected* boundary's live config (which may itself legitimately be `null`/Automatic) and passes that same nullable value through, so "Apply to All" from an Automatic boundary is a valid, if edge-case, "clear every transition" action.
- **Duration slider clamp — made live and visible, not just enforced server-side**: `SyncTransitionUI` computes `effectiveMax = min(2.0, half(incoming.Duration), half(outgoing.Duration))` at selection time (recomputed every time the boundary is (re)selected) and sets `TransitionDurationSlider.Maximum` to it directly — the user physically cannot drag past what `TransitionResolver` will actually allow, rather than being able to drag to 2s and observe a shorter dissolve with no explanation. A `TransitionDurationClampHint` TextBlock (same `PropertyHintStyle`/conditional-Visibility idiom as `OverlayCustomPositionHint`) appears whenever `effectiveMax < 2.0`, stating the exact clamped ceiling. Guarded `sliderMax = max(Slider.Minimum, effectiveMax)` before assigning `Maximum` — WinUI throws if `Maximum < Minimum`, which an extremely short neighbouring segment could otherwise produce.
- **Duration/Easing controls disabled (not hidden) when there's nothing to tune**: `TransitionDurationSlider.IsEnabled`/`TransitionEasingCombo.IsEnabled` are only `true` when the family is a real effect (not Automatic, not Hard Cut) — mirrors the existing `MotionBlurSlider.IsEnabled = MotionBlurToggle.IsOn` convention already in this file rather than introducing a new disabled-control idiom.
- **`EditorPage` subscriptions added** (next to the existing text-overlay block): `Timeline.TransitionSelected += OnTransitionSelected` (pushes state onto the pane or hides it via `PropertiesPanel.SetPaneAvailable(Transition, false)` on `null`) and `Timeline.TransitionRemoveRequested += OnTransitionRemoveRequested` (routes to the same `DeleteTransition` helper the pane's own "Remove Transition" button uses — both set `InTransition = null` through `UpdateTransitionOperation` and keep the boundary selected/re-synced afterward, rather than closing the pane, since the underlying segment still exists and now legitimately shows "Automatic").
- **`InvalidatePreview()` called explicitly after every edit** (Family/Variant/Duration/Easing change, Apply-to-all, Remove) even though `UndoRedoManager.StateChanged` → `OnUndoRedoStateChanged` already calls it on every `Execute` — matches the existing `RefreshOverlayPreview()`-after-every-`Execute` convention in the text-overlay handlers (belt-and-suspenders against the async-dispatch path being skipped, as that region's own comment documents for `_compositorReady == false`). Since T3 already rewired `RenderFrameAtAsync` to call `TransitionResolver.Resolve(model, position)` fresh every frame directly off the live `TimelineModel`, no separate re-sync-into-renderer step (like text overlays' `SyncTextOverlaysToRenderer`) was needed — mutating `InTransition` in place is picked up automatically on the next rendered frame.
- **What didn't work / discarded**: (1) A `Remove Transition` button felt like scope creep at first since T5's right-click already exists, but added it anyway for parity with every other pane (`RemoveOverlayButton`/`RemoveSlideButton`) and because right-click discoverability is poor — it routes through the identical `DeleteTransition` helper, so it's zero extra editing-logic surface. (2) Considered re-running full `SyncTransitionUI` after every micro-edit (Variant/Duration/Easing changes) the way `OverlayPreset_SelectionChanged` does, but that pattern exists there specifically because a *preset* touches ~15 unrelated fields at once; a single Duration/Easing/Variant tweak only needs `InvalidatePreview()`, matching the lighter-weight handlers like `OverlayWidthSlider_ValueChanged`. Only the Family combo's handler re-runs full `SyncTransitionUI`, since changing family flips multiple pieces of visibility/enablement/variant-population at once.
- **Test coverage** (`TransitionEditOperationsTests.cs`, `Musio.Core`-only per the `Musio.Tests` project reference constraint — no UI test possible): apply/undo/redo for `UpdateTransitionOperation` covering all four state transitions (unconfigured→set, set→unconfigured, set→different-set, explicit-`None`↔null), a non-existent-id no-op test, and a `Update_FirstSegment_...` test documenting (not enforcing — that guarantee lives one level up) that this class trusts its caller about segment 0. `ApplyTransitionToAllBoundariesOperation` tests cover mixed starting states (null + two different configs) round-tripping independently through one Undo, redo, a null-config "clear all" case, and single-segment/empty-timeline no-ops.
- **Build & test**: ARM64 `Musio.App.csproj` build → 0 errors (only pre-existing `MVVMTK0045`/`CS0649`/`CS0169` warnings, none from this task's files). x64 `Musio.Tests.csproj` build → 0 errors. `dotnet test` (`$env:DOTNET_ROLL_FORWARD="Major"`): **919 passed, 1 failed, 2 skipped (922 total)** — the 1 failure (`ExportAudioPlanTransitionTests.ExplicitNoneTransition_SuppressesTheFadeEvenNextToASlide`) is in a sibling-owned file (`ExportAudioPlan.cs`/its test, T7's scope) confirmed via `git status` (untracked/modified by someone else) and file `LastWriteTime` timestamps landing *during* this session, i.e. mid-edit by a concurrent agent — not caused by, or fixable within, this task's file scope. `FullyQualifiedName~TransitionEditOperationsTests` alone: **15 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped**.
