## Transition properties pane review-fix (Task 6 of feature/segment-transitions, GPT-5.6 Sol review pass): `EditOperation.cs`, `TransitionEditOperationsTests.cs`, `EditorPage.xaml.cs`

- **Feature/area**: Same file set as the original T6 entry above, all siblings finished by this pass. A GPT-5.6 Sol review found 4 Medium issues; this entry documents the fix pass. `git status --porcelain`/`git diff --stat` at the end confirmed only these files changed (plus already-landed sibling files this pass never touched).
- **Bug 1 (Medium) — `UpdateTransitionOperation` accepted segment 0 as a target**: `Execute`/`Undo` located the segment purely by `Id` via `FirstOrDefault`, so nothing stopped a config from being written to `model.Segments[0].InTransition` — dormant data that would activate the moment that segment was ever reordered away from index 0. Worse, the original test (`Update_FirstSegment_...`) asserted the config WAS written, codifying the bug as "working as intended" and explaining why 15/15 green tests didn't catch it. **Fix**: `Execute`/`Undo` now resolve the segment's live index via a small `IndexOf` helper and treat `index <= 0` as not-found (mirrors `ApplyTransitionToAllBoundariesOperation`'s existing, already-correct, `for (i = 1; ...)` loop and `TransitionResolver`'s own `index <= 0 -> None` rule) — segment 0 can never receive or restore a config through this operation, full stop. **Reversed the test's assertion** (`Assert.IsNull` in place of `Assert.IsNotNull`, plus an added `Undo` is-also-a-no-op check) rather than deleting it, so the invariant stays pinned under its original descriptive name. Audited every other test in the file for the same mistake — none of the others assumed segment 0 could receive a config (`ApplyToAll_*` tests already asserted `IsNull` on segment 0 correctly, since `ApplyTransitionToAllBoundariesOperation` never had this bug).
- **Bug 2 (Medium) — pane went stale after undo/redo and after opening a different project**: `SyncTransitionUI` was only ever called from `TransitionSelected`'s own handler, never from `OnUndoRedoStateChanged` or the `ModelReloaded` callback — so Ctrl+Z after a transition edit left the pane showing the just-undone value (with the model itself correctly reverted), and switching projects could leave a stale pane open referencing a segment id from the OLD model. **Fix — mirrored the existing zoom-selection pattern in both handlers exactly, rather than inventing a new one**: in `OnUndoRedoStateChanged`, added a check that re-resolves `_selectedTransitionId`'s boundary via `GetTransitionBoundarySegments` on every state change — if it still resolves (both `Incoming`/`Outgoing` non-null), calls `SyncTransitionUI(transitionId)` to re-pull the (possibly just-undone) live value onto the controls; if it no longer resolves (segment removed/reordered by the undo/redo itself), calls `Timeline.ClearTransitionSelection()` (T5's own method — not reimplemented) exactly like the pre-existing `Timeline.SelectedZoomKeyframeId`/`ClearZoomSelection()` check right above it. In the `ModelReloaded` callback, added a single `Timeline.ClearTransitionSelection()` call alongside the pre-existing `Timeline.ClearZoomSelection()`/`Timeline.ClearClipSelection()` — since `ClearTransitionSelection()` raises `TransitionSelected(null)` (confirmed by reading T5's implementation) which routes through the already-existing `OnTransitionSelected` handler to reset `_selectedTransitionId` and hide the pane, no new hide-the-pane logic was needed at all.
- **Bug 3 (Medium) — the duration clamp hint could display a number the resolver would not honour**: at a very short neighbouring segment (`TrimSegmentEdgeOperation.MinDuration` = 100ms halves to 50ms), the true resolver clamp (`effectiveMax`) could fall below the slider's own 0.1s `Minimum`. The old code did `sliderMax = Math.Max(Minimum, effectiveMax)` to avoid a `Maximum < Minimum` WinUI exception, then displayed `sliderMax` (i.e. 0.1s) in the hint — letting the user persist 100ms while `TransitionResolver` silently rendered 50ms, defeating the entire point of surfacing the clamp. **Fix**: introduced `tooShortForSlider = effectiveMax < Slider.Minimum`; when true, the slider is disabled (`IsEnabled = false`) and the hint always states the true `effectiveMax` (never the substituted `sliderMax`) with explicit wording that the boundary "will render at exactly that length regardless of the value shown above." When `effectiveMax >= Minimum`, behaviour is unchanged (slider still clamps live to `effectiveMax`, hint states `effectiveMax`). Chose "disable + tell the truth" over "lower the slider's XAML `Minimum` to 50ms" because the true floor is boundary-dependent (`TrimSegmentEdgeOperation.MinDuration` sets the shortest *segment*, not the shortest *transition* — a segment can theoretically be shorter still via other paths) and hardcoding a lower bound into the XAML would just move the same lie to a different threshold rather than eliminating it.
- **Bug 4 (Medium) — no test protected the `null`/explicit-`None`/configured distinction through actual save→open serialization**: all 15 existing tests were in-memory-only (`EditOperation.Execute`/`Undo` against a `TimelineModel`), and the pre-existing `MusioPackageTests.cs` had zero `InTransition` assertions. **Fix**: added a new `TransitionPackageRoundTripTests` test class (own `[TestClass]`, own temp-dir `[TestInitialize]`/`[TestCleanup]`, in the SAME file as `TransitionEditOperationsTests.cs` per the review's explicit "your own new test file, not `MusioPackageTests.cs`" instruction) with one round-trip test building a 4-segment timeline mixing all three states (segment 0 = no boundary/must stay null; segment 1 = `null`/unconfigured; segment 2 = explicit `TransitionConfig { Type = None }`; segment 3 = fully configured `WhipPanLeft`/0.85s/`EaseInOut`) through `MusioPackageService.SaveAsync`/`OpenAsync`, asserting each segment's reloaded `InTransition` (or lack thereof) independently, including `Type`/`Duration`/`Easing` on the two non-null cases. Mirrors `MusioPackageTests.BuildProject`'s helper shape (temp source/working folders, `WriteFile` for dummy media) rather than reusing its private helpers directly, since those are `private` to a class this task was told not to edit.
- **Verification discipline**: re-built both the x64 test project and the ARM64 app project after every fix batch (not just at the end), confirming 0 `: error` matches each time before moving to the next issue, to avoid discovering a compounding mistake only after all four fixes were made.
- **Build & test**: ARM64 `Musio.App.csproj` → exit code 0, tail shows `Musio.App -> ...\Musio.App.dll` with no errors. x64 `Musio.Tests.csproj` → 0 errors. Full suite: **921 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped (923 total)** — up from the review's baseline of 920 by exactly the 1 new round-trip test; the previously-failing sibling test (`ExportAudioPlanTransitionTests`, T7's file, out of scope) now passes since T7 finished before this pass began. `FullyQualifiedName~TransitionEditOperationsTests|FullyQualifiedName~TransitionPackageRoundTripTests` alone: **16 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped** (15 unit tests + 1 new round-trip test).

## Transition rendering review-fix (Task 4 of feature/segment-transitions, GPT-5.6 Sol review pass): `TransitionRenderer.cs`, `TransitionRendererTests.cs`

- **Feature/area**: Same two files as the original T4 entry above. A GPT-5.6 Sol code review found 5 issues (1 High, 4 Medium) in the initial landing; this entry documents the fix pass. Scope stayed identical (only `TransitionRenderer.cs` + new `TransitionRendererTests.cs`); `git status --porcelain` at the end confirmed no sibling files were touched.
- **Bug 1 (High) — render-target leak on the exception path**: `Render` allocated `target` before the `using var ds = target.CreateDrawingSession()`, so only the drawing session was disposed on failure — any exception from session creation or a helper/effect leaked the native `CanvasRenderTarget`. **Fix**: extracted the switch/drawing-session body into a new `private static void RenderInto(CanvasRenderTarget target, ...)`; `Render` now does `try { RenderInto(...); } catch { target.Dispose(); throw; } return target;` — ownership transfers to the caller only on successful return.
- **Bug 2 (Medium) — blur amount was resolution-dependent**: `RenderZoomBlur`/`RenderWhipPan` applied `GaussianBlurEffect`/`DirectionalBlurEffect` in each input bitmap's OWN source-pixel space, then relied on `DrawImage`'s dest/src `Rect` overload to scale into output space — this divides the *effective* output-space blur radius by that input's own source→output scale factor, so a 320px input and a 1920px input blended into the same 100px output ended up blurred by a ~6x-different amount (reviewer's own worked example, confirmed independently: a 64px vs 640px hard-edge bitmap into the same 200x200 output gave edge-widths of 112px vs 20px, a 5.6x ratio, pre-fix). **Fix chosen: compensate the blur "amount" scalar by the source→output scale factor** (`CompensateBlurForScale` = isotropic, geometric-mean of scaleX/scaleY, clamped to [0,250] — Direct2D's blur-radius ceiling; `CompensateDirectionalBlurForScale` = single-axis for the 1-D directional blur) rather than allocating an output-sized intermediate `CanvasRenderTarget` per input and blurring there. Chose compensation over intermediates because it's pure arithmetic (no extra GPU allocation/draw per frame) and this renders 30-60x/sec during export — the per-frame cost is unchanged (still exactly 2 effect-graph draws per helper, same as before the fix). Verified by reverting to the raw uncompensated `MaxBlur * envelope` and re-running the new `RenderZoomBlur_BlurWidthInOutputSpace_IsComparableAcrossDifferentSourceResolutions` test: failed pre-fix with `ratio=5.60` (matching the reviewer's ~6x prediction almost exactly), passed post-fix.
- **Bug 3 (Medium) — Glitch/CrossFade with null outgoing didn't fade to black**: `RenderCrossFade` only cleared to black in an `outgoing is null` branch; `RenderGlitch` delegates its base layer to `RenderCrossFade`, so both were affected. **Fix — root-caused rather than patched locally**: made `RenderCrossFade` clear to opaque black UNCONDITIONALLY (see bug-3b below for why this needed to be unconditional, not just moved into a null-check), which fixes both `CrossFade` directly and `Glitch` via delegation with no Glitch-specific code. **Audited every other helper for the same gap while in there** and found two more, undiscovered by the reviewer: `RenderFade` (used by `Fade`/`DipToWhite`) skipped drawing entirely when outgoing was null during the first half, which for `DipToWhite` (dips through WHITE) showed solid white instead of black at progress 0 — fixed by filling black at the same opacity the real outgoing bitmap would have used. `RenderSlide` (`SlideLeft/Right/Up/Down`) had NO null-outgoing handling at all (left the native transparent background showing wherever incoming hadn't slid over yet) — fixed with the same `else { ds.Clear(Black); }` pattern `RenderWipe`/`RenderPush`/`RenderWhipPan` already used correctly. Verified all four fixes (CrossFade/Glitch, Fade/DipToWhite, Slide*) by individually reverting each and re-running `Render_NullOutgoing_AtProgress0_IsOpaqueBlack`: reverting CrossFade's clear failed for `CrossFade` AND `Glitch`; reverting Slide's clear failed for all 4 `Slide*` types; each passed again once restored.
- **Bug 3b (found via the new pixel tests, NOT in the reviewer's list, but directly tightly-coupled to bug 3's fix) — partial-opacity layers over a transparent background don't composite to opaque**: even with `outgoing` present, `RenderCrossFade` drew `outgoing` at `(1-progress)` opacity then `incoming` at `progress` opacity directly onto the render target's native (transparent) background with no prior clear — for two fully-opaque-RGBA source bitmaps, the resulting alpha channel is `(1-progress) + progress*progress`, which is `< 1` (translucent output) for any `0 < progress < 1`. Same issue independently found in `RenderWhipPan` (there triggered even at `progress=0`/full nominal opacity, apparently from `DirectionalBlurEffect`'s soft border-mode producing a partially-transparent edge sample when a small bitmap is scaled up steeply — one source-edge pixel maps to many output pixels). **Fix**: both helpers now unconditionally `ds.Clear(Black)` before any partial-opacity draws (matching the pattern `RenderZoomBlur` already used) — once the destination is opaque, the standard "over" compositing operator always yields `resultAlpha = srcAlpha + dstAlpha*(1-srcAlpha) = srcAlpha + (1-srcAlpha) = 1` regardless of how many translucent layers stack on top, so this is robust to future additional layers too. Found via the new `Render_EveryTransitionType_CoversEntireOutput_NoTransparentGaps` test asserting `pixel.A == 255` at sampled points (corners/edges/center) — a test that only checks target *dimensions* (the pre-review-fix baseline) can never catch this, since alpha is invisible to a `SizeInPixels` assertion. `RenderPush`/`RenderSlide` were NOT affected (they draw at full, not partial, opacity, relying on positioning rather than blending) — confirmed by the same coverage test passing for those types without any change.
- **Bug 4 (Medium) — `None`/`default` hard cut didn't scale a mismatched input**: `ds.DrawImage(incoming)` with no dest/src `Rect` draws at the bitmap's native pixel size — crops a larger input, leaves a smaller one's remainder transparent. Reviewer correctly noted this is pre-existing but newly, genuinely reachable (`None` is user-selectable and the default for any unconfigured boundary). **Fix**: `ds.DrawImage(incoming, new Rect(0,0,width,height), new Rect(0,0,incoming.SizeInPixels.Width, incoming.SizeInPixels.Height))` — same dest/src-Rect pattern every other helper already used. Verified by reverting to the unscaled call and re-running both `Render_EveryTransitionType_CoversEntireOutput_NoTransparentGaps` and the new `Render_LargeInputIntoSmallOutput_ScalesEntireImage_RatherThanCropping` (a 400x400 bitmap with a right-edge green marker band, rendered via each type at progress=1 into a 50x50 output, asserting the marker reappears near the output's own right edge): both failed pre-fix for `None` specifically (`alpha=0` at the uncovered corner; marker `G=0`, i.e., invisible/cropped) and passed once restored. All other 19 types passed both tests even pre-fix, confirming the bug was isolated to `None`/`default`.
- **Bug 5 (Medium) — tests couldn't have caught any of the above**: the original test suite only asserted `Render` didn't throw and that the returned target's `SizeInPixels` matched the constructor arguments — both true regardless of what was actually drawn. **Fix — added real pixel-level assertions**, all driven by `Enum.GetValues<TransitionType>()` (so future enum members are automatically covered) except where a type is legitimately exempt: (a) `Render_EveryTransitionType_CoversEntireOutput_NoTransparentGaps` — small-into-large and large-into-small, both with/without outgoing, samples 9 points (corners/edge-midpoints/center) and asserts `alpha==255`; (b) `Render_LargeInputIntoSmallOutput_ScalesEntireImage_RatherThanCropping` — right-edge-marker bitmap, regression test for bug 4; (c) `Render_AtProgress0_MatchesOutgoing_AndAtProgress1_MatchesIncoming` — same-size solid-colour bitmaps, centre-pixel colour check with a small tolerance, special-cased only for `TransitionType.None` (which hard-cuts to incoming at BOTH endpoints, by design — documented in the test why this is not itself a bug); (d) `Render_NullOutgoing_AtProgress0_IsOpaqueBlack` (excludes `None`, which is exempt from the fade-from-black contract) — regression test for bug 3/3b; (e) `RenderZoomBlur_BlurWidthInOutputSpace_IsComparableAcrossDifferentSourceResolutions` — regression test for bug 2, using a relative (amplitude-invariant) edge-width proxy (count of scanline pixels between the 10th/90th percentile of that scanline's own min/max) rather than an absolute pixel comparison, specifically so the test is robust to the two cases rendering at different contrast/opacity levels; (f) `GlitchSliceOffset_MatchesKnownLiteralValues` — pins the deterministic formula against 10 literal `(sliceIndex, progress) → value` pairs pre-computed independently in PowerShell from the known formula (Knuth hash mod 997 for phase, alternating sign, `sin`-based envelope), rather than only the pre-existing "two calls agree" test (which would still pass if the function secretly depended on hidden mutable state that just didn't happen to change between two calls in the same run).
- **Verified every new/changed pixel assertion actually fails pre-fix**: for bugs 2, 3, 3b, and 4, temporarily reverted each specific fix in isolation, rebuilt, and re-ran only the relevant new test(s), confirming a genuine failure with the exact expected symptom (leak N/A — bug 1 has no pixel-level test, verified by code inspection of the try/catch/dispose structure instead), then restored the fix and re-ran to confirm it passed again. This is stronger than "the test currently passes" — it demonstrates the test would have caught the original bug.
- **`Assert.Inconclusive` GPU-unavailability pattern**: kept unchanged/intact in every new test (checked `TryCreateDevice() is null` first, exactly like the pre-existing tests) — the review's fifth issue was about assertion strength, not about the skip convention, which remains correct as-is.
- **Build & test**: `Musio.Core.csproj` and `Musio.Tests.csproj` both built via the verified MSBuild command, 0 errors (pre-existing CS0618 warnings from sibling files only). `dotnet test` (`$env:DOTNET_ROLL_FORWARD="Major"`): full suite **894 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped (896 total)** — risen from the prior T4 entry's 790 because sibling agents (T2/T7 review-fix passes, etc.) landed more tests concurrently; filtering to `FullyQualifiedName~TransitionRendererTests` showed **130 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped** (up from 49, reflecting the new pixel-assertion tests described above). No previously-passing test regressed.

## Rolling transitions in the LIVE PREVIEW review-fix (Task 3 of feature/segment-transitions, GPT-5.6 Sol review pass): `EditorPage.xaml.cs` only

- **Feature/area**: Same single file as the original T3 entry above. A GPT-5.6 Sol review of that landing found 4 issues (1 High, 3 Medium); this entry documents the fix pass. Scope stayed identical (`EditorPage.xaml.cs` only) even though a sibling (T6, the transition properties pane) was concurrently editing `EditorPage.PropertyPanels.cs` and adding new subscriptions to `EditorPage.xaml.cs` itself during this pass — re-read the file fresh before editing and confirmed via `git diff` afterward that none of T6's additions were touched or reverted.
- **Bug 1 (High) — endless renderer-rebuild loop between the two sides of a transition**: `ComposePreviewFrameAtOffsetAsync`'s primary-file branch called `EnsurePrimaryRendererForSegmentAsync(seg)`, which rebuilds the SINGLE `_previewRenderer` to match whichever segment last asked for it. Composing a dissolve calls this once for the incoming segment and once for the outgoing one; if the two primary-file segments carry DIFFERENT `FrameStyleOverride`/`CursorStyleOverride`, incoming's compose rebuilds the singleton for its own style, outgoing's compose then sees a mismatch and rebuilds it back, and incoming's NEXT tick sees a mismatch again — an unbounded rebuild loop (each rebuild reopens cursor data and reallocates render targets on the UI thread) that froze the app on exactly the scenario this feature exists to support. **Fix, mirroring `SegmentFrameComposer`'s `SourceKey`-keyed context cache**: added a new `_primaryStyleRenderers` dictionary keyed by the effective `(BackgroundStyle, CursorStyle)` pair (both are `record`s, so structural equality works as a dictionary key with no custom comparer needed) holding independent `PreviewRenderer` instances, bounded to `MaxCachedPrimaryStyleRenderers = 3` with LRU eviction (`TrimPrimaryStyleRendererCache`, disposing evicted entries — mirrors `TrimSegmentPreviewCache`'s existing shape for appended segments). New `GetPrimaryTransitionCompositorAsync(seg)` resolves a segment's compositor for transition-composing ONLY: the common case (style matches whatever the singleton is currently built with — true for any unconfigured project, or when neither transition side carries an override) short-circuits straight to `_previewRenderer` with zero new allocation; only a genuine per-segment-style mismatch builds (once) or looks up (every tick after) an alt-style `PreviewRenderer` that shares the SAME `_frameReader`-decoded bitmap but composites it separately — so composing outgoing NEVER touches state incoming depends on, or vice versa, because neither side's compose can ever mutate the other's compositor. The ordinary (non-transition) render path still calls `EnsurePrimaryRendererForSegmentAsync` exactly as before (that call site — `RenderSegmentVideoAsync`'s caller in `RenderFrameAtAsync` — was left untouched), so normal single-style playback costs exactly what it did before this fix.
  - **Why the alternation loop cannot recur**: verified by construction, not just by testing — `GetPrimaryTransitionCompositorAsync` never writes to `_previewRenderer`, `_primaryRenderBackground`, `_primaryRenderCursor`, or `_compositorReady` (the four fields `EnsurePrimaryRendererForSegmentAsync`'s rebuild mutates); it only reads them (for the common-case short-circuit check) and writes into the SEPARATE `_primaryStyleRenderers` dictionary. Since composing either side of a transition (via `ComposePreviewFrameAtOffsetAsync`) now calls this method instead of `EnsurePrimaryRendererForSegmentAsync`, neither side's compose can flip the singleton's recorded style out from under the other — there is no shared mutable state left for the two composes to fight over.
  - Also invalidated correctly: cleared+disposed (`DisposePrimaryStyleRenderers`, which also bumps `_primaryPreviewStateGeneration`, see bug 2) at every site that already tears down/rebuilds `_previewRenderer` — `Unloaded`, `RecoverGraphicsDeviceAsync`, `InitializePreviewCoreAsync`, and the start of `RebuildPreviewRendererCoreAsync` (a global-config or per-segment-override change invalidates every previously-built alt-style entry, since they were built by layering the OLD global config). Deliberately NOT cleared in `ApplyPreviewResolutionAsync` (the adaptive-quality decode-resolution swap) — alt-style compositors, like the singleton itself, are independent of decode resolution (`RenderPreviewFrame` accepts whatever size bitmap the current `_frameReader` happens to produce), so clearing there would just discard warm cache for no correctness benefit; confirmed this matches how `_previewRenderer` itself is (not) touched in that method.
- **Bug 2 (Medium) — async invalidation still uses disposed/stale state, and bitmaps leak on throw**: two related races/leaks in `ComposePreviewFrameAtOffsetAsync`. (a) Graphics recovery, `RebuildPreviewRendererCoreAsync`, or a `DisposeSegmentPreviews()`-driven cache clear can run to completion (this is single-threaded UI-thread interleaving at await points, not true parallelism, but the effect on a resumed continuation is the same as a race) while this method is awaiting a frame decode or a webcam extraction, leaving the continuation to use a disposed-or-replaced primary renderer/reader or a disposed appended-segment context. (b) When composing threw, the decoded `bitmap`/`segBitmap` (GPU resources) had not yet reached their `.Dispose()` call and were not covered by any `finally`, so an exception leaked them (the outer `RenderFrameAtAsync` try/catch contained the failure but never saw the bitmap to clean it up). **Fix**: reintroduced the generation-guard concept the original T3 pass had deliberately retired (correctly, for the crossfade CACHE specifically — that hazard is gone) but had not replaced for this DIFFERENT hazard (disposal racing an in-flight compose). Added a new `private int _primaryPreviewStateGeneration`, bumped everywhere `_previewRenderer`/`_frameReader` are disposed or replaced (`Unloaded`, `RecoverGraphicsDeviceAsync`, `InitializePreviewCoreAsync`, `RebuildPreviewRendererCoreAsync`'s start via `DisposePrimaryStyleRenderers`, and `ApplyPreviewResolutionAsync` after its reader swap). The primary branch snapshots `reader = _frameReader` and `stateGen = _primaryPreviewStateGeneration` right after resolving the compositor (itself now possibly-awaiting), then re-validates both (`stateGen != _primaryPreviewStateGeneration || !ReferenceEquals(reader, _frameReader)`) immediately after the frame-decode await and again after the webcam-extraction await, bailing to `null` on any mismatch rather than compositing a stale bitmap against replaced/disposed state. The appended-segment branch reuses the EXISTING `_segmentPreviewGeneration` field the same way (captured right after `GetOrBuildSegmentPreviewAsync` returns a confirmed-valid `ctx`, re-checked after the decode await and again after the webcam-extraction await) rather than adding a redundant second counter. Both branches' bitmap handling was restructured into `try { ... } finally { bitmap.Dispose(); }` (previously the primary branch disposed inline in 3 different spots and had no coverage for a throw from `RenderPreviewFrame`/`SetWebcamFrameForPreviewAsync`), so the decoded bitmap is disposed on every path including an exception — matching the review's literal ask.
- **Bug 3 (Medium) — appended-source compositor duration diverges from export**: `GetOrBuildSegmentPreviewAsync` initialized an appended segment's compositor with only THAT segment's own `SourceStart + SourceDuration`, while `SegmentFrameComposer.ResolveSourceDuration` (exporter) uses the longest extent of every segment sharing that source file. Since `FrameCompositor.InitializeAsync` truncates cursor/auto-zoom timelines to the supplied duration, an outgoing segment whose rolled `OutgoingLocalOffset` (T1) advances past its own cut into a same-source neighbour's range had its preview cursor/auto-zoom frozen at the cut while export kept them going — a direct, visible preview/export divergence, exactly the failure class this whole feature exists to eliminate, and one the original T3 pass's own doc comment mistakenly claimed was already mirrored (it mirrored the "span past SourceDuration to the segment's own SourceStart+SourceDuration" idea, but not the "longest extent across ALL segments sharing the file" part). **Fix**: added `private static TimeSpan ResolveSegmentSourceDuration(TimelineModel model, string videoFilePath, TimeSpan fallback)`, mirroring `SegmentFrameComposer.ResolveSourceDuration` exactly (scans `model.Segments.OfType<VideoSegment>()` for same-file segments, keeps the max `SourceStart+SourceDuration`), and now passes its result instead of the bare per-segment value into `renderer.InitializeAsync(...)`. The PRIMARY recording needed no equivalent fix: `RebuildPreviewRendererCoreAsync` already initializes the singleton with `project.Duration`, which by construction spans every primary-file segment regardless of how the timeline is split.
- **Bug 4 (Medium) — adaptive quality never observed video→slide transition load**: `IsVideoPosition` (the gate deciding whether `UpdatePreviewFrameAsync` feeds a frame's elapsed time to `_adaptivePreviewQuality.ObservePlaybackFrame`) only checked the segment AT the playhead via `GetSegmentAtTime`. During an active video→slide dissolve the playhead sits over the (cheap) incoming slide, so this was always false even though the OUTGOING side recomposes a full, uniquely-offset video frame every tick under the rolling model — new per-tick cost the legacy cache (which composed that same outgoing side only once per dissolve) never created, and so was never visible to the feedback loop before. **Fix**: `IsVideoPosition` now additionally resolves `TransitionResolver.Resolve(model, position)` and returns true when the resolution is active AND either `IncomingSegment` or `OutgoingSegment` is a `VideoSegment` — cheap since `Resolve` is pure/already called once per tick elsewhere, so this second call adds negligible cost of its own.
- **Build & test**: ARM64 `Musio.App.csproj` → 0 errors, 0 warnings from `EditorPage.xaml.cs` (still zero `CS0618`). x64 `Musio.Tests.csproj` → 0 errors. `dotnet test`: hit 3 different **transient** failures across repeated runs during this pass — `ExportAudioPlanTransitionTests.ExplicitNoneTransition_SuppressesTheFadeEvenNextToASlide`, a `DebugScratchTests.Debug_ResolveAtSlideBoundary` (a scratch file that no longer exists on disk by the time it was investigated — `Test-Path` returned `False`), and later a batch of 4 more `ExportAudioPlanTransitionTests` failures — every one of them confined to `ExportAudioPlan.cs`/its test file, confirmed via `git diff --stat`/`git status` to be a sibling's actively-in-progress, uncommitted work (262 insertions/16 deletions mid-edit), never touched by this task. Proved this task's own change was clean by running `--filter "FullyQualifiedName!~ExportAudioPlanTransitionTests"` (898 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped) and then, after waiting ~45s for the sibling to settle, a full unfiltered run: **920 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped (922 total)** — 0 failures, no drop in any previously-passing test.

## Wiring preview audio fade windows (Task 9 of feature/segment-transitions): concluded NOT FEASIBLE — `AudioPlaybackEngine.cs` and `ExportAudioPlan.cs` remarks corrected, `EditorPage.xaml.cs` call sites documented but left unwired

- **Feature/area**: T7 built `AudioFadeWindow`/`EqualPowerFadeSampleProvider`/the windowed `AudioPlaybackEngine.Load` overload but could not wire it because `EditorPage.xaml.cs` was owned by T3 at the time. This task's job was to build fade windows from `TransitionResolver` and pass them into the two `_audioPlayer.Load(...)` call sites in `EditorPage.xaml.cs` (now at lines ~4936 and ~5007, inside `LoadAudioWaveformAsync` and `ReloadAudioPlayer` respectively) — **or**, if the mapping cannot be done honestly, document why instead of shipping a drifting crossfade.
- **Approach tried**: Traced the exact time domain `AudioFadeWindow.Start`/`Duration` are compared against (`AudioFileReader.CurrentTime`, i.e. the loaded WAV's own native seconds-from-file-start — confirmed via `EqualPowerFadeSampleProvider.GainAt`/`Read` in `AudioPlaybackEngine.cs`, which captures `position.CurrentTime` before every `source.Read`). Then traced how `EditorPage.xaml.cs` currently maps OUTPUT/authoring time (`Preview.PlayheadPosition`/`Timeline.PlayheadPosition`, the same clock `TransitionResolver.Resolve(timeline, outputTime)` takes) into that native-file time: `AudioPositionForVideo(videoPosition) => videoPosition + TimeSpan.FromSeconds(_audioOffsetSeconds)` — a single **constant** offset, re-verified unchanged in the CURRENT file (post T3's rolling-preview rework, which only touched VIDEO composition, not this method) via `grep`. Confirmed via `LoadAudioWaveformAsync`/`ReloadAudioPlayer`/`GetUnmutedAudioPaths` that the ONLY files ever passed to `_audioPlayer.Load` are `project.AudioFilePaths` (the PRIMARY recording's own continuous system/mic WAVs, filtered only by mute state) — never `VideoSegment.AudioFilePaths` (appended recordings' own audio, which `LoadAppendedTrackVisualsAsync` loads ONLY to build timeline waveform visuals, never into the playback engine).
- **What didn't work / why wiring was rejected**: A `TransitionResolver` boundary exists only where the timeline has a cut/trim/reorder/insertion — precisely the condition under which "output time minus one constant offset" stops corresponding to the right instant in the raw continuously-played file (the two sides of a real cut are two far-apart or even out-of-order points in the source file, not points a fixed distance apart). Converting a transition's output-time window into native-file time via that constant offset would therefore place the audible ramp over unrelated content, not the actual moment of the edit — drifting on exactly the projects that have a transition to show, which is strictly worse than today's honest hard cut. Two further, independent blockers confirmed the same conclusion: (1) a `VideoSegment` ↔ `TextSlideSegment` boundary (the common legacy-fallback case `TransitionResolver` applies to unconfigured slide edges) has no recorded audio on the slide side at all, so there is no file position for half the crossfade to reference regardless of offset arithmetic; (2) appended recordings' own audio is never loaded into `_audioPlayer` in the first place, so most cross-recording transition boundaries in a multi-clip project have no loaded file to attach a window to, independent of point 1. A real fix requires rebuilding this engine's preview call sites around the same per-segment/per-placement model `ExportAudioPlan.BuildFromSegments` already uses for export (delay + trim + take-duration keyed to each segment, not one constant offset per whole project) — a materially larger architecture change than adding a windows parameter, and out of scope for "wire the existing overload up".
- **What worked**: Documented this precisely instead of forcing a subtly-wrong crossfade — matching T7's own honest treatment of the (structurally different) export-side WinRT limitation. Rewrote `AudioPlaybackEngine.cs`'s class `<remarks>` with a 3-point enumerated explanation (constant-offset mapping breaks at real cuts; slide boundaries have no audio on one side; appended-segment audio isn't even loaded) and what a real fix would require. Corrected `ExportAudioPlan.cs`'s top-of-file `NOTE` comment and its class-remarks "Preview does not crossfade" paragraph, which previously said a "follow-up task (already queued)... must derive AudioFadeWindows" — since that follow-up (this task) was attempted and closed as infeasible, left-over "still pending" language would have misled the next reader into re-attempting the same rejected approach. Left both `EditorPage.xaml.cs` call sites (`_audioPlayer.Load(validPaths)` and `_audioPlayer.Load(paths)`) functionally unchanged, adding only a one-line comment at each pointing to `AudioPlaybackEngine`'s remarks so a future reader sees immediately that the missing `fadeWindowsByPath` argument is deliberate, not an oversight.
- **Build & test**: ARM64 `Musio.App.csproj` → 0 errors (only pre-existing, unrelated `MVVMTK0045` warnings from ViewModels). x64 `Musio.Tests.csproj` → 0 errors. `dotnet test`: **921 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped (923 total)** — no new tests added (no new pure-testable logic was extracted, since the conclusion was "do not wire"), no regression from the prior 920/0/2 baseline (the +1 passed reflects unrelated sibling work already landed in the tree, not this task).

## T8: feature/segment-transitions integration sweep — consolidation, back-compat verification, cross-cutting edge cases

- **Feature/area**: Final integration pass across the whole feature (T1-T7/T9 above). Scope: deduplicate the one remaining split-brain helper between preview/export, verify existing (no-`InTransition`) projects render byte-identically to before the feature, and reason through/test split/reorder/ripple-delete/speed/short-segment edge cases. Did not touch `TransitionRenderer.cs`, `ExportAudioPlan.cs`, `AudioPlaybackEngine.cs`, or `TransitionPropertiesView`/`PropertiesPane` — those sub-features were already complete and green.
- **Approaches tried — consolidating `CollapseContiguousSourceBoundary`**: confirmed (by diffing both copies line-for-line) that T3's `EditorPage.xaml.cs` private copy was byte-for-byte identical in behaviour to T2's `internal` `SegmentFrameComposer` version (same `OrdinalIgnoreCase` file-path compare, same "no incoming video context → no-op", same "genuine trimmed gap (outgoing < incoming) → no-op", same `incomingTime - 1/fps` collapse floored at zero, same `fps <= 0 → 30fps` fallback). Promoted `SegmentFrameComposer.CollapseContiguousSourceBoundary` from `internal` to **`public`** (kept it ON `SegmentFrameComposer`, in `Musio.Core.Export`, rather than extracting to a new home in `Musio.Core.Timeline`/`Processing`: it is fundamentally an export/preview *composition* policy, not a `TransitionResolver`-level timeline concern — `TransitionResolver` stays pure/timeline-only and knows nothing about source files or fps; moving it there would have been a bigger, riskier refactor for no behavioural gain since `Musio.Core.Export` was already `using`'d by `EditorPage.xaml.cs`). Deleted the `EditorPage.xaml.cs` private copy entirely and repointed its one call site to `SegmentFrameComposer.CollapseContiguousSourceBoundary(...)`. Updated every doc comment that described the old file as having "no equivalent existing safety net" / "deliberately re-implemented" to instead point at the shared, now-`public` method. Rebuilt both x64 tests and ARM64 app afterward — 0 errors, confirming both pipelines now call the exact same code.
- **Checked whether `ClampSourceTime`/`SelectAvailableSourceDuration` needed the same treatment and confirmed they do NOT**: re-verified T3's original reasoning still holds by reading `VideoFrameReader.GetFrameIndex` directly — `Math.Clamp(index, 0, FrameCount - 1)` against the reader's own frame count is exactly the same bound `ClampSourceTime`/`SelectAvailableSourceDuration` compute explicitly (`VideoFrameReader.Duration = FrameCount/fps`), and BOTH the primary preview reader and every appended-segment `ctx.Reader` are genuine `VideoFrameReader`s that pass every source time straight into `LoadFrameAtTimeAsync`. So preview relies on an *implicit* clamp (the reader's own bounds-check) where export uses an *explicit* one (`ClampSourceTime`) — behaviourally identical, but the divergence in mechanism was already called out prominently in `ComposePreviewFrameAtOffsetAsync`'s XML `<remarks>` (a T3 addition); this pass strengthened that comment further rather than leaving it as a bare one-line note, and did not promote either helper to `public` since there is no actual behavioural gap to close.
- **What worked — back-compat verification (reasoning + reading code, since these are exactly the invariants `TransitionResolverTests.cs` already pins)**: confirmed via `TransitionResolver.Resolve`'s legacy-fallback branch (`incoming is not TextSlideSegment && outgoing is not TextSlideSegment → None`) that video→video boundaries with no `InTransition` still hard-cut, and slide-touching boundaries with no `InTransition` still get exactly `SlideTransitions.DefaultDuration` (500ms) + `TransitionEasing.Linear` (pinned by `#pragma warning disable CS0618` around that one intentional dependency). Confirmed `TransitionType`'s 20 members and `TransitionEasing`'s 4 members are strictly append-only (no reordering/renaming) by reading the enum bodies directly — both matter because `MusioPackage.JsonOptions` registers a `JsonStringEnumConverter`, so enum values round-trip **by name**, and reordering would have been a silent, undetectable-by-build format break. Confirmed `TransitionConfig`'s three properties (`Type`/`Duration`/`Easing`) are unrenamed. Confirmed T6's `TransitionPackageRoundTripTests.SaveThenOpen_PreservesNullExplicitNoneAndConfigured_AsDistinctPerBoundaryStates` genuinely exercises the null-vs-`Type=None` distinction through real `MusioPackageService.SaveAsync`/`OpenAsync` (not just in-memory `EditOperation` snapshots) by reading `MusioPackage.JsonOptions`'s `DefaultIgnoreCondition = JsonIgnoreCondition.WhenWritingNull` — a `null` `InTransition` is omitted from the JSON entirely and a missing property deserializes back to `null` (the record's own default), while an explicit `Type = None` config is a real object and round-trips as one; this test would fail immediately if that ignore-condition or the property's nullability were ever changed. Legacy (pre-`InTransition`) `.musio` files need no dedicated new test beyond this: since `InTransition` has always been optional/omitted-when-null, a truly old file is byte-for-byte the same shape as any current file with unconfigured boundaries, which is already what dozens of pre-existing `MusioPackageTests.cs` round-trips exercise every run (and directly mirrors the precedent `Open_ToleratesLegacyPerSegmentTextOverlays` already set for a different retired field).
- **Cross-cutting edge cases — new `TransitionCrossCuttingTests.cs`** (7 new tests, `Musio.Core`-only per the `Musio.Tests`/`Musio.Core`-reference constraint): held-frame-fallback-on-trim-to-EOF and the `min(configured, half(incoming), half(outgoing))` duration clamp were already fully covered by existing tests (`SegmentFrameComposerTransitionTests`'s `ClampSourceTime`/`SelectAvailableSourceDuration` cases; `TransitionResolverTests`'s `Resolve_DurationClampedByShortIncomingNeighbour`/`Outgoing`) — added nothing duplicate there. Newly covered:
  - **Reorder + boundary identity (the task's own "especially" case)**: confirmed by direct test that a transition is identified by, and travels with, the INCOMING segment's own object reference — `ReorderSegmentOperation`/`MoveSegmentOperation` move the same segment (never clearing/copying `InTransition`), so after a reorder the SAME configured effect dissolves from whatever NEW predecessor now precedes that segment. **This is coherent by design, not broken** — verified it holds even through the sharpest sub-case: moving a transition-owning segment to index 0 leaves its `InTransition` non-null but dormant (`TransitionResolver`'s `index <= 0 → None` rule silences it, exactly as `UpdateTransitionOperation.Execute`'s own guard-comment already documented), and moving it away from index 0 again reactivates the identical config against its new predecessor — no data loss, no crash, matches the pre-existing code comment's stated expectation.
  - **Ripple-delete (`RemoveSegmentOperation`)**: removing the OUTGOING side of a configured boundary leaves the incoming segment's config untouched, now dissolving against its new predecessor (coherent, same reasoning as reorder); removing the segment that OWNS the config deletes it along with the segment, leaving a plain unconfigured (legacy-fallback-eligible) boundary with no dangling reference. Both verified directly, no bug found.
  - **Speed-adjusted segments**: confirmed by reading `TransitionResolver.Resolve` that it never reads `VideoSegment.SpeedFactor` at all — it operates purely on output-timeline `Start`/`Duration`/`End`, so a transition activates/clamps identically regardless of either neighbour's speed. Speed only affects how the resolved `OutgoingLocalOffset` maps into SOURCE time, which is `SegmentFrameComposer.MapLocalOffsetToSourceTime`'s job (T2, already tested with 2x/3x speed cases). Added one test pinning the resolver's speed-agnosticism directly so this isn't just an inference from reading code.
  - **REPORTED, NOT FIXED — split duplicates a configured transition onto a brand-new internal boundary it was never configured for**: `SplitSegmentAtTimeOperation` builds both halves via `segment with { Id = ..., Duration = ..., ... }` (a record `with`-expression), which — because it never overrides `InTransition` — copies the ORIGINAL segment's config onto **both** halves. The first half correctly inherits it (that boundary, e.g. `A -> firstHalf`, is exactly the original boundary the config was configured for). But the second half also inherits an identical copy, even though the boundary between the two new halves (`firstHalf -> secondHalf`) is a purely mechanical split point that never had a transition configured on it. `TransitionResolver.Resolve` then genuinely activates the configured effect at that new internal boundary. T2's `CollapseContiguousSourceBoundary` (same-source-contiguous-split fix) only masks this for the SOURCE-TIME-SAMPLING side of the story (so a `CrossFade` at that spot looks close to static) — it does nothing to stop `TransitionRenderer` from actually running a directional/stylised effect (`WipeUp`/`PushLeft`/`Glitch`/`ZoomBlur`/etc.) across what the user experiences as one continuous, unbroken clip they just split. **Verified with a real test that reproduces it** (`Split_SegmentWithTransition_DuplicatesTheConfigOntoTheNewInternalBoundary_KnownBug`) rather than only reasoning about it — the test pins CURRENT (buggy) behaviour with an explanatory comment rather than asserting the fix, per this task's explicit instruction to report cross-cutting inconsistencies rather than silently redesign edit-operation behaviour that was out of this pass's scope. A real fix belongs in `SplitSegmentAtTimeOperation` (e.g. explicitly null out `secondHalf`'s `InTransition` for both the `VideoSegment` and `TextSlideSegment` branches) and should go through the same review process the rest of this feature did, not be slipped into an integration pass.
- **What didn't work / considered and rejected**: considered extracting `CollapseContiguousSourceBoundary` into a brand-new static class in `Musio.Core.Timeline` alongside `TransitionResolver` for symmetry with T1's "pure resolver" framing — rejected because it needs no timeline/model types at all (pure `double`/`string`/`int` arithmetic keyed to source-file semantics), and moving it away from `SegmentFrameComposer` (where its sibling `ClampSourceTime`/`SelectAvailableSourceDuration`/`MapLocalOffsetToSourceTime` already live and where its own extensive XML docs already cross-reference those siblings) would have scattered one cohesive unit of "how do we sample past a cut" logic across two files for no reader benefit.
- **Build & test**: `MSBuild.exe src\Musio.Tests\Musio.Tests.csproj ... /p:Platform=x64` → 0 errors (only the pre-existing `CS0618` warnings from `SlideTransitionsTests.cs`/`TransitionResolverTests.cs`, unrelated). `MSBuild.exe src\Musio.App\Musio.App.csproj ... /p:Platform=ARM64` → 0 errors, `Musio.App -> ...\Musio.App.dll`, confirming the `SegmentFrameComposer`-namespace call site compiles cleanly from `Musio.App`. `dotnet test` (`$env:DOTNET_ROLL_FORWARD="Major"`): **928 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped (930 total)** — baseline 921 + 7 new `TransitionCrossCuttingTests`; filtering to `FullyQualifiedName~TransitionCrossCuttingTests` alone showed **7 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped**, confirming every new test (including the bug-reproduction one) actually executed rather than silently no-op'd.

## T8 addendum: split-boundary transition-inheritance bug — fixed (not just reported)

- **Feature/area**: Follow-up to the T8 entry directly above. The user distinguished this bug from the reorder-vs-boundary-identity question: reorder was genuinely open-ended ("report, don't redesign"), but the split bug had one unambiguous correct behaviour, so it was fixed in this same pass rather than deferred.
- **The fix**: `SplitSegmentAtTimeOperation` (both the `VideoSegment` and `TextSlideSegment` branches) and `SplitAndInsertTextSlideOperation` (its `VideoSegment` branch) now explicitly set `secondHalf`'s `InTransition = null` inside the `with` expression that builds it. `firstHalf` is left untouched in both — it correctly keeps the original boundary's config, since `predecessor -> firstHalf` *is* the original boundary. `secondHalf`'s leading edge (`firstHalf -> secondHalf`, or `_slide -> secondHalf` for the insert-slide variant) is a brand-new boundary the split just created and the user never configured, so it must be unconfigured (`null`), not an inherited copy — and specifically `null`, not `TransitionConfig { Type = None }`, because `null` stays eligible for the legacy slide-adjacent fallback (e.g. if a slide is later inserted next to it) while explicit `None` would permanently suppress that fallback too. This is the same null-vs-`None` distinction the base T8 entry already establishes for save/load.
- **Audit of every `record with { ... }` construction from an existing segment in `EditOperation.cs`**, checking whether each one could leak `InTransition` onto a boundary it doesn't belong to:
  - **Fixed — `SplitSegmentAtTimeOperation`** (`VideoSegment` and `TextSlideSegment` branches): confirmed bug, now fixed as above.
  - **Fixed — `SplitAndInsertTextSlideOperation`** (`VideoSegment` branch): identical shape/bug, now fixed as above. (The inserted `_slide` itself is caller-provided, not built via `with` from the original video, so it was never part of this bug.)
  - **Already correct — `TrimSegmentEdgeOperation`** (`video with {...}`, `slide with {...}`, `_previous with {...}`): does not split a segment into two; one segment keeps its own identity and its own single leading boundary throughout, so inheriting `InTransition` via `with` is exactly right here — nothing to fix.
  - **Not applicable — `SplitOperation`**: operates on the legacy `TimelineClip` model, which has no `InTransition` property at all.
  - **Not applicable — `ApplyClipSpeedOperation`**: operates entirely on `TimelineClip`/`ZoomKeyframe` (legacy model), same reason.
  - **Not applicable — `AppendVideoSegmentOperation`**: appends a caller-provided segment directly; does not clone via `with` at all.
  - **Harmless, not fixed — `UpdateTextOverlayPropertiesOperation`**'s `overlay with {...}`: builds a `TextOverlaySegment`, which lives in `model.TextOverlays`, a list `TransitionResolver.Resolve` never reads (it only reads `model.Segments`). Any `InTransition` value on an overlay segment is inert/dead data regardless of how it got there — worth knowing, not worth "fixing" since there is no observable behaviour to change.
- **Undo verified for both fixed operations**: both `SplitSegmentAtTimeOperation.Undo` and `SplitAndInsertTextSlideOperation.Undo` restore from a full pre-execute snapshot (`_previousSegments = [.. model.Segments]` then `Clear()`/`AddRange()` on undo), so undo was already correct by construction and needed no code change — but this was **not** assumed, it was pinned with two new tests per operation (one where the original segment had a configured transition, one where it was `null`), confirming the exact original segment reference and its exact `InTransition` come back unchanged either way.
- **Regression-test discipline**: before applying the fix, the two new/updated tests (`Split_SegmentWithTransition_OnlyFirstHalfKeepsIt_SecondHalfIsUnconfigured`, `SplitAndInsertTextSlide_SecondHalfIsUnconfigured_FirstHalfKeepsOriginalTransition`) were run against the pre-fix code by temporarily removing just the two `InTransition = null,` lines (not via `git stash` on the whole file, since the entire feature branch is still uncommitted and stashing the file would have reverted T2-T9's other unrelated changes too — a near-miss worth flagging for next time: stash/revert **hunks**, never whole files, on an uncommitted branch). Both failed with the expected `Assert.IsNull failed` message, confirming they are genuine regression tests and not tautologies; the two lines were then restored and the full suite re-run green.
- **Bug-class lesson for future segment-property additions**: any time a new property is added to `TimelineSegment` (or a subtype), audit every `record with { ... }` call site that **splits** one segment into two or otherwise manufactures a "new boundary" from an existing segment's data. A `with` expression copies every property it doesn't explicitly override, which is exactly right when a segment keeps its single identity (e.g. a trim) and exactly wrong when the operation is manufacturing a brand-new boundary that was never configured (e.g. a split). This is easy to miss because it doesn't fail to compile, doesn't crash, and for effects that degrade gracefully when collapsed to a same-source cut (like `CrossFade` via T2's `CollapseContiguousSourceBoundary`) it doesn't even look wrong in casual testing — it only becomes visibly obviously broken for directional/stylised effects (`WipeUp`, `PushLeft`, `Glitch`, ...), which makes it exactly the kind of bug that looks intermittent/hard-to-reproduce from a user bug report. The concrete audit heuristic that worked here: grep for every `\w+ with \{` in the file, then for each hit ask "does this construct a segment that becomes the INCOMING side of a boundary that didn't exist before this operation ran?" — if yes, every newly-introduced-boundary segment needs its inherited boundary-scoped properties explicitly reset, not left to `with`'s copy-everything default.
- **Build & test**: same toolchain as the base T8 entry. `MSBuild.exe src\Musio.Tests\Musio.Tests.csproj ... /p:Platform=x64` → 0 errors. `dotnet test` (`$env:DOTNET_ROLL_FORWARD="Major"`) → **932 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped (934 total)** — 928 (prior T8 baseline) + 4 new tests (1 corrected/renamed split test, 2 new undo round-trip tests, 1 new `SplitAndInsertTextSlideOperation` test). `MSBuild.exe src\Musio.App\Musio.App.csproj ... /p:Platform=ARM64` → 0 errors, app builds clean.

## Post-merge review of PR #73 (segment transitions): VideoEncoder audio-duration probe

- **Feature/area**: `src\Musio.Core\Export\VideoEncoder.cs` — `TryGetMediaDurationAsync` (renamed `TryGetAudioFileDurationAsync`), the probe feeding `ClampToSourceDuration` for `AudioSourceKind.AudioFile` placements.
- **Review claim**: the probe opened standalone audio files with `MediaClip.CreateFromFileAsync` (the VIDEO-clip API), which `may fail for audio-only inputs`; because the probe swallows exceptions and returns `null`, and `ClampToSourceDuration` no-ops on `null`, that would silently disable the new take/fade clamp for every audio-file placement.
- **Verified empirically, NOT assumed — the claimed failure does NOT reproduce**: wrote a throwaway MSTest that generates a 2s 44.1kHz/16-bit mono PCM WAV and transcodes it (`MediaTranscoder` + `MediaEncodingProfile.CreateMp3/CreateM4a/CreateWma`), then probes each with BOTH APIs. Result on current Windows: `[wav] MediaClip=00:00:02 ; Track=00:00:02 || [mp3] MediaClip=00:00:01.9286250 ; Track=00:00:01.9286250 || [m4a] MediaClip=00:00:02.0201133 ; Track=00:00:02.0201133 || [wma] MediaClip=00:00:02.0450000 ; Track=00:00:02.0450000`. `MediaClip.CreateFromFileAsync` opens audio-only files fine and reports durations identical to `BackgroundAudioTrack` to the tick. **So there was no live bug and no behaviour change from the fix.** Worth remembering: `MediaClip` is not as strictly video-only as its docs imply — do not assert `MediaClip` throws on audio in a test, it will not.
- **What worked / kept anyway**: switched the probe to `BackgroundAudioTrack.CreateFromFileAsync(file).OriginalDuration` — deliberately the exact same API `MuxAudioAsync` already uses for `AudioSourceKind.AudioFile`, so the value clamped against is literally the `OriginalDuration` `ApplyPlacement` later trims against (no second decoder's interpretation can diverge). Also normalises a zero/negative duration to `null`. Rationale for keeping a no-op-today change: the failure mode of relying on an undocumented capability here is *silent* (swallowed exception -> unclamped placement -> nothing surfaced anywhere), and calling the audio API the mux already uses costs nothing.
- **Testability**: made the probe `internal` (`Musio.Core.csproj` already has `InternalsVisibleTo(Musio.Tests)`) and added `src\Musio.Tests\AudioFileDurationProbeTests.cs` — generates a real 2s silent WAV and asserts the probe resolves ~2s (`Assert.Inconclusive` if Media Foundation is unavailable, per the house pattern in `BackgroundCompositorPreloadTests`), plus missing-file and undecodable-file -> `null` cases. WinRT `StorageFile`/`BackgroundAudioTrack`/`MediaTranscoder` all work fine unpackaged from the vstest host.
- **What didn't work**: `dotnet build`/`dotnet test` still fail with the pre-existing WindowsAppSDK PriGen `MSB4062`. On this host there is no VS IDE install — MSBuild lives at `C:\Program Files (x86)\Microsoft Visual Studio\2022\BuildTools\MSBuild\Current\Bin\amd64\MSBuild.exe` and vstest at `...\BuildTools\Common7\IDE\CommonExtensions\Microsoft\TestWindow\vstest.console.exe` (`vswhere.exe` is NOT present, so locate them by path, not by `vswhere`).
- **Left alone deliberately**: `ProbeEmbeddedAudioAsync` still uses `MediaClip` — correct there, since it must both read `EmbeddedAudioTracks` and report the host video's duration. Probe failure still leaves the placement in `available` (unclamped) rather than dropping it: a probe failure is not proof the file has no usable audio, and the mux already tolerates a single unreadable track.
- **Verification**: MSBuild x64 clean (Core + Tests); full suite **952 tests, 950 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed**.

## PR #75 review round 2: `BackgroundAudioTrack` disposal in the duration probe

- **Feedback**: `TryGetAudioFileDurationAsync` creates a `BackgroundAudioTrack` solely to read `OriginalDuration` and never closes it; suggested disposing via `IDisposable`/`IClosable` in a `try/finally` in case it pins a decoder or file handle.
- **Verified empirically — NOT actionable as written**: reflection over the CsWinRT projection shows `BackgroundAudioTrack` implements only `ICustomQueryInterface, IWinRTObject, IDynamicInterfaceCastable, IUnmanagedVirtualMethodTableProvider, IEquatable<BackgroundAudioTrack>` — **no `IDisposable`/`IClosable`** (`typeof(IDisposable).IsAssignableFrom(typeof(BackgroundAudioTrack)) == False`). Same for `MediaClip`. A `using` or `.Dispose()` on either does not compile. Additionally, deleting the probed file **immediately while the returned track is still alive succeeds** — no handle is held.
- **Key distinction worth remembering**: `MuxAudioAsync`'s `muxComp.BackgroundAudioTracks.Clear()` in its `finally` is NOT evidence these objects are individually closable — it releases what `MediaComposition` pins once a track is **added to a composition**. A standalone track that was never added to one pins nothing.
- **What worked instead of a no-op `try/finally`**: documented the reflection + delete-while-alive findings on the method, and converted the concern into an observable regression guard — `TryGetAudioFileDurationAsync_DoesNotPinTheProbedFile` probes a generated WAV then asserts the file is still exclusively openable (`FileShare.None`) and deletable. If a future WinRT version does start holding the source open, it fails in that test instead of surfacing as a mysteriously locked export source.
- **Verification**: MSBuild x64 clean; full suite **953 tests, 951 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed**.

## Repo-wide maintainability audit: duplication, large files, abstraction opportunities (analysis only, no code changed)

- **Feature/area**: whole repository — `Musio.App`, `Musio.Core`, `Musio.Tests`, plus repo hygiene. Baseline measured: **208 source files / 61,830 lines** (App 23,627 · Core 24,461 · Tests 13,748), 23 XAML files.
- **Approach that worked**: three parallel `explore` agents scoped to non-overlapping areas (UI god-classes / Core pipeline / App services + tests), combined with a mechanical PowerShell duplicate-block detector (sliding 6-line window over all `.cs`, trimmed, ignoring comment/brace-only windows). The detector found **196 distinct duplicated 6-line blocks** and independently corroborated every agent finding — worth rerunning rather than re-deriving by hand.
- **Biggest context-burden finding (not a code finding)**: `learnings.md` is **493 KB / 1,771 lines / 213 headings**, and `.github/copilot-instructions.md` requires reading it at the start of *every* task. It is larger than any source file in the repo. The settled playbooks occupy only lines 13–156; the remaining ~1,600 lines are per-fix history, much of it explicitly superseded. Recommended split: `learnings/playbooks.md` (read always) + dated archives (read on demand).
- **Largest files**: `EditorPage.xaml.cs` **7,457 lines — 108 fields, 301 methods, zero `#region`**, ~28 distinct responsibilities; `TimelineControl.xaml.cs` 3,654; `EditOperation.cs` 1,732 (**33 command classes in one file**); `FrameCompositor.cs` 1,304; `SegmentFrameComposer.cs` 1,104. 16 files exceed 600 lines.
- **Duplication hot spots (ranked)**: (1) **no interop layer** — 148 `[DllImport]` across 19 files, 44 externs declared in >1 file, `RECT` declared 6×, `POINT` 6×, `MONITORINFOEX` 3×; (2) `RegionSelectorOverlay` vs `WindowSelectorOverlay` — GDI screenshot ~180 dup lines, Escape hook ~50, host-window scaffolding ~70 (borderless-presenter setup duplicated 4× counting `MiniWindow`/`RecordingOverlayWindow`); (3) `TimelineControl` per-track gesture stacks (~600, with `var model = Model; if (model is null) return;` repeated **78×**); (4) `EditOperation` snapshot/restore (3× full-timeline + 4× segment-list) and Camera-track ops mirroring TextOverlay-track ops (~85); (5) XAML — `CropCellRadioStyle`/`OverlayAnchorCellStyle` are letter-for-letter identical (the file even *says* it was copied), color-picker row repeated 9×; (6) tests — **zero shared infrastructure**, `ModelWith(...)` copy-pasted into 8 files in two already-diverged variants, temp-dir setup/teardown in 14 files, `MakeBitmap` an exact duplicate across 2 files.
- **Confirmed NOT a problem (do not re-investigate)**: the preview and export paths do **not** fork rendering logic — `SegmentFrameComposer` delegates per-frame work to `FrameCompositor` (`SegmentFrameComposer.cs:658`), which is the single source of truth. `TransitionRenderer`, `TransitionResolver`, `AnimatedTextEngine`, `CubicBezierEasing` and `IFrameSource` are all correctly shared. Residual Core duplication is peripheral geometry/boilerplate, not pipeline logic.
- **Correctness risks surfaced while counting lines** (worth fixing regardless of any refactor): `TransitionRenderer` acquires `CanvasDevice.GetSharedDevice()` but, unlike `FrameCompositor`/`VideoEncoder`, wires **no `DeviceLost` guard**; the OOM-guarded `FrameCompositor.CreateRenderTarget` (L1009) is used by none of the other 22 `new CanvasRenderTarget` sites; the aspect-ratio switch exists twice (`FrameCompositor.cs:1132` vs `AspectRatioHelper.cs:16`) and must be hand-synced; `AspectRatioHelper.FitWithinBounds` is `private`, so 3 other call sites reimplement letterbox math; there is no shared `TimeToFrame` helper, so Round-vs-Floor policy varies across 8+ inlined copies; and nothing enforces the `RecalculateSegmentPositions()` post-condition across the 16 segment-mutating operations.
- **Recommended sequencing** (risk-ordered, so it can be resumed): (1) split `learnings.md`, split `EditOperation.cs` into 6 files, `#region`/partial-split `EditorPage.xaml.cs` — pure organization, zero behavior risk; (2) `Musio.Core\Interop\`, test builders + `TempDirectoryFixture`, `Debouncer`/`SuppressScope`, shared XAML styles; (3) `GrowOnlyBuffer`/`GpuContext`/`TimelineSnapshot`/`FrameTimeConverter`; (4) `OverlayWindowBase`, `TrackGestureHandler<T>`; (5) **last** — `IProjectService` + DI, then the `EditorPreviewController`/`StyleController`/`TextEditController` extractions, which are gated on the preview generation-counter invariants (`_previewInitGeneration`, `_primaryPreviewStateGeneration`, `_segmentPreviewGeneration`) and are the most fragile code in the repo.
- **What didn't work / avoid**: reading `EditorPage.xaml.cs` or `learnings.md` linearly — both blow past the 20 KB read limit; use `view` with `view_range` windows or grep for method signatures instead. Also note `ProjectService.Instance` is referenced at ~68 sites inside `EditorPage` alone: **no UI extraction is unit-testable until an `IProjectService` seam exists**, so DI is a prerequisite for wave 5, not an optional cleanup.

## Meta — Split `learnings.md` (493 KB / 2,283 lines) into playbooks + two archives, rewrote `.github/copilot-instructions.md`

- **Feature/area**: `learnings.md` structure, per the maintainability-audit recommendation above. Documentation only — no code touched.
- **Approach that worked**: sliced the file by exact line number (verified with `grep -n '^## '` for headings and `Get-Content` array indexing) rather than editing in place, since the file is too large to safely round-trip through a single read/edit. Moved lines 13–156 (`# Settled Playbooks` + all 7 `## Playbook:` subsections) verbatim into `learnings/playbooks.md`. Split the remaining historical entries (line 157 → end) at a blank-line/heading boundary nearest the midpoint (line 1218/1219, between the "Editor regressions exposed by MP4-backed editing" and prior entries) into `learnings/archive-2026-H1.md` (1,062 body lines) and `learnings/archive-2026-H2.md` (1,065 body lines, this file — new entries append here). Replaced the root `learnings.md` with a ~30-line index pointing at the three new files. Rewrote `.github/copilot-instructions.md` to read `learnings/playbooks.md` always, consult an archive only when working in one of six named high-churn areas, and append new entries to `learnings/archive-2026-H2.md`.
- **Verification (byte-for-byte, not just line-count)**: re-sliced `git show HEAD:learnings.md` with the same indices and ran `Compare-Object` against the body of each new file (stripping only the newly-added header lines) — **0 diffs** on all three splits. Arithmetic: header (12) + playbooks body (144) + archive-H1 body (1,062) + archive-H2 body (1,065) = 2,283, matching the original line count exactly. No entry was reworded, reordered, or dropped.
- **What didn't work / avoid**: double-quoted PowerShell heredoc-style arrays containing backtick-quoted identifiers (e.g. `` `.musio` ``) triggered a parser error (backtick is an escape character in double-quoted strings) — switched to single-quoted string arrays for the injected headers instead.

## W1-2 — Split `EditOperation.cs` (2,022 lines, 1 interface + 33 command classes) into `Timeline/Operations/` (7 files)

- **Feature/area**: `src/Musio.Core/Timeline/EditOperation.cs` -> deleted; replaced by `src/Musio.Core/Timeline/Operations/{IEditOperation,ClipOperations,ZoomOperations,SegmentOperations,CameraOperations,TextOverlayOperations,TransitionOperations}.cs`. Pure file-level move, zero behavior change (Wave 1, W1-2). Namespace kept as `Musio.Core.Timeline` in every new file so no other file in the repo needed a `using` change.
- **Approach that worked**: rather than manually copying 34 class bodies through view/edit round trips (error-prone at this size), wrote a small Node.js script (no `python`/`py` executable available on this host, `node` was) that sliced the original file by the exact 1-based `grep -n '^public (interface|class) '` start lines from the work-item table, assigned each class block to its target file per the table, and wrote the 7 new files with a `namespace Musio.Core.Timeline;` + `using Musio.Core.Processing;` header. Followed with a second verification script that stripped headers back out of the 7 new files, reassembled the 34 class blocks in the **original** source order, and diff-compared the result character-for-character against the original file's body (everything after line 4) — got an exact match before deleting the original. This two-script slice-then-reassemble-and-diff pattern is the reliable way to do large pure-move file splits: it proves byte-fidelity mechanically instead of trusting a long sequence of manual edits.
- **`using Musio.Core.Processing;` pruning**: work item said not to blanket-add the using, only where a symbol needs it. Grepped the new files for any Processing-namespace type (`WebcamOverlayStyle`, `MotionBlurSettings`, `CameraDriftSettings`, `CursorStyle`, etc. — enumerated from `grep -n '^public (class|record|struct|enum|interface) '` across `Processing/*.cs`) and found only `CameraOperations.cs` references one (`WebcamOverlayStyle`, used by `AddCameraSegmentOperation`/`UpdateCameraSegmentPropertiesOperation`). Removed the using from the other 6 files; rebuild showed 0 errors and 0 unused-using warnings, confirming the trim was correct.
- **Arithmetic proving the move is line-count-clean**: original 2,022 lines. New files total 2,034 (`IEditOperation.cs` 9, `ClipOperations.cs` 557, `ZoomOperations.cs` 268, `SegmentOperations.cs` 580, `CameraOperations.cs` 223, `TextOverlayOperations.cs` 271, `TransitionOperations.cs` 126). Delta = +12, entirely explained by new per-file headers: `CameraOperations.cs` keeps a 4-line header (namespace + blank + using + blank), the other 6 files get a 2-line header (namespace + blank only) = 4 + 6×2 = 16 header lines total, vs. the original's single 4-line header → 16 − 4 = 12, matching exactly.
- **`Musio.Core.csproj` uses default globbing** (`<Project Sdk="Microsoft.NET.Sdk">` with no explicit `<Compile Include>`/`<Compile Remove>` items) — confirmed by reading the file; no csproj edit was needed for the new `Operations/` subfolder to be picked up.
- **Build & test**: x64 `Musio.Tests.csproj` → 0 errors (only pre-existing unrelated `CS0618` warnings in `SlideTransitionsTests.cs`/`TransitionResolverTests.cs`, none from the moved files). ARM64 `Musio.App.csproj` → 0 errors (only pre-existing `MVVMTK0045` warnings, unrelated). Full suite twice (before and after the using-pruning pass): **951 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped (953 total)** both times — exact match to the orchestrator's stated baseline, no test touched.
- **What didn't work / avoid**: `python`/`python -c` is not on PATH on this host (only a Microsoft Store app-execution-alias stub that errors); use `node` for one-off scripting instead. Also note `learnings.md` on disk may be mid-split by a concurrent documentation-only agent (it was, during this task) — check for a `learnings/` directory with `playbooks.md`/`archive-2026-H*.md` before appending, and follow whatever the current root `learnings.md` says (it now points here, to `archive-2026-H2.md`).

## W1-3 — Split `EditorPage.xaml.cs` (8,604 lines, 462 members, 5 nested types) into 5 partials + slimmed conductor

- **Feature/area**: `src/Musio.App/Pages/EditorPage.xaml.cs` (`public sealed partial class EditorPage : Page`) split into `EditorPage.{Preview,Timeline,Style,TextEditing,Shortcuts}.cs` plus a slimmed `EditorPage.xaml.cs` conductor. Pure declaration move (Wave 1, W1-3): zero characters changed inside any method body.
- **Approach that worked (scaled up the W1-2 slice-then-reassemble-and-diff pattern to a much larger, brace-heavy file)**: wrote a Node.js structural parser that walks the file tracking brace depth per line and, at depth transitions relative to the class body (depth 1), enumerates every member (field/method/property/nested type) as a `[sigStart, bodyClose]` line range. Two real parsing gotchas found and fixed: (1) a trailing `// comment` after a field's `;` broke "line ends with `;`" detection — strip line comments before checking; (2) exactly one method in the whole file (`InitializeWebcamOverlay`, ~line 6316) has its opening `{` and first statement on the same physical line, which merged it with the next method under a naive "line is exactly `{`" heuristic — fixed by checking "line *starts with* `{`" and computing that line's net brace delta explicitly. Verified this was the *only* such same-line-brace case at class-body depth (grepped for both variants; the other 2 hits were harmless object-initializer literals nested deep inside method bodies).
- **Coverage is the load-bearing correctness proof, not manual review**: after enumerating members, concatenated all of them in original order and diff-compared the result character-for-character against the original file's class body (`lines[27..8601]`, 0-based) — exact match (382,927 chars both sides) before writing any output file. This is what makes a mechanical, non-manual split trustworthy at this size; manual cut/paste of ~460 members would not have been checkable this way.
- **Destination assignment**: built a 66-entry, gap-verified line-range table giving each member a *positional* destination (based on where the prompt's per-file member lists placed its neighbours), then for every true field (see discriminator below) scanned the whole file for word-boundary usages outside its own declaration and mapped each usage line back to a positional destination; if a field's usages spanned more than one destination file, it was forced to the **Conductor** file regardless of its position (the prompt's own guidance: fields used from ≥2 partials must stay shared). ~50 of ~149 fields ended up overridden to Conductor this way, including `_previewRenderer`, `PrimaryVideoPath`, `_selectedPrimarySegmentId`, `_suppressWebcamEvents` (backs two unrelated "webcam" concerns — camera-fullscreen selection in Timeline *and* webcam-overlay dragging in Style), `_suppressZoomPropertyUpdate`, and `_selectedTextSlideId`.
- **Real-field vs. expression-bodied-method/property discriminator**: any member ending in a bare `;` was initially mis-tagged `kind='field'`; the correct test is presence of `=>` (expression body, e.g. `NormalizeHex`, `SaveProject_Click`) or the `extern` keyword (`GetSystemMetrics` P/Invoke) in the declaration — **not** presence of `(`, since legitimate field initializers like `_segmentPreviews = new();` or `AudioDriftTolerance = TimeSpan.FromMilliseconds(120);` contain `(` without being methods.
- **Judgment calls made** (both flagged by the prompt, plus undocumented straddling cases found during review): transition event wiring (`OnTransitionSelected`/`OnTransitionRemoveRequested`) → Timeline; `GetTransitionBoundarySegments`/`SyncTransitionUI` and all `Transition*` panel handlers → TextEditing (6 of 7 call sites there); `RefreshOverlayEditPreviewForCurrentPlayhead` → Timeline (its one call site); `RebuildPreviewRendererAsync`/`CoreAsync` → Style (grouped with the other style-driven renderer-rebuild methods, despite the "preview" name); `GetSystemMetrics`/`GetDpiScale` → Conductor (confirmed dead code via grep — 1 reference each, the declaration itself — left in place per behavior-preservation, not "fixed").
- **No compile-time risk from *which* file a member lands in**: partial classes share one scope, so a method/field is visible from every partial file regardless of physical location — the prompt's claim that a wrong field guess is "a compile error" is not literally true. The only real compile risks are duplicate declarations (content moved without removing the original), missing per-file `using` directives, or accidentally altering text during the copy. This de-risks placement decisions considerably: correctness is about not losing/duplicating/altering text, not about optimal per-file placement.
- **File headers**: started every new partial file with the *full* original 22-line `using` block (safe over-inclusion; unused-using is a warning here, not an error — repo has no `TreatWarningsAsErrors`) rather than hand-trimming per file, to avoid an extra build-fix-rebuild cycle per file; this built clean on the first try.
- **Arithmetic**: 462 total members = 39 Preview + 59 Timeline + 93 Style + 141 TextEditing + 26 Shortcuts + 104 Conductor. Final line counts: `EditorPage.xaml.cs` (conductor) 1128, `.Preview.cs` 1696, `.Timeline.cs` 1279, `.Style.cs` 1559, `.TextEditing.cs` 2547, `.Shortcuts.cs` 534 (total 8743; the +139 over the original 8604 is entirely the 5 new files' duplicated `using`/`namespace`/class-decl headers plus closing brace, not content growth). All 5 pre-existing nested types (`SegmentPreview`, `PrimaryStyleRenderer` → Preview; `ITextEditTarget`, `SlideTextEditTarget`, `OverlayTextEditTarget` → TextEditing) verified present exactly once via grep.
- **Build & test**: ARM64 `Musio.App.csproj` → 0 errors on the **first** build attempt (only pre-existing `MVVMTK0045` warnings in unrelated ViewModel files). Full suite: **951 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped (953 total)** — exact match to the stated baseline. Note `Musio.Tests.csproj` does **not** compile `Musio.App` (only `Musio.Core`), so its build is not a signal for this file at all — ARM64 `Musio.App` build is the only real compile check for `EditorPage.xaml.cs`.
- **What didn't work / avoid**: building incrementally file-by-file (as the work item suggests for manual moves) adds little value once correctness is proven mechanically via the coverage-diff check before any file is written — a single ARM64 build after generating all 6 files at once was sufficient here and succeeded on the first try, because the risk that remained (missing usings, wrong field placement) is compiler-caught regardless of build order. Reserve strict one-file-at-a-time builds for genuinely manual/hand-edited splits where a bad edit could silently alter behavior instead of just failing to compile.

## W2-1 — Consolidate duplicated P/Invoke declarations into `Musio.Core/Interop/`

- **Feature/area**: 148 `[DllImport]` P/Invoke declarations scattered across 19 files, consolidated into 5 shared files under `src/Musio.Core/Interop/` (`NativeStructs.cs`, `MonitorInterop.cs`, `VirtualDesktopInfo.cs`, `HookInterop.cs`, `NativeMethods.cs`). Followed the work item's 5-step order (structs → VirtualDesktopInfo → hooks → monitor → remaining shared GDI/window functions), building after each step.
- **What worked**:
  - **Treat `IntPtr`/`nint` and parameter-name differences as cosmetic, not disagreements.** `nint` is a true C#/.NET alias for `System.IntPtr` on this TFM (`net9.0-windows10.0.26100.0`); P/Invoke binds positionally so parameter names never matter. Every "duplicate" that differed only in these two ways was safe to fold into one shared declaration.
  - **A regex-based extern scanner (Node.js, since `python`/`py` triggers a Windows Store stub on this host) that matched `[DllImport(...)]`/`[LibraryImport(...)]` followed by the `extern` line is enough to find candidates, but is blind to fully-qualified attribute syntax** (`[System.Runtime.InteropServices.DllImport(...)]`). Three files (`RecordingViewModel.cs`, `EditorPage.xaml.cs`, `FrameCompositor.cs`) and part of `MainWindow.xaml.cs` used this syntax and hid real duplicates from the scan — found only by grepping for `DllImport` more broadly. Treated as a separate out-of-scope follow-up rather than silently pulled into the same consolidation pass, since the work item's stated scope was the attribute-visible set.
  - **The "STOP and report, don't pick a winner" rule caught real, intentional variation**: `SetWindowsHookEx`/`UnhookWindowsHookEx`/`GetModuleHandle` differ in `SetLastError` between the App layer (fire-and-forget, no error checking) and Core layer (checked via `Marshal.GetLastWin32Error()` after a null/zero return) — folding them would have silently dropped error-checking capability in Core call sites even though it compiles and passes tests. Also genuinely different: `DwmGetWindowAttribute` (returns `int`) vs `DwmGetWindowAttributeRect` (same native function, `EntryPoint` override, returns a `RECT` out-param) are two distinct marshallings of one native entry point kept as separate C# signatures on purpose; `GetMonitorInfo` has an extra `MONITORINFO`-only overload in `ShellCoordinator.cs` alongside the shared `MONITORINFOEX` overload; `GetWindowLong` differs by an `EntryPoint = "GetWindowLongPtrW"` override in one file only; `GetCursorPos`/`SetLayeredWindowAttributes`/`SetWindowLongPtr`/`DefWindowProcW` differ in `[MarshalAs]`/`SetLastError`/parameter type attributes. All 11 groups (28 declarations) were left exactly as-is, each with a documented reason.
  - **Name-collision gotcha**: `SelectionHighlight.cs` has a public *instance* method `ShowWindow(IntPtr hwnd)` (1-arg, business logic) that textually collides with the native 2-arg `ShowWindow(hwnd, nCmdShow)` P/Invoke of the same name. A blanket regex bulk-replace of call sites would have wrongly prefixed the instance-method's own declaration/self-calls. Always grep for a local method/property of the same name as a native function before bulk-replacing call sites in a file.
