  - **Re-running the duplicate scanner after believing a step was "done" caught a real miss**: `SelectionHighlight.cs` still had its own private `GetDpiForMonitor`/`MDT_EFFECTIVE_DPI` extern+const, identical to the ones already centralized in `MonitorInterop.cs` during Step 1 — it was overlooked because Step 1's file-by-file sweep didn't re-grep every consumer for every consolidated symbol. **Always do one final repo-wide duplicate rescan after "finishing" a consolidation wave, even when each individual file was carefully checked**, rather than trusting the step-by-step tracking alone.
  - **Result**: 148 → 98 extern declarations (43 → 11 duplicate-name groups, all 11 the intentionally-preserved disagreements above); net 463 lines removed from 15 consumer files, 5 new shared files added (~130 lines total, all documentation + declarations, no logic).
- **What didn't work / avoid**:
  - Trusting a mid-conversation summary's "already handled" list without spot-checking after context compaction — one file (`SelectionHighlight.cs`'s `GetDpiForMonitor`) had been missed despite being in scope for Step 1, and was only caught by the final rescan, not by re-reading prior notes.
  - Bulk PowerShell regex replace on call sites is safe and fast *if and only if* a negative lookbehind guards against double-prefixing on re-runs (`(?<!NativeMethods\.)\bFoo\(`) and a prior grep has ruled out a same-named local method/property in that file.
- **Build & test**: x64 `Musio.Tests.csproj` clean rebuild → 0 errors (only pre-existing `CS0618` warnings). ARM64 `Musio.App.csproj` clean rebuild → 0 errors (only pre-existing `MVVMTK0045` warnings). Full suite: **951 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped (953 total)** — exact match to the orchestrator's stated baseline, both before and after the final `GetDpiForMonitor` fix.

## W2-3/4/5 — Debouncer/SuppressScope helpers, XAML style consolidation, DialogHelper/PickerHelper routing (`Musio.App`)

- **Feature/area**: Three ordered sub-items in `src/Musio.App`, run in the same working tree after the W1-3 `EditorPage.xaml.cs` split (so every member had to be located by name via `grep -n`, not stale line numbers). W2-3 added `Helpers/Debouncer.cs` (wraps `DispatcherTimer`, trailing-edge `Schedule()`/`Stop()`) and `Helpers/SuppressScope.cs` (`ref struct` with a `ref bool` field), converting 4 debounce timers and 8 suppress-flag sites across `EditorPage.xaml.cs`/`.Style.cs`/`.TextEditing.cs`. W2-4 merged two byte-identical `ControlTemplate`s (`CropCellRadioStyle`/`OverlayAnchorCellStyle` → `AnchorGridCellStyle`) and extracted two `CornerRadius` design tokens (`SmallCornerRadius`=4, `SectionCardCornerRadius`=8) into `Themes/PropertyPaneStyles.xaml`, applied only to confirmed-matching sites (19 of ~33 total `CornerRadius="4"/"8"` occurrences repo-wide). W2-5 added `Helpers/PickerHelper.cs` (wraps `FileOpenPicker` construction + hwnd-init) and `DialogHelper.BuildProgressDialog`, converting all 4 `FileOpenPicker` sites (wallpaper in `.Style.cs`, import-video in `.Shortcuts.cs`, slide-image in `.TextEditing.cs`, open-project in `OpenProjectsPage.xaml.cs`) and 2 plain-OK `ContentDialog` sites (`ShowProjectDialogAsync` in the conductor, the error dialog in `OpenProjectsPage.OpenAsync`) to the helpers.
- **What worked**:
  - **`ref struct` + `ref` field for an exception-safe suppress guard**: `net9.0`'s C# 13 lets a `ref struct` hold a `ref bool _flag` and mutate the caller's field directly; a duck-typed `public void Dispose()` (no `IDisposable` needed) makes `using var _ = SuppressScope.Enter(ref flag);` behave exactly like the `try/finally { flag = false; }` it replaces, including firing on exception.
  - **Matching original statement *timing* relative to the old `finally`, not just wrapping the whole method**: at sites where a statement (e.g. `ScheduleStyleUpdate();`) ran *after* the original `finally` had already reset the flag, a whole-method `using var _ = SuppressScope.Enter(...)` would wrongly extend the "suppressed" window to cover that trailing statement (because `using var` disposes at the end of its enclosing block, not at a specific line). Fixed by using an explicit `using (SuppressScope.Enter(ref flag)) { /* only the original try-body */ }` block so `Dispose()` fires at the exact original `finally` position, leaving the trailing statement textually and temporally outside it.
  - **Conservative behavior-preservation over "consistency"**: of suppress-flag toggle sites found by name-based grep across the split files, only sites with an actual `try/finally` guard were converted; sites that are plain `flag = true; ...; flag = false;` with no exception safety at all were left untouched, since wrapping those in `SuppressScope`/`using` would *add* a guarantee that doesn't exist today — a real behavior change under the task's own "match exactly, don't improve" rule.
  - **Correction (review catch)**: the first pass missed 3 real try/finally sites in `SettingsPage.xaml.cs` (`_suppressStartupModeEvents`, `_suppressExportDefaultEvents`, `_suppressCaptureQualityEvents` in `SettingsPage_Loaded`) because the initial sweep was scoped to `EditorPage.*` files only — the task's "14 suppress sites" framing was itself `EditorPage`-specific, so a partial-class-split file wasn't automatically in scope even though its sites matched the pattern exactly. **Lesson: when a task frames scope around one file/class that has since been split, re-verify scope boundaries app-wide by grepping the *pattern* (e.g. `_suppress\w+\s*=\s*true`) across the whole project, not just the files the task happened to name** — a name-based file scope from a stale prompt can silently exclude a sibling file with the identical pattern. After the correction, final tally across `src/Musio.App/**`: **11 sites converted** (8 in `EditorPage.*` + 3 in `SettingsPage.xaml.cs`) vs **~15 deliberately left alone** (no try/finally guard: `EditorPage.Timeline.cs` `_suppressSpeedApply` ×2, `_suppressWebcamEvents` ×1, `_suppressZoomPropertyUpdate` ×1 [a second call site sharing a field whose *other* site was converted]; `EditorPage.TextEditing.cs` `_suppressOverlayEvents` ×1, `_suppressTransitionEvents` ×1, `_suppressSlideEvents` ×2; `EditorPage.Style.cs` `_suppressStyleEvents` (`ApplyBackgroundStyle`) ×1, `_suppressWebcamEvents` (`SyncWebcamOverlayUI`) ×1; `SettingsPage.xaml.cs` `_suppressWebcamEvents` ×1 [try wraps async device enumeration, not the flag — genuinely a different shape]; `RecordToolbarControl.xaml.cs` `_suppressPickerLaunch` ×1 [flag is set in one method and consumed/reset in a *different* event handler, not via `finally` — a cross-method hand-off pattern, not the target shape at all]). A full re-sweep via `grep -i 'suppress\w*\s*=\s*true'` across `src/Musio.App/**` after the correction found no further misses.
  - **Diff-before-merge for "identical" XAML**: normalized just the `x:Key` name and ran `Compare-Object` between the two `ControlTemplate` blocks before merging them — confirmed byte-identical (63 lines each) *before* deleting either copy, per the task's explicit instruction to skip the merge if they'd drifted.
  - **Numeric literal ≠ design token**: found 24 `CornerRadius="4"` sites app-wide but only extracted 12 of them (property-pane color-swatches/anchor-grid containers/tab buttons) into `SmallCornerRadius`; explicitly left 9 `CornerRadius="4"` uses in `EditorPage.xaml` alone (8 are 8×8 "handle" squares using radius=half-of-size to render as circles — a different design intent that happens to share the number 4, not the same token) and other ambiguous-context files (`WindowSelectorOverlay.xaml`, `NavigationRailStyles.xaml`, etc.) untouched, to avoid silently coupling unrelated visual intents to one shared resource.
  - **`PickerHelper.PickSingleFileAsync(location, filters, viewMode: null)` with an optional `PickerViewMode?`** preserved a real per-site difference: 3 of the 4 `FileOpenPicker` call sites set `ViewMode = Thumbnail`, but `OpenProjectsPage.Open_Click` never did — passing `viewMode: null` (the default) and only assigning `picker.ViewMode` when non-null kept that difference intact through the shared helper instead of normalizing it away.
  - **Letting the picker/dialog helpers propagate exceptions instead of swallowing them**: `PickerHelper.PickSingleFileAsync` and `DialogHelper.BuildProgressDialog` do no try/catch of their own, because each of the 4-6 call sites has its own different outer handling (log-and-continue via `DiagLog.Write`, silent no-op `catch { }`, or none) that had to be preserved verbatim around the new helper call.
  - **A stale claim in the work item caught and reported, not silently "fixed"**: the work item described 5 `FileOpenPicker` sites including `ExportViewModel.cs`, but that file actually uses `FileSavePicker` (different class: `FileTypeChoices` dict instead of `FileTypeFilter` list, `SuggestedFileName`, `PickSaveFileAsync`, hwnd sourced from a different property). Left it untouched since there was no duplication among `FileSavePicker` sites to justify a shared helper, and reported the discrepancy.
  - **Only rebuild-from-clean (delete `bin`/`obj`) after XAML changes** (W2-4); the later W2-5 `.cs`-only changes built correctly with a normal incremental ARM64 build, no wipe needed — confirms the stale-`.g.cs` trap is specifically a XAML-codegen risk, not a general one.
- **What didn't work / avoid**: none of the deviations above were "fixed" by forcing them into the new helpers — per the task's explicit instruction, sites that didn't match the standard shape (~10 try-less suppress flags, `ExportViewModel`'s `FileSavePicker`, non-property-pane `CornerRadius` literals) were left alone and reported rather than silently normalized, since doing so would have been an unrequested behavior change riding along with a pure-refactor task.
- **Build**: ARM64 `Musio.App.csproj` → 0 errors after each of the 3 sub-items (only pre-existing `MVVMTK0045` warnings), including one full clean `bin`/`obj` rebuild after W2-4's XAML edits. Per orchestration split (concurrent agent owned `Musio.Tests`/x64), this agent did not build or run `Musio.Tests` — ARM64 `Musio.App` build was the sole correctness signal for this task, which is sound since none of the touched files are compiled into `Musio.Tests`.

## W3-1 — GPU resource layer: `Win2DUtils`/`GrowOnlyBuffer`/`DeviceLostGuard` + FIX 1 (OOM/COMException-guarded `CanvasRenderTarget` allocation) + FIX 2 (`TransitionRenderer` DeviceLost guard)

- **Feature/area**: Added 3 new shared files under `src/Musio.Core/Processing/`: `GpuContext.cs` (`GpuContext.GetSharedDevice()` pass-through + shared `RecoverableDeviceLostException` + `DeviceLostGuard`), `Win2DUtils.cs` (`Win2DUtils.CreateRenderTarget(device, width, height, dpi, purpose)`, wraps `OutOfMemoryException`/`COMException` into a `DiagLog`-logged `InvalidOperationException`), `GrowOnlyBuffer.cs` (`Ensure` = exact-size-match recreate-and-throw, `TryEnsureAtLeast` = grow-only-if-smaller catch-and-return-false, `Clear` = reusable dispose, `Dispose` = permanent teardown). Routed **all ~23** production `new CanvasRenderTarget(...)` call sites across `Musio.Core` (13 files) and `Musio.App` (`EditorPage.Preview.cs` ×4, `EditorPage.Timeline.cs` ×1) through `Win2DUtils.CreateRenderTarget` (**FIX 1**, deliberate: OOM/COMException at these sites now throws the wrapped/logged `InvalidOperationException` instead of the raw native exception). Converted the 4 named grow-only scratch-buffer duplicates (`FrameCompositor`'s 2 methods, `TextOverlayRenderer.EnsureBlurScratch`, `CursorRenderer`'s shutter blur scratch) to `GrowOnlyBuffer`. Gave `TransitionRenderer` a `DeviceLostGuard` (**FIX 2**, deliberate: it previously had none at all, unlike `FrameCompositor`/`VideoEncoder`). Also fully migrated `VideoEncoder`'s and `FrameCompositor`'s hand-rolled DeviceLost subscribe/flag/unsubscribe to `DeviceLostGuard`, and removed the verbatim-duplicated `RecoverableDeviceLostException` class from both.
- **What worked**:
  - **Tracing the *failure-path* side effect, not just the success-path order, to prove a reordering is safe.** The work item claimed the 4 scratch-buffer sites universally "allocate new before disposing old," but `FrameCompositor`'s two methods and `CursorRenderer` actually disposed old-first. Standardizing all 4 on allocate-then-dispose (the safer order) was only provably behavior-neutral because `FrameCompositor.CreateRenderTarget`'s catch clause already called `ReleaseCachedRenderTargets()` (disposing *both* cached buffers unconditionally) on any allocation failure — so the observable end-state on failure (both fields null) is identical regardless of per-call ordering. Without that trace, standardizing the order would have been an unverified, unmarked behavior change.
  - **Two distinct growth policies must stay two distinct methods, not be unified.** `FrameCompositor`/`TextOverlayRenderer` use exact-size-match-and-recreate (`!=`), throw on failure; `CursorRenderer` uses grow-only-if-smaller (`<`, quantized dims), catch-and-return-`false` on failure (graceful degradation). `GrowOnlyBuffer.Ensure` vs `TryEnsureAtLeast` preserve both without merging them — merging would have silently added exception-throwing to a site that currently degrades gracefully, or vice versa.
  - **`Clear()` (reusable, dispose-and-null) vs `Dispose()` (permanent, sets a disposed flag) must be separate methods** on `GrowOnlyBuffer`, because `FrameCompositor.ReleaseCachedRenderTargets()` is called multiple times over an instance's lifetime (on every allocation failure, not just final teardown) — a holder that could only be disposed once would break that call.
  - **`TryEnsureAtLeast` must catch `InvalidOperationException`, not `OutOfMemoryException`/`COMException` directly**, because `Win2DUtils.CreateRenderTarget` already wraps those before they can propagate. This is a necessary, correct consequence of routing through the shared wrapper — verified it still preserves `CursorRenderer`'s original non-throwing contract (any other exception type, e.g. `ArgumentException`, still passes through unmodified since the wrapper only touches OOM/COM).
  - **Sites with a pre-existing broad `catch (Exception ex)` (log-and-continue, no rethrow)** — `VideoThumbnailExtractor`'s two allocations, both `Mp4FrameSource` sites — are unaffected in control flow by FIX 1, since `InvalidOperationException` is still an `Exception` and still gets caught; only the logged exception type/message changes. Confirmed this explicitly before routing them, to avoid a silent behavior change disguised as "just routing through the wrapper."
  - **DPI/dimension variance across the 23 sites was narrow enough to solve with one signature**: only `VideoThumbnailExtractor.FlipVertically` passes `source.Dpi` (not `96`), and only `WebcamCompositor`'s shadow target uses `float` width/height (padding math) instead of `int`. `Win2DUtils.CreateRenderTarget(CanvasDevice device, float width, float height, float dpi, string purpose)` accommodates both via implicit numeric conversion — no overloads needed.
  - **Reordering constructor statements fixed a real CS8602 nullable warning**: constructing a `DeviceLostGuard` with a lambda that referenced `_bgCompositor` (`onLost: () => _bgCompositor.InvalidateImageCache()`) *before* `_bgCompositor` was assigned triggered a spurious "possibly null reference" warning in `FrameCompositor`'s constructor, even though the lambda only runs after full construction. Fixed by simply assigning `_bgCompositor` first.
  - **`RecoverableDeviceLostException` was duplicated verbatim** in `FrameCompositor.cs` and `VideoEncoder.cs` — confirmed via repo-wide grep that nothing catches it by qualified type name, so consolidating into one shared type in `GpuContext.cs` was safe.
  - **Build & test**: `Musio.Tests` x64 clean rebuild → 0 errors (only pre-existing `CS0618` obsolete warnings in `SlideTransitionsTests.cs`/`TransitionResolverTests.cs`). `Musio.App` ARM64 clean rebuild → 0 errors (only pre-existing `MVVMTK0045` warnings). Full suite: **951 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped (953 total)** — exact match to baseline, both before and after the full FIX 1 site sweep. `TransitionRendererTests`/`TransitionRendererHardwareTests`/`BackgroundCompositorPreloadTests`' `Assert.Inconclusive("Win2D CanvasDevice unavailable")` fallbacks did **not** trigger on this host — `new CanvasDevice(forceSoftwareRenderer: true)` succeeded, so these actually exercised the real Win2D/GPU path rather than being skipped. `Mp4FrameSourceSequentialTests`' 2 `Skipped` are pre-existing `[Ignore("Known defect...")]` attributes, unrelated to GPU availability. `PreviewFrameReaderTests`/`Direct3DDeviceHelperTests` have no Inconclusive fallback (call `CanvasDevice.GetSharedDevice()`/pure ABI marshaling directly) and passed.
- **What didn't work / avoid**:
  - Considered unifying `AnimatedTextEngine.EnsureBlurScratch` (a near-verbatim, undocumented 5th duplicate of the exact scratch-buffer idiom, whose doc-comment literally references `TextOverlayRenderer.EnsureBlurScratch`) and `TextSlideRenderer`'s `_gradientCache` (a 6th near-duplicate, allocate-then-swap already, but keyed on a `(size, colors, angle)` tuple rather than just size) into `GrowOnlyBuffer` — decided **not** to, since the work item's Task 1 named only 4 sites and opportunistic widening risks an unmarked behavior change if the near-duplicate's contract differs subtly. Routed their lone `CanvasRenderTarget` calls through `Win2DUtils` anyway (in scope per FIX 1's "route all sites" mandate) but left their buffer-growth/caching logic untouched.
  - `VideoWriter.cs`'s render-target *pool* (a stack of reusable targets keyed on `(width, height)`, cleared on mismatch) is yet another distinct pattern (pooling multiple targets, not caching one) — routed its single allocation site through `Win2DUtils` but did not attempt to fold it into `GrowOnlyBuffer`, which has no pooling concept.
  - `PreviewRenderer.cs`/`VideoThumbnailExtractor.cs` were named in the work item's "device acquisition ~6 classes" list but neither actually calls `CanvasDevice.GetSharedDevice()` directly (device is passed in as a parameter or delegated to an owned `FrameCompositor`) — nothing to centralize there; noted as a discrepancy rather than silently skipped.

## W3-2 — Geometry/time helpers: `AspectRatioHelper.GetRatioValue`, `FrameTimeConverter`, cursor-index/shutter-blur dedup (FIX 3, FIX 4, FIX 5)

- **Feature/area**: `Musio.Core/Processing/AspectRatioHelper.cs` (new `GetRatioValue`), new `Musio.Core/Processing/FrameTimeConverter.cs`, `FrameCompositor.cs` (deleted private `GetAspectRatioValue`, added `ResolveCursorIndex` and `DrawProgressiveShutterSamples` helpers), `Export/SegmentFrameComposer.cs`, `Export/VideoEncoder.cs`, `Export/TimelineMapper.cs` (all converted inlined time↔frame math onto `FrameTimeConverter`).
- **What worked**:
  - **FIX 5 (highest risk): enumerated all 14 inlined time↔frame sites and recorded each site's existing Round/Floor policy BEFORE writing `FrameTimeConverter`**, then gave each policy its own named method (`TimeToFrameRounded`, `TimeToFrameFloor`, `FrameToTime`) instead of collapsing to one. No policy was changed anywhere — see the report table in this task's final summary for the full site-by-site mapping. `TimeToFrameFloor` is documented as truncate-toward-zero (a plain `(int)` cast), explicitly NOT `Math.Floor`, since they differ for negative inputs and the original code was always the cast form.
  - **FIX 3 was straightforward and safe to converge**: `FrameCompositor.GetAspectRatioValue` (private, `float`, `-1f` for Auto) and `AspectRatioHelper.GetRatio` (public, `(int,int)`, `(0,0)` for Auto) were verified to compute the identical ratio for every enum value; added `AspectRatioHelper.GetRatioValue` with byte-for-byte the same switch arms/sentinel, deleted the private duplicate, repointed both call sites.
  - **Cursor-index formula** (`ComposeFrame(double)` and `ResolveZoomState`) folded into one `ResolveCursorIndex(double timeSeconds)` — both call sites used the exact same `Math.Clamp(Math.Round((t + offset) * fps), 0, count-1)` expression, confirmed identical before merging.
  - **Shutter-blur progressive-averaging loop** (`DrawCompositeWithMotionBlur` / `CropSourceFrameWithMotionBlur`) factored into a shared `DrawProgressiveShutterSamples(sampleCount, timeSeconds, shutterSeconds, Action<double sampleTime, float opacity> drawSample)` — the loop and `1f/(i+1)`/sample-time math were byte-identical between the two sites; only the per-sample draw call differed (composite-crop-rect draw vs. source-crop-sample draw), so a delegate captures exactly that difference without touching the numeric sequence.
- **What didn't work / reported instead of converged (FIX 4 + WebcamLayout divergence — do NOT unify these without a follow-up decision)**:
  - **`AspectRatioHelper.FitWithinBounds` (private) was NOT made public and the 3 "duplicate" sites were NOT converged onto it** — each of the 3 sites named in the work item computes something meaningfully different, not just a cosmetically-different copy of the same formula:
    - `SegmentFrameComposer.EnsureCanonicalSize` draws with **continuous double-precision** scale/width/height directly into a `Rect` (no integer rounding or even-forcing at all) — converging it onto `FitWithinBounds` (which rounds to `int` then forces even) would change the drawn pixel size/aspect for any non-even source.
    - `Mp4FrameSource.ComputeOutputDimensions` rounds via `Math.Round` then forces even via `& ~1` (**floors to even**, e.g. 5→4), and has an explicit "already fits, don't upscale" early-return that `FitWithinBounds` does not have (`FitWithinBounds` always scales up to fill maxWidth/maxHeight). `AspectRatioHelper.EnsureEven` **rounds up** on odd (5→6) — the opposite direction from `& ~1`.
    - `VideoThumbnailExtractor.cs` (both `ExtractAsync` and `ExtractFromCapturedFramesAsync`) contains **no two-bound "fit within max W and max H" computation at all** — its only sizing math is `width = Round(targetHeight * aspect)`, a fit-to-a-fixed-height calc, not a letterbox-fit. The work item's `~107` reference for a `FitWithinBounds` reimplementation does not correspond to any code in this file as it exists post-Wave-1/2/3-1; treat that line item as stale.
    - Conclusion: all 3 genuinely diverge from `FitWithinBounds` and from each other (continuous vs. round vs. floor-to-even; upscale-always vs. never-upscale; two-bound fit vs. fit-to-height). Left `FitWithinBounds` `private` and unchanged; no test references it directly (confirmed via grep — only a comment in `ExportDimensionsTests.cs` names it).
  - **`WebcamLayout.ComputeExtractSize` "duplicate" named in the work item does not exist as described** — there is no method by that name anywhere in the repo (`WebcamLayoutCalculator.ComputeAnimatedLayout` is the only public API on that type, and it does layout/positioning, not extract-resolution sizing). The actual webcam-extract-size logic (`displaySize`/`minDimension`/`WebcamExtractWidth`/`WebcamExtractHeight`) exists **only once**, in `SegmentFrameComposer.AttachWebcamAsync` (~lines 951–990) — `WebcamCompositor.cs` has no analog at all (confirmed via repo-wide grep for `ExtractWidth`/`ExtractHeight`/`minDimension`/`displaySize`). Nothing to converge; flagged as a stale work-item reference rather than silently ignored.
  - **Build & test**: `Musio.Tests` x64 clean rebuild → 0 errors. `Musio.App` ARM64 clean rebuild → 0 errors (both only pre-existing warnings: `CS0618` in `SlideTransitionsTests.cs`/`TransitionResolverTests.cs`, `MVVMTK0045` throughout `Musio.App` ViewModels). Full suite: **951 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped (953 total)** — exact match to baseline before and after.

## Wave 3, W3-3 — EditOperation shared state: TimelineSnapshot, ZoomOperationHelpers, SegmentEditOperationBase (FIX 6)

- **Feature/area**: `src/Musio.Core/Timeline/Operations/*.cs` (post-Wave-1 split; `EditOperation.cs` no longer exists — 33 operation classes live in `ClipOperations.cs`, `SegmentOperations.cs`, `ZoomOperations.cs`, `CameraOperations.cs`, `TextOverlayOperations.cs`, `TransitionOperations.cs`, `IEditOperation.cs`).
- **Task (a) — `TimelineSnapshot`/`SegmentListSnapshot`**: added `TimelineSnapshot.cs` with two `internal sealed` types. `TimelineSnapshot` (full legacy-model snapshot: Clips/ZoomKeyframes/SpeedSegments/Duration/TrimEnd, `includePlayhead` opt-in) replaced hand-rolled fields in `CutOperation`, `ApplyClipSpeedOperation`, `RippleDeleteOperation`. Confirmed by reading all three: only `ApplyClipSpeedOperation`/`RippleDeleteOperation` snapshot+restore `PlayheadPosition`; `CutOperation` never touches it — preserved as opt-in, did NOT add playhead restore to `CutOperation`. `SegmentListSnapshot` (Segments-only, `Restore` always calls `RecalculateSegmentPositions()`) replaced the `_previousSegments`/`Clear`/`AddRange`/recalculate quadruplet in `SplitAndInsertTextSlideOperation`, `SplitSegmentAtTimeOperation`, `ReorderSegmentOperation`, `MoveSegmentOperation`.
- **Minor convergence, verified safe**: `ApplyClipSpeedOperation`/`RippleDeleteOperation` previously rebuilt `TimelineClip` via `.Select(c => new TimelineClip(...))` instead of a true shallow `[.. model.Clips]` copy (unlike `CutOperation`). Since `TimelineClip` is an immutable `record` (value-equal either way) and grep found zero reference-equality checks on clips anywhere in `Musio.Tests`, unifying to `TimelineSnapshot`'s shallow copy is behavior-neutral — verified, not assumed.
- **Task (b) — `ZoomOperationHelpers`**: added static `SuppressClickIfAutoToManual(model, wasManual, sourceClickTicks)` / `RestoreClickSuppression(...)`, taking primitives (not a `ZoomKeyframe`) because `MoveZoomKeyframeOperation` only has primitive `_previousIsManual`/timestamp fields, not a stored keyframe object like the other three. Converted all 4 zoom ops (`MoveZoomKeyframeOperation`, `RemoveZoomKeyframeOperation`, `ResizeZoomSegmentOperation`, `UpdateZoomSegmentPropertiesOperation`) × Execute/Undo (8 call sites). Confirmed the guard/null semantics are identical at every site before converting.
- **Task (c) / FIX 6 — `SegmentEditOperationBase`**: template-method base class with `protected abstract bool ExecuteCore/UndoCore(TimelineModel)`; the base's public `Execute`/`Undo` call `RecalculateSegmentPositions()` **only when the core method returns true**. This — not an unconditional always-call — is what made the conversion behavior-neutral: audited all 9 segment-mutating operations (not the "16" the work item's stale line-number-era note estimated; grep-confirmed only `SegmentOperations.cs` classes ever call `RecalculateSegmentPositions`, since Clip/Camera/TextOverlay/Zoom/Transition operations deliberately operate on other lists/tracks and never call it — see the explicit comment at the top of the Camera-ops section) and found every one of the 9 (`AddTextSlideOperation`, `UpdateTextSlideOperation`, `RemoveSegmentOperation`, `ReorderSegmentOperation`, `AppendVideoSegmentOperation`, `SplitAndInsertTextSlideOperation`, `SplitSegmentAtTimeOperation`, `MoveSegmentOperation`, `TrimSegmentEdgeOperation`) already called it unconditionally at the very end of the success path, with only a fast-path "nothing to do" guard (invalid id/index, degenerate split, etc.) skipping it — never a genuine omission. All 9 were safe to convert by returning `false` on the existing guard paths and `true` at the end of the existing mutation path (placement unchanged — still "last thing before return"). No operation had to be left as `IEditOperation` due to a missing-call finding.
- **What worked**: the bool-return template method preserves every existing guard's behavior exactly (including `MoveSegmentOperation.Undo`, which was unconditional pre-refactor — its `UndoCore` always calls `_snapshot?.Restore` and returns `false`, since `SegmentListSnapshot.Restore` already recalculates internally, avoiding a double recalculate call for the 4 ops that use both a `SegmentListSnapshot` and the base class).
- **What didn't work / would have been wrong**: a literal "always call `RecalculateSegmentPositions` after `ExecuteCore`/`UndoCore` runs, no bool" template would have added a recalculate call on every guard/no-op path (e.g. `UpdateTextSlideOperation` when the id doesn't resolve) that doesn't happen today — rejected as a silent behavior change even though it's likely harmless in practice.
- **Build & test**: `Musio.Tests` x64 rebuild → 0 errors, 0 new warnings (only pre-existing `CS0618` in unrelated test files). `Musio.App` ARM64 rebuild → 0 errors. Full suite: **951 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped (953 total)** — exact match to baseline. Targeted filter across all 9 verification test classes named in the work item plus `TransitionCrossCuttingTests` (which exercises `SplitSegmentAtTimeOperation.DidSplit`): 144/144 passed.

## Wave 4, W4-2 — `EditorGraphicsDeviceManager` (device lifecycle + recovery retry/re-entrancy machinery)

- **Feature/area**: new `src/Musio.App/Services/EditorGraphicsDeviceManager.cs`; `EditorPage.xaml.cs` (device fields/methods removed, constructor/Unloaded wiring repointed); `EditorPage.Preview.cs` (one read-site of the in-progress flag repointed).
- **Approaches tried / what worked**:
  - **Split "device lifecycle + retry/re-entrancy" from "recovery work" along a delegate boundary, exactly as the work item specified**, instead of trying to move `EditorPage`'s renderer/preview fields out. The manager owns `_graphicsDevice`, `_graphicsRecoveryQueued`, `_graphicsRecoveryRequested`, `_graphicsRecoveryInProgress`, `Attach()`/`Detach()`, `OnGraphicsDeviceLost`, and the queued-retry `do/while` loop. `EditorPage.RecoverGraphicsDeviceAsync` (now just the injected `Func<Task>`) keeps 100% of its original body **except** the outer `if (_pageUnloaded || _graphicsRecoveryInProgress) return; _graphicsRecoveryInProgress = true; try { ... } finally { _graphicsRecoveryInProgress = false; }` wrapper, which moved into the manager's private `RunRecoveryAsync`. The mid-method `DetachGraphicsDevice(); AttachGraphicsDevice();` pair (which re-subscribes to a fresh `CanvasDevice.GetSharedDevice()` after tearing down old renderers, before rebuilding new ones) became two calls back into the manager (`_graphicsDeviceManager.Detach(); _graphicsDeviceManager.Attach();`) at the exact same point in the sequence — preserves the "detach/attach happens after old GPU resources are disposed, before `InitializePreviewCoreAsync` rebuilds them" ordering.
  - **Grepped the whole `Musio.App` tree (not just `EditorPage.xaml.cs`) for every field/method name before deleting them**, and found `EditorPage.Preview.cs`'s `UpdatePreviewFrameAsync` reads `_graphicsRecoveryInProgress` to decide whether to coalesce a render request rather than run it — a cross-partial-file reference the work item didn't call out. Exposed it as a public `EditorGraphicsDeviceManager.IsRecoveryInProgress` read-only property and repointed that one read site. The build would have caught the miss anyway (the field would no longer exist in that partial class), but finding it via grep up front avoided a spurious compile-error round trip.
  - **Used `GpuContext.GetSharedDevice()` instead of `CanvasDevice.GetSharedDevice()` directly** in the new manager's `Attach()`, per the work item's steer to complement rather than duplicate Wave 3's `GpuContext`. `GpuContext.GetSharedDevice()` is a pure pass-through to `CanvasDevice.GetSharedDevice()`, so this is behavior-identical, not a functional change. Did **not** reuse `GpuContext.DeviceLostGuard` — it only exposes a one-shot `IsLost`/`ThrowIfLost` flag for Core-side renderers to detect loss and rethrow on their next frame; it has no dispatcher-queue marshaling, retry-on-still-requested loop, or in-progress re-entrancy guard, which is the actual thing this work item needed to extract. Reimplementing the `EditorPage` machinery on top of `DeviceLostGuard` would have been a bigger, riskier change than the work item asked for.
  - Preserved the literal `if (!_dispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(...)) Interlocked.Exchange(ref _graphicsRecoveryQueued, 0);` shape verbatim (not simplified to a plain `TryEnqueue(...)`), per the explicit warning in the work item — added a comment on the reset explaining why it's load-bearing (a `false` `TryEnqueue` return means the retry loop body never runs, so nothing else would ever clear the flag).
- **What didn't work / considered and rejected**: flattening `RecoverGraphicsDeviceAsync`'s two `if (_pageUnloaded) return;` checks (one right after the `_isRendering` wait loop, one after `InitializePreviewCoreAsync` completes) into a single check was tempting but wrong — the second guards against the page having unloaded *during* the `await InitializePreviewCoreAsync()` call (which dispatches back through the same UI-thread queue as `Unloaded`), so both must remain as separate sequential checks exactly where they were.
- **Build & test**: `Musio.Tests` x64 rebuild → 0 errors, 0 new warnings. `Musio.App` ARM64 rebuild → 0 errors, 0 new warnings (only pre-existing `MVVMTK0045` in ViewModels). Full suite: **951 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped (953 total)** — exact match to baseline. `EditorPage` has no unit tests (needs a XAML host + GPU), so this item's correctness signal is the ARM64 build plus the lifetime audit in the task report, not the test suite.

## Wave 4, W4-1 — `OverlayScreenshotHelper` + `OverlayWindowBase` (GDI screenshot, Escape hook, borderless host-window scaffolding)

- **Feature/area**: new `src/Musio.App/Controls/OverlayScreenshotHelper.cs` and `OverlayWindowBase.cs`; `RegionSelectorOverlay.xaml.cs` and `WindowSelectorOverlay.xaml.cs` (ShowAsync/hook/GDI-screenshot code replaced with calls into the two new types); `MiniWindow.xaml.cs` and `RecordingOverlayWindow.xaml.cs` (presenter-setup 5-liner folded into a shared static helper only).
- **Approaches tried / what worked**:
  - **Confirmed Wave 2's `Musio.Core.Interop` already fully absorbed the GDI/hook P/Invoke set and the `SM_*VIRTUALSCREEN` constants** (`NativeMethods`, `HookInterop`, `VirtualDesktopInfo`, `MonitorInterop`, `NativeStructs`) before touching anything — confirmed the two remaining work-item items (5 and 6) really were pre-done, so this item's real scope was exactly the 3 subsystems the updated work item called out.
  - **`OverlayScreenshotHelper.CaptureDesktopScreenshotAsync(bool includePixels)`**: one static method replacing the ~85-line duplicate in each overlay. Returns a small `readonly struct OverlayScreenshotResult { Source, Pixels, Width, Height }`. The window picker passes `includePixels: false`; the local `pixelData` byte[] is still allocated internally (unavoidable — `GetDIBits`/`SoftwareBitmap.CopyFromBuffer` need it to build the bitmap), but is never returned/retained when the flag is false, so the window picker never holds a live reference to the full-desktop buffer past the call — matching its original behavior exactly (the original `WindowSelectorOverlay.CaptureDesktopScreenshotAsync` never returned pixels either).
  - **`OverlayWindowBase` chosen as an `internal abstract` class used via composition (a private nested `OverlayHost : OverlayWindowBase` inside each picker), not as the picker's own base class.** `RegionSelectorOverlay`/`WindowSelectorOverlay` are `sealed partial class : UserControl`, and their XAML root element is `<UserControl>` — the C# base type is dictated by the `.xaml` root tag via codegen. Making the pickers inherit `OverlayWindowBase` directly would require changing both `.xaml` root elements to a custom base type, which is out of the work item's named file scope (`.xaml.cs` only) and adds risk in a zero-test-coverage UI surface for no behavioral benefit. The nested-subclass pattern gets the same abstraction (`abstract OnEscapePressed()`, `abstract Content`, `abstract DispatcherQueue`, `virtual OnBeforeScreenshotAsync()`, `abstract OnHostWindowReady(OverlayScreenshotResult)`) without touching XAML at all.
  - **`OverlayWindowBase.ShowOverlayAsync<T>` had to be made `public`, not `protected`** — the owning picker (`RegionSelectorOverlay`/`WindowSelectorOverlay`) calls it on its `_overlayHost` field from outside the nested subclass, so `protected` (accessible only from within `OverlayWindowBase` or a subclass) doesn't reach it; only the abstract/virtual members the nested `OverlayHost` overrides stay `protected`.
  - **Broadened the try/finally coverage slightly, deliberately, and documented it**: `RegionSelectorOverlay`'s original code ran the shell-hide + `Task.Delay(300)` *before* entering its `try/finally`, while `WindowSelectorOverlay`'s ran it *inside*. Since `Task.Delay(constant)` cannot throw in this codepath (no `CancellationToken` passed, no negative delay), unifying both onto the safer (`WindowSelectorOverlay`-style) "hide+delay inside the try" shape is a no-observable-difference change that the work item explicitly permits ("verify your finally coverage is at least as complete as today's").
  - **Preserved each file's own `Task.Delay` value** (300 ms region / 400 ms window) and each file's own title ("Select Region" / "Select Window") and `includeScreenshotPixels` flag as per-call arguments to `ShowOverlayAsync`, not unified constants.
  - **Preserved `WindowSelectorOverlay`'s "enumerate windows + compute virtual-desktop bounds before the screenshot" ordering** via the new `virtual OnBeforeScreenshotAsync()` hook (default no-op, overridden only by `WindowSelectorOverlay.OverlayHost`) — `RegionSelectorOverlay` doesn't need it and doesn't override it.
  - **Folded the 4× duplicated `OverlappedPresenter` setup** (`SetBorderAndTitleBar(false,false)` + `IsAlwaysOnTop/IsResizable/IsMaximizable/IsMinimizable`) into `OverlayWindowBase.ConfigureBorderlessAlwaysOnTopPresenter(AppWindow)` (public static) — confirmed byte-for-byte identical across `RegionSelectorOverlay`, `WindowSelectorOverlay`, `MiniWindow.ConfigureWindow`, and `RecordingOverlayWindow.ConfigureWindow` before folding; `MiniWindow`/`RecordingOverlayWindow` keep their own surrounding code (title bar drag-grip, DWM border/caption/corner-rounding, capture-affinity, sizing) untouched — only the presenter block moved.
  - **Cleaned up now-unused usings** in both overlay files after moving GDI/hook code out (`System.Runtime.InteropServices`, `System.Runtime.InteropServices.WindowsRuntime`, `Microsoft.UI.Windowing`, `Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Media.Imaging`/`Windows.Graphics.Imaging` where no longer referenced) and added `Microsoft.UI.Dispatching` (needed for the nested `OverlayHost.DispatcherQueue` override's return type).
- **What didn't work / rejected**: an inheritance-based `OverlayWindowBase : UserControl` design with the pickers deriving from it directly — rejected specifically because it requires editing the `.xaml` root element type in a file the work item didn't list, on a UI surface with no automated test coverage.
- **Build & test**: `Musio.Tests` x64 rebuild → 0 errors, 0 new warnings. `Musio.App` ARM64 rebuild → 0 errors, 0 new warnings (only pre-existing `MVVMTK0045`/`CS0618`). Full suite: **951 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped (953 total)** — exact match to baseline, both before and after the `MiniWindow`/`RecordingOverlayWindow` presenter fold-in. Neither overlay has automated test coverage; correctness signal is the ARM64 build plus the capture/lifetime audit in the task report — manual verification (drag-select/snap/Escape/multi-monitor/mixed-DPI for region; hover/Escape/minimized-exclusion for window; shell minimize+restore on confirm/cancel/error for both) is still required from a human reviewer.

## Wave 4, W4-3 — `EditorTimelineMediator` (timeline ↔ preview ↔ undo/redo wiring, partial extraction)

- **Feature/area**: new `src/Musio.App/Services/EditorTimelineMediator.cs` (static class); `EditorPage.Timeline.cs` (11 handler/sync methods reduced to thin forwards, `RemoveZoomSegment_Click` deduped against `OnZoomSegmentRemoveRequested`); `EditorPage.TextEditing.cs` (3 raw duplicates of `RefreshSlidePreview()` collapsed to call it, new `RefreshOverlayPreviewAndTimeline()` helper collapses 3 raw `Timeline.InvalidateAllCanvases(); RefreshOverlayPreview();` duplicates).
- **Approaches tried / what worked**:
  - **First did an equivalence-class pass over every "invalidate timeline + refresh preview" call site** across `EditorPage.Timeline.cs`, `.TextEditing.cs`, `.Preview.cs`, `.Style.cs`, `.Shortcuts.cs`, `.xaml.cs` before touching anything, per the work item's explicit requirement. Found the "21 sites" figure in the work item was approximate: most `RefreshOverlayPreview()`/`ScheduleOverlayPreviewRefresh()`/`InvalidatePreview()` call sites were ALREADY unified behind those three named methods (used consistently, correctly NOT collapsed into one — debounced vs immediate vs full-mapper-rebuild are genuinely different refresh cadences and must stay separate). The real duplication was a much smaller set of **raw, un-wrapped 2-statement pairs**: 3 sites in `SlideTextBox_TextChanged`/`SlideTextColorPicker_ColorChanged`/`SlideBgColorPicker_ColorChanged` byte-for-byte duplicating `RefreshSlidePreview()`'s body, and 3 sites (`OverlayPreset_SelectionChanged`/`OverlayDurationBox_ValueChanged`/`OverlayEnabledToggle_Toggled`) duplicating `Timeline.InvalidateAllCanvases(); RefreshOverlayPreview();`. Unified only those — every other site was either already a single named call or a genuine class-of-one (GPU-recovery refresh, zoom-region-edit enter/exit, webcam-style preview, playhead-scrub sync) and was left untouched.
  - **Confirmed `SlideDurationBox_ValueChanged`'s `Timeline.InvalidateAllCanvases(); InvalidatePreview();` is NOT the same class as `RefreshSlidePreview()`** despite superficial similarity — duration changes shift every subsequent segment's position (needs the full mapper rebuild + zoom resync `InvalidatePreview()` does), while text/color changes don't move anything on the timeline. Did not unify these.
  - **Confirmed `OnUndoRedoStateChanged`'s explicit `Timeline.Refresh(); InvalidatePreview();`** is a harmless pre-existing redundancy (`InvalidatePreview()` already calls `Timeline.Refresh()` internally) — left untouched rather than "fixed", per behavior-preservation rules (not a listed `[FIX n]`).
  - **Extracted only the genuinely decoupled half of the mediator scope.** Two kinds of code moved cleanly into `EditorTimelineMediator` (a static class, not an instantiated service, since it owns no lifetime/state):
    1. The "construct edit operation → `UndoRedoManager.Execute` → done" glue for primary/camera/zoom/text-overlay track move+resize gestures (`OnSegmentMoveRequested`, `OnSegmentTrimRequested`, `OnCameraSegmentMoved/Resized`, `OnZoomSegmentMoved/Resized`, `OnTextOverlayMoved/Resized`, `OnZoomSegmentRemoveRequested`) — none of these touch a XAML-named control beyond `TimelineControl` itself, which is passed as a parameter.
    2. The model→renderer sync helpers explicitly named in the work item (`SyncZoomStateToRenderer`, `SyncTextOverlaysToRenderer`, `SyncSegmentZoomStateToRenderers`, plus their shared `ManualKeyframesForSource`/`SegmentForSource`) — rewritten as pure static functions taking `TimelineModel`, a `PreviewRenderer?`, and `PrimaryVideoPath` as parameters instead of reading `_previewRenderer`/`_segmentPreviews` fields directly. `EditorPage.Timeline.cs` keeps same-named instance-method wrappers (e.g. `private void SyncZoomStateToRenderer() => EditorTimelineMediator.SyncZoomStateToRenderer(ViewModel.Model, _previewRenderer, PrimaryVideoPath);`) so the ~15 existing call sites spread across `Preview.cs`/`Style.cs`/`TextEditing.cs`/`xaml.cs` needed ZERO edits — this was the key move that made the extraction low-risk: physically relocate the logic, but never require touching call sites outside the mediator's own file.
    3. Deduped `RemoveZoomSegment_Click` (properties-pane delete button) against `OnZoomSegmentRemoveRequested` (timeline's own delete gesture) — both had an identical 3-line body; `RemoveZoomSegment_Click` now just forwards to `OnZoomSegmentRemoveRequested`.
  - **`_segmentPreviews` is a `Dictionary<string, SegmentPreview>` where `SegmentPreview` is a `private sealed class` nested in `EditorPage` (declared in `Preview.cs`)** — to avoid exposing that private type across the assembly boundary, `SyncSegmentZoomStateToRenderers`'s mediator signature takes `IEnumerable<(string SegmentId, PreviewRenderer? Renderer)>` instead of the dictionary type; the `EditorPage` wrapper projects `_segmentPreviews.Select(kv => (kv.Key, kv.Value.Renderer))` before calling in.
- **What didn't work / deliberately NOT extracted** (see full report to reviewer for the complete list): `OnUndoRedoStateChanged`, `OnSegmentSelected`, `OnZoomSegmentSelected/Created`, `OnCameraSegmentSelected/Created`, `OnTextOverlaySelected/Created`, `OnTransitionSelected` and the transition property-panel handlers in `TextEditing.cs` were all left in place — each reads/writes multiple XAML-named controls directly (`ZoomLevelCombo`, `CameraFullscreenPanel`, transition combo boxes, text-slide/overlay panes) or private `EditorPage` fields (`_selectedTransitionId`, `_zoomRegionEditMode`, etc.) that have no clean parameter-passing shape; moving them would require either passing `EditorPage` itself (not a real extraction) or 6+ delegate parameters per method (indirection risk with no benefit). `EnsureTimelineMapper`/`GetMappedDuration`/`InvalidatePreview` were also left in place — `InvalidatePreview` needs `_timelineMapper`/`_lastRenderedFrameIndex`/`_compositorReady` cache fields and the async `UpdatePreviewFrameAsync` page method, none of which have a clean pure-function shape either.
  - Also confirmed via `grep` that Timeline-control event subscriptions (`Timeline.SegmentMoveRequested += OnSegmentMoveRequested;` etc., all in `EditorPage.xaml.cs`'s constructor-time wiring block) and the `ViewModel.UndoRedoManager.StateChanged += OnUndoRedoStateChanged;` subscription (+ its resubscribe inside the `ModelReloaded` handler) needed **zero changes** — every `On*` method kept its exact name and signature, so every `+=` target still resolves to the same (now-thin) method.
- **Build & test**: `Musio.App` ARM64 rebuild → 0 errors, 0 new warnings (only pre-existing `MVVMTK0045` in unrelated files). `Musio.Tests` x64 rebuild → 0 errors. Full suite: **951 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped (953 total)** — exact match to baseline. `EditorPage`/`TimelineControl` have no automated coverage; manual verification (undo/redo across every track type, selection clearing, panel re-sync, playhead consistency, slider debounce) is still required from a human reviewer.

## Wave 4, W4-4 — TrackGestureHandler<TSegment> (STOPPED after Stage 1 — coordinate converter only)

- **Feature/area**: new src/Musio.App/Controls/TimeCoordinateConverter.cs (static class: TimeToX/XToTime/SourceTimeToX/XToPrimarySourceTime, extracted verbatim from TimelineControl.xaml.cs); TimelineControl.xaml.cs's four eponymous instance methods reduced to one-line adapters passing Model, TimeRulerCanvas.ActualWidth, ActualWidth. Stage 2 (a generic TrackGestureHandler<TSegment> unifying the zoom/camera/text-overlay *_PointerPressed/Moved/Released/RightTapped handlers) was investigated and explicitly NOT attempted.
- **What worked (Stage 1)**: Made the four conversion methods static, taking TimelineModel?, the ruler-canvas width, and the control width as explicit parameters instead of reading instance state — this is what let them move out of TimelineControl with zero behavior change (they're pure functions of those three inputs). Kept TimelineControl.TrackContentInset as a const alias (= TimeCoordinateConverter.TrackContentInset) rather than editing its other 3 call sites in TimeRulerCanvas_Draw, to keep the diff minimal. Build (Musio.Tests x64, Musio.App ARM64) 0 errors; full suite 951/953 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed — exact baseline match.
- **Why Stage 2 was stopped (report exactly, don't smooth over)**: a line-by-line diff of ZoomTrack_* vs CameraTrack_* vs TextTrack_* found FIVE separate real behavioral divergences beyond simple threshold values, not just naming/cosmetic differences:
  1. ***_RightTapped UI flow differs, not just the event fired.** ZoomTrack_RightTapped shows a MenuFlyout with a "Remove Zoom Segment" MenuFlyoutItem and only removes on that item's Click; CameraTrack_RightTapped/TextTrack_RightTapped call their *RemoveRequested event immediately with no confirmation/menu step at all.
  2. **Edge-hit gating differs.** HitTestZoomSegment only tests left/right edge hit-zones if (kf.Id == _selectedZoomKeyframeId) — i.e. you must select a zoom segment (first click) before you can grab its edge (second click). HitTestCameraSegment/HitTestTextOverlay test edges unconditionally on every hit-test, so camera/text segments can be edge-grabbed on the very first click. Unifying hit-test into one generic algorithm would silently change one track's UX.
  3. **Source-domain resolution for drag-to-create differs by domain model, not just code shape.** Zoom/TextOverlay support cross-recording attribution (XToSegmentVideoTime/XToKeyframeFileTime, tracking _zoomCreateFile/_textOverlayCreateFile, null = primary); Camera has no per-file field at all and always resolves through XToPrimarySourceTime (primary-source-time-only), because CameraSegment doesn't model cross-recording ownership the way ZoomKeyframe/TextOverlaySegment do.
  4. **Edge hit-width is a separate constant per track even though the values coincide today**: ZoomSegmentEdgeHitWidth = 8 (zoom-only) vs SegmentEdgeHitWidth = 8.0 (shared by camera + text). Collapsing these into one generic parameter would still be behavior-preserving today (values match) but removes the independent tunability the separate constants exist for.
  5. **Move-commit zero-clamping differs.** Camera's and Text's *_PointerReleased body-move case explicitly clamps 
ewStart/
ewStart to TimeSpan.Zero before raising *Moved; Zoom's does not clamp at all (relies on downstream validation).
  - Given these 5 substantive divergences (not just parameterizable thresholds), a truly generic TrackGestureHandler<TSegment> would have to thread most of "shared" logic through per-track delegates/flags anyway (hit-test delegate, create-domain-resolution delegate, right-click-flow delegate, clamp-behavior flag), shrinking the real code-sharing benefit while raising the chance of a delegate mis-wiring that flips one of these behaviors for one track — on a file with **zero automated test coverage**, where a gesture regression is invisible to dotnet test and only surfaces as an immediately-felt UX bug (per the work item's own warning). Per the work item's explicit instruction ("if at any point stage 2 looks like it cannot be done without changing behavior, STOP, keep stage 1, and report exactly what blocked you"), Stage 2 was not attempted; Stage 1 lands standalone.
- **What didn't work / rejected**: did not attempt a partial/hybrid generic handler (e.g. genericizing only PointerMoved, or only the create-gesture flow) — even the "simple" pieces (PointerMoved's switch on _dragMode) are entangled with the per-track _zoomDragCurrentX/_cameraDragCurrentX/_textOverlayDragCurrentX fields and the DragMode enum's per-track-prefixed values, so a partial extraction would still need the same per-track delegate surface as a full one, for a smaller win.
- **Build & test**: Musio.Tests x64 rebuild → 0 errors, 0 new warnings. Musio.App ARM64 rebuild → 0 errors, 0 new warnings (only pre-existing MVVMTK0045). Full suite: **951 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped (953 total)** — exact match to baseline.

## Meta — Waves 1-4 code-optimization programme (orchestrated, 4 stacked PRs)

- **Feature/area**: repo-wide maintainability. Four stacked PRs off `master`: #76 file organisation, #77 shared helpers, #78 Core helpers + correctness fixes, #79 UI consolidation. Baseline held at **951 passed / 2 skipped / 0 failed** through every one of the 11 work items.
- **What worked — mechanical fidelity proofs instead of review-by-reading.** For the three pure-move splits, correctness was *proven*, not inspected: `Compare-Object` of each new `learnings/` file against the original slice (0 diffs); per-type byte comparison of all 34 `EditOperation` classes; and a multiset line comparison for the 8,604-line `EditorPage` split showing 0 lines lost or altered, with all 139 additions classified as `using`/`namespace`/brace. An altered line surfaces as an unmatched original-side entry — reading 8,600 lines could never give that guarantee.
- **What worked — "report, don't resolve" as an explicit standing instruction.** Every sub-agent prompt said: where two apparent duplicates disagree, stop and report rather than pick a winner. This directly prevented several real regressions: consolidating `ShellCoordinator`'s `MONITORINFO`-only `GetMonitorInfo` (a genuinely different marshalling); converging `Mp4FrameSource`'s round-**down**-to-even (`& ~1`) with `AspectRatioHelper`'s round-**up**-to-even (`value + 1`); and collapsing `RefreshOverlayPreview` (immediate) into `ScheduleOverlayPreviewRefresh` (debounced). Each would have compiled and passed all 951 tests.
- **What worked — designing prompts so the safe half can land alone.** W4-4 was mandated as two stages with an explicit stop condition. The agent extracted `TimeCoordinateConverter`, then found five real behavioural divergences across the three track gesture stacks and stopped. Without the staged framing the likely outcome was an all-or-nothing attempt at a 600-line generalisation over a file with zero coverage.
- **What worked — preserving asymmetry by making it explicit in the API.** Repeatedly the right answer was to *encode* a divergence rather than erase it: `TestTimelineBuilder.TransitionVideo` deliberately has **no default** for `videoFilePath`, forcing each call site to keep stating `C:\clip.mp4` vs `C:\primary.mp4`; `GrowOnlyBuffer` exposes two growth policies because its four call sites used two; `TimelineSnapshot` makes playhead capture opt-in because `CutOperation` never restored it; `SegmentEditOperationBase.ExecuteCore` returns `bool` so recalculation only fires where it already did.
- **What didn't work — trusting the audit's own numbers.** The analysis that produced this plan was measurably wrong in several places, and only implementation contact caught it: `Measure-Object -Line` **skips blank lines**, so every quoted file length was ~15% low (EditorPage is 8,604, not 7,457); `WebcamLayout.ComputeExtractSize` does not exist; `SegmentFrameComposer.SyncZoomState` is `private`, not `public`; the `VideoThumbnailExtractor` letterbox reference was stale; "16 operations must call `RecalculateSegmentPositions`" was really 9, none of which had a genuine omission; and "21 refresh sites to unify" were mostly already unified. **Write prompts that tell the agent to verify cited evidence and correct it, rather than treating the plan as ground truth.**
- **What didn't work — file-scoped sweeps after a class split.** W2-3 converted the `try/finally` suppress-flag guards in `EditorPage.*` but missed three identical ones in `SettingsPage.xaml.cs`, because the prompt framed the work around the originally-named file. Review caught it. After a partial-class split, **grep the pattern app-wide, never just the files the task names**.
- **Build coordination on this repo**: x64 (`Musio.Tests`) and ARM64 (`Musio.App`) write to separate `bin`/`obj` subtrees, so two agents can build concurrently **only** if each is pinned to one platform and one project. Anything touching both `Musio.Core` and `Musio.App` must run alone.
- **Verification**: final full clean rebuild (all three projects, `bin`/`obj` deleted) 0 errors; suite 951/2/0. `EditorPage`, `TimelineControl` and the overlays have no automated coverage, so PR #79 carries an explicit manual checklist (TDR recovery, both pickers at mixed DPI, undo/redo per track, slider debounce).

## Investigation — preview renders garbage + filmstrip empty (root cause NOT found; Core exonerated)

- **Feature/area**: reported after running the Waves 1-4 optimization branch: preview showed hard-edged white/black garbage rectangles that appeared only after some playback load, and the filmstrip stopped loading. Only log evidence was `[Filmstrip] no thumbnails for segment '<path>' (primary '')`.
- **The single most useful discovery — the preview path logged into the void.** All nine error handlers in `EditorPage.Preview.cs`/`EditorPage.Timeline.cs` used `System.Diagnostics.Debug.WriteLine`, which reaches an attached debugger and **nothing else**. So every preview/thumbnail failure was invisible in `diag.log`, which is why a visibly-broken render produced a near-empty log. Routed them all to `DiagLog`, and split the filmstrip's silent give-up into two distinguishable causes: extraction returned nothing, versus a completed strip discarded because a newer generation superseded it (a repeating supersede means a rebuild storm, not a decode failure). `DeviceLostGuard` now logs too. **If this recurs, read `diag.log` first — it will now say why.**
- **Hypotheses tested and DISPROVEN (do not re-investigate these):**
  1. *`GrowOnlyBuffer` reallocating every frame* — W3-1 added a `ReferenceEquals(_device, device)` check to `CursorRenderer`'s scratch buffer where the old code checked size only. If WinRT returned a fresh projection wrapper per call this would thrash GPU memory. **Verified reference-stable** for `CanvasDrawingSession.Device`, `CanvasRenderTarget.Device` and `GetSharedDevice()`; locked in by `GrowOnlyBufferDeviceIdentityTests`.
  2. *Thumbnail extraction broken* — ran the real `VideoThumbnailExtractor` against both of the reporter's actual project videos: 8 and 15 tiles decoded, no failures.
  3. *FIX 1 changed the exception type so a `catch` stopped matching* — the documented risk of wrapping OOM/`COMException` into `InvalidOperationException`. Grepped every `catch` naming those types; none guard a render-target allocation, so no site lost its handler.
  4. *Sustained playback + adaptive-resolution rebuild corrupting the compositor* — the actual reported trigger. Built `PreviewSustainedRenderProbeTests` to drive `FrameCompositor` across three generations at different source scales using the reporter's own video: clean throughout (~0.1% white). **Core compositing is exonerated.**
  5. *Device loss / TDR* — no display-driver reset events in the Windows System log for the period.
  6. *My deploy clobbering the running app* — the loose-layout folder was last written 23:11:56, before the 23:13:32 process start, so nothing was swapped underneath it.
- **Every Wave 1-4 change re-verified as faithful during this pass**: the `EditorPage` split was already proven a zero-line-change move; W3-1's four `EditorPage.Preview.cs` conversions are semantically identical; W4-2's `IsRecoveryInProgress` is the same field behind a property; W4-3's `RefreshSlidePreview`/`RefreshOverlayPreviewAndTimeline` bodies are byte-equivalent to the lines they replaced. `FrameCompositor.Dispose` still releases both buffer holders, and no code disposes a holder-backed target behind the holder's back.
- **What the remaining evidence points at** (unproven): `(primary '')` means the primary strip was never registered, i.e. `GenerateTimelineThumbnailsAsync` returned false — either extraction failed in-app (it does not fail standalone) or the pass was superseded by a newer generation. A rebuild storm would explain every symptom at once: superseded thumbnails leave the filmstrip permanently empty, and half-built frames flash as garbage. Consistent with the observed ~28% CPU and 673MB->1GB working-set growth while the editor sat open, and with the prior "runaway compositor rebuild" entry in this archive. The new discard logging distinguishes this in one repro.
- **What didn't work / avoid repeating**: inspecting diffs alone. Four plausible-sounding culprits were all wrong, and each was only settled by executing something — a probe test, the real extractor, the event log, or file timestamps. Note also that `ComposeFrame(bitmap, int)` validates against `TotalFrames`, so a harness must pass `duration:` to `InitializeAsync` or every call throws `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`; and Win2D forbids two concurrently-open drawing sessions on one render target.
- **Deliberately did NOT do**: speculative edits to the render path. No defect was found there, and changing faithful code on suspicion risks masking the real fault while leaving it in place.

## Root cause — preview corrupts under fast timeline scrubbing (regression from W3-1 `GrowOnlyBuffer`)

- **Feature/area**: `Musio.Core/Processing/GrowOnlyBuffer.cs` and its call sites (`FrameCompositor`, `CursorRenderer`, `TextOverlayRenderer`). Reported as: preview renders hard-edged garbage rectangles after fast scrubbing, and stays broken.
- **What isolated it**: an A/B build. Master scrubs cleanly; the optimization branch corrupts. That narrowed it to the 15 wave commits, and the trigger (fast scrubbing → constant buffer resizes) pointed at the one commit that changed GPU buffer lifetime. A second free A/B fell out of the same run: **master logs the identical `[Filmstrip] no thumbnails ... (primary '')`**, so the missing filmstrip is a PRE-EXISTING bug and was a red herring for this regression — do not conflate them again.
- **Two distinct faults, both from unifying things the call sites deliberately disagreed on:**
  1. **Replacement ordering.** `FrameCompositor`'s composite+crop buffers and `CursorRenderer`'s shutter scratch all did `Dispose()` → allocate. Only `TextOverlayRenderer` did allocate → `Dispose()`. `GrowOnlyBuffer` adopted allocate-first for all, so the two largest buffers in the pipeline (full output resolution, ~14 MB each at 2304×1536) were **held twice across every resize**. Scrubbing resizes them constantly via adaptive preview resolution and zoom.
  2. **Device comparison.** `FrameCompositor` and `CursorRenderer` compared **size only**; `TextOverlayRenderer` compared the device with Win2D's `!=`. `GrowOnlyBuffer` added `!ReferenceEquals(_device, device)` to all three. `CursorRenderer` obtains its device from a **per-frame `CanvasDrawingSession`**, and reading a device back can return a different projection wrapper around the same device — so the check can report a change every frame and reallocate a full-size render target each time.
- **Why every test stayed green through all of it**: neither fault changes a single rendered pixel. They produce *correct* frames while burning GPU memory, until the device can no longer serve allocations and output degenerates. Pixel/output assertions are structurally blind to this. The new tests therefore assert **reuse and disposal ordering**, not image content — `GrowOnlyBufferReplacePolicyTests` proves the old target is disposed during the swap under the default policy, and `GrowOnlyBufferDeviceIdentityTests` proves 20 constant-size frames allocate exactly once.
- **The device-identity test caught fault 2 by accident, and only under parallelism.** Run standalone it passed; run alongside the rest of the suite it failed with "did not round-trip to the creating device instance". **Do not assume `ReferenceEquals` on any CsWinRT-projected object is stable** — compare with the projection's own equality operator.
- **What didn't work / avoid repeating**: four rounds of pure diff-reading concluded "verbatim, equivalent" for `TimeCoordinateConverter`, the shutter-blur fold, the aspect-ratio switch and the `EditorPage` split — all correct, and all irrelevant. The regression was in *resource lifetime*, which reads as equivalent because the drawing code genuinely is. Reviewing an extraction for behaviour-preservation must explicitly include allocation ordering, disposal ordering, and comparison semantics, not just the arithmetic and control flow.
- **Rule to carry forward**: when a helper absorbs N call sites, enumerate every axis on which those sites differ *before* writing it, and make each axis a parameter rather than a decision. Here the axes were growth policy (correctly parameterised), replacement ordering (wrongly unified) and device comparison (wrongly unified) — two out of three were missed, and the commit message asserted the opposite of what the code did.
- **Verification**: `Musio.Tests` x64 and `Musio.App` ARM64 clean rebuilds, 0 errors; suite **957 passed, 3 skipped, 0 failed**; fixed build deployed and launched on ARM64 for scrub verification.

## PR review round on the Wave 1-4 optimization stack (PRs #76-#80)

- **Feature/area**: `Musio.Core/Timeline/Operations/*`, `docs/CODE_OPTIMIZATION_TODO.md`, `Musio.Core/Processing/Win2DUtils.cs`, `Musio.App/Controls/OverlayWindowBase.cs` + `OverlayScreenshotHelper.cs`.
- **The file split had a systematic off-by-one-file defect.** Splitting `EditOperation.cs` into seven files left **every** new file ending with the section header and XML doc comment belonging to the *first type of the next file*. So `ClipOperations` ended with the "Segment-based edit operations" header, `SegmentOperations` with the camera header + `AddCameraSegmentOperation`'s summary, `CameraOperations` with the text-overlay header + `AddTextOverlayOperation`'s summary, and `TextOverlayOperations` with `UpdateTransitionOperation`'s entire summary/remarks. Four orphaned doc blocks (CS1587) *and* four types silently stripped of their documentation.
- **Why the fidelity proof missed it**: the split was verified by extracting all 34 *type bodies* and byte-comparing them, plus a multiset line comparison. Both are blind to this: no line was lost or altered, and no type body changed — only the file boundaries were drawn one block too early. **A move-only refactor needs its cut points verified against type *boundaries*, not just its content verified against a line multiset.** Cheap check: every split file should end with `}` and start with a type/namespace, never with a comment.
- **Do not "just delete" an orphaned doc block.** In three of four cases the block was the missing documentation of a real type; deleting it would have quietly destroyed docs while making the warning go away. Move it to the type it describes.
- **`MoveSegmentOperation`'s truncated summary pre-existed on `master`** (it lost its opening `<summary>` long before this split). Reviewers attributing it to the split were wrong about the cause but right about the defect — check `git show master:<file>` before accepting blame for a flagged line.
- **Two reviewer claims were wrong and were rebutted rather than "fixed":** that `private static readonly IntPtr WM_KEYDOWN = 0x0100;` and `HookInterop.CallNextHookEx`'s `nint`/`IntPtr` mix would not compile. On .NET 7+/C# 11 **`IntPtr` and `nint` are the same type**, so there is no conversion and no x86 truncation risk. Settled by a clean `Musio.App` x64 MSBuild (0 errors), not by argument. Expect this false positive to recur on any interop code in this repo.
- **Reviewer claims that WERE valid, and all were inherited verbatim from `master`'s per-overlay copies** — consolidation is what made them fixable once instead of twice: `GetDIBits`/`BI_RGB` leaves the 4th byte undefined while `SoftwareBitmapSource` demands `Bgra8`/`Premultiplied` (alpha 0 renders the whole picker screenshot invisible — now forced to 255); and the Escape `WH_KEYBOARD_LL` hook installed with a NULL `hMod` and an unchecked result, unlike `KeyboardHookRecorder` (now `GetModuleHandle(null)` + `SetLastError` + `DiagLog` on failure).
- **Backlog citations rot the moment the split they describe lands.** OPT-001..004 and OPT-204 all cited the pre-split monolith and were stale in the very PR that made them stale. If a backlog item cites line numbers in a file the same PR is moving, re-derive the citations in that PR.
- **Kept `Assert.Inconclusive` over `[Ignore]`** for `PreviewSustainedRenderProbeTests` against reviewer advice: this repo uses `Assert.Inconclusive` for environment gates (46 uses across 9 files) and reserves `[Ignore]` for tests failing against known defects. `[Ignore]` would also make the probe unrunnable without editing code. Documented the intent on the class instead.
- **Stack mechanics**: fix in the branch that *owns* the file, then merge forward `wave1 -> wave2 -> wave3 -> wave4 -> code-optimization-all`. One conflict, in `SegmentOperations.cs`, where wave1's new section header met wave3's `SegmentEditOperationBase` base-class change on the same line — keep both.
- **Verification**: `Musio.Tests` x64 and `Musio.App` x64 builds 0 errors at every level of the stack; suite **957 passed, 3 skipped, 0 failed** on waves 3/4/all and **951/2/0** on wave 1, matching each branch's pre-change baseline. All seven review threads answered and resolved.
- **What didn't work**: `dotnet test` (and `dotnet build`) still fail here with the PriGen `MSB4062` error — the playbook rule holds. Build with VS MSBuild first, then `dotnet test --no-build`. On this host MSBuild lives under **Build Tools 2022** (`C:\Program Files (x86)\Microsoft Visual Studio\2022\BuildTools\MSBuild\Current\Bin\MSBuild.exe`), not the Enterprise 18 path the playbook cites, and `vswhere -find` returned nothing — fall back to `vswhere -property installationPath`.

## Restacking the optimization PRs after #76/#77 merged, and the `ExecuteCore` bool contract (PR #78)

- **Feature/area**: branch topology for the wave stack; `Musio.Core/Timeline/Operations/SegmentEditOperationBase.cs`.
- **The conflict was topological, not textual.** Once #76 and #77 merged, `wave2` had been **rebased** before merging, so its three commits gained new SHAs in `master`. `wave3` still carried the *old* SHAs plus merge commits, so GitHub reported `CONFLICTING` even though the trees agreed. Re-merging cannot fix this; the branch has to be replayed.
- **`git rebase origin/master` is the right tool and it does the hard part for you** — it detected all three rewritten wave2 commits as patch-identical and skipped them ("skipped previously applied commit"), leaving only wave3's own five commits. **Verify with `git diff <backup> HEAD`, which must be empty**; take a `backup/<branch>-prerebase` branch first so that check is possible at all.
- **After rewriting a branch, do NOT rebase its child onto it directly.** `git rebase wave3` from `wave4` tried to replay wave3's *old* commits, because the amended wave3 was no longer patch-identical, and immediately hit add/add conflicts on files wave3 had created. Use the three-arg form instead — `git rebase --onto <new-wave3> <backup/wave3-prerebase> wave4` — which replays only the commits genuinely unique to the child. Same pattern again for the integration branch.
- **The one real conflict recurs at the same spot every time**: `SegmentOperations.cs` line 3, where wave1's `// ─── Segment-based edit operations ───` header meets wave3's `: SegmentEditOperationBase` base-class change on the same line. Resolution is always "keep both". Two markdown sections in `CODE_OPTIMIZATION_TODO.md` (OPT-105/106 vs OPT-107) also collide as add/add; they are independent, so keep both, ordered by OPT number.
- **Valid review finding on the `ExecuteCore`/`UndoCore` bool.** The docs said it means "the segment list was mutated", but four `UndoCore` overrides restore the entire list through `SegmentListSnapshot.Restore` and still return `false` — because `Restore` recalculates internally and returning `true` would re-flow positions twice. The flag really gates "does the base class still owe a `RecalculateSegmentPositions` call". **When a base class turns a return value into a control-flow gate, document the gate, not the event that usually implies it** — otherwise the two cases that return `false` for opposite reasons (nothing changed vs. already handled) look like a bug, and a future override reading the old wording would skip a recalculation it needs.
- **Verification**: `Musio.Tests` and `Musio.App` x64 builds 0 errors; **957 passed, 3 skipped, 0 failed** on wave3 and wave4 after restacking. Content diffs against the pre-rebase backups were empty except the intended doc change.

## Blank editor: unobserved Win2D device loss on CanvasControl (preview + all tracks)

- **Feature/area**: `PreviewCanvas`, `TimelineControl`, `EditorGraphicsDeviceManager`, `EditorPage` preview/reload lifecycle.
- **Symptom**: the preview surface and every timeline track blanked at once, leaving a live but empty shell. Not a crash — the window stayed responsive.
- **Evidence that drove the diagnosis** (all four mattered): `diag.log` stopped at a routine `[Filmstrip] primary strip installed` model reload; `crash.log` was two days stale, so **no** managed exception reached `Application.UnhandledException` / `AppDomain` / `TaskScheduler.UnobservedTaskException`; `Get-WinEvent Application` had no `APPCRASH` or `RADAR_PRE_LEAK_64`; and crucially there was **no** `shared CanvasDevice lost` line, so `EditorGraphicsDeviceManager.OnGraphicsDeviceLost` never fired. A total blank with zero exception, zero log and zero device-lost notification is the signature below.
- **Root cause**: the app had **9 `CanvasControl`s (1 preview + 8 timeline tracks) and not one `CreateResources` subscription** — `grep -r CreateResources src` returned nothing. `CanvasControl` recovers from GPU device loss (TDR / driver update / display change) **internally and silently**: it builds a replacement device and raises `CreateResources` with `Reason == NewDevice`, which is the *only* notification the app gets. It does not necessarily raise `CanvasDevice.DeviceLost` on the shared device, which is all `EditorGraphicsDeviceManager` was watching. Every externally cached GPU resource — the preview `CanvasRenderTarget`, the filmstrip/waveform `CanvasBitmap`s — then belonged to the dead device, so every canvas drew nothing, forever, with no diagnostic.
- **Fix**: `PreviewCanvas` and `TimelineControl` each subscribe `CreateResources`, act only on `CanvasCreateResourcesReason.NewDevice` (**not** `FirstTime`, which fires at startup), drop the dead-device bitmaps, and raise a `DeviceRecreated` event. `EditorPage` routes both into the new public `EditorGraphicsDeviceManager.RequestRecovery(reason)`, reusing the existing queued/coalescing recovery machinery. The enum member is `NewDevice` — there is no `CanvasCreateResourcesReason.DeviceLost`, which is a compile error.
- **Second, independent bug found while reading that path — the post-recovery repaint could never draw.** `RunRecoveryAsync` holds `_graphicsRecoveryInProgress = true` for the whole of `RecoverGraphicsDeviceAsync`, and `UpdatePreviewFrameAsync` **parks** rather than renders while `IsRecoveryInProgress`. So recovery's own terminal `await UpdatePreviewFrameAsync(position, force: true)` always parked — and `_pendingRenderPosition` is drained **only** by an already-running render loop, so nothing ever drew it. Recovery had already called `Preview.ClearFrame()` + `ClearThumbnails()` + `ClearSegmentTrackVisuals()`, so it logged "recovery completed" over a blank editor. Fixed by adding a `flushPendingRenderAsync` delegate the manager invokes **after** its recovery scope closes (flag now false); `RecoverGraphicsDeviceAsync` deliberately parks the position instead of pretending to draw it. **A guard that suppresses rendering for the duration of an operation must have an explicit flush after that operation, not a call issued from inside it.**
- **Also hardened (same "silent teardown" class)**: (1) `InitializePreviewCoreAsync` disposed the reader/renderer/audio and cleared track visuals *before* checking `CurrentProject`, then returned silently if it was null — permanently blank, `_frameReader == null` so every later render returned at line 1, no retry, no log. The project is now resolved **before** any teardown and the method leaves the editor intact and logs instead. (2) The `ModelReloaded` dispatcher lambda had no `try`/`catch`, so a throw skipped `InitializePreviewAsync()` entirely. (3) `LoadAppendedTrackVisualsAsync` (fire-and-forget) had no handler, so one unreadable recording abandoned every remaining segment *and* the final `Timeline.Refresh()`; now isolated per segment.
- **What didn't work / process**: guessing the trigger from the log alone. The last entries pointed at a model reload, which sent the first pass at `ModelReloaded`, `ProjectService` null-races and the shell/navigation state machine — none were the cause. What actually settled it was inverting the question to "what can blank *every* Win2D surface with **no** exception and **no** log?", for which `grep -rn CreateResources src` returning zero matches was decisive in one command. **Let the absence of a log entry be evidence: it rules out every path that logs.**
- **Verification**: `Musio.App` and `Musio.Tests` ARM64 Debug clean builds via BuildTools MSBuild, 0 errors / 0 new warnings; suite **957 passed, 3 skipped, 0 failed** (unchanged).

## Feature branch setup: external audio import (voice-over + music)

- **Feature/area**: branch topology only — created `feature/external-audio-import` off `master` (`a455067`) and pushed to origin. No code changed yet.
- **Scoping notes for the upcoming work** (gathered so the next round doesn't re-discover them):
  - Recorded audio today is a flat `List<string> Project.AudioFilePaths` (+ per-`RecordingSource` list) with a single `AudioToVideoOffsetSeconds`. There is **no** concept of an independently-positioned audio track, so external voice-over/music needs a new model type rather than another entry in `AudioFilePaths` (which is assumed to be recording-aligned everywhere).
  - `Musio.Core/Export/ExportAudioPlan.cs` already has `AudioSourceKind.AudioFile` and an `AudioPlacement` record with `TrimFromStart`/`TakeDuration`/`Delay`, which is the natural insertion point for external tracks — but note its header comment: neither pipeline actually ramps gain, and `VideoEncoder` subtracts fades back out before muxing because `BackgroundAudioTrack` has no envelope API. Per-track *constant* volume may be possible; time-varying gain is not, in export.
  - `AudioPlaybackEngine.Load(IEnumerable<string>)` plays project-level files continuously at a constant output-time offset, not per-placement, so timeline-positioned external tracks will need a real per-track offset/mix path there.
  - Existing infrastructure to reuse: `VideoImportService` (transcode/probe pattern to mirror for audio), `AudioWaveformGenerator` (timeline lane), `AudioFileDurationProbe` (see `fix/audio-file-duration-probe`), `MusioPackage`/`MusioPathRewriter` (packaging must learn about the new paths).
- **What worked**: branch created cleanly from an up-to-date `master` with a clean tree; `git push -u` succeeded.
- **What didn't work**: n/a (no build/test run this round — no code changes to validate).

## Insert menu: external audio (voice-over + music) — `feature/external-audio-import`

- **Feature/area**: new `Musio.Core/Models/AudioTrack.cs` + `Project.AudioTracks`, new `Musio.Core/Media/AudioImportService.cs`, `ExportAudioPlan`/`AudioPlacement.Volume`/`VideoEncoder.ApplyPlacement`, `AudioPlaybackEngine.LoadPlacements`/`SyncTo`, `MusioPathRewriter`, `ProjectService.ImportAudio`/`RemoveAudioTrack`, EditorPage Insert menu + second preview engine, `TimelineControl` "Added" lane.
- **The design decision that made it tractable: inserted audio is a PROJECT-level, OUTPUT-timeline object, not another entry in `Project.AudioFilePaths`.** Everything downstream assumes those are recording-aligned (single `AudioToVideoOffsetSeconds`) and must follow the segments through trims/reorders. A voice-over is the opposite: anchored to a moment in the finished video and must NOT be re-cut.
