Because its `StartTime` is already output time, it maps to `AudioPlacement.Delay` directly and is appended to the plan *unconditionally*, identical on both the segment and legacy paths — no segment/speed/transition arithmetic at all.
- **`BackgroundAudioTrack.Volume` is the one level control export DOES have.** The long-documented "no gain envelope" limitation is about *time-varying* ramps; a CONSTANT gain muxes fine, which is exactly what a music bed needs. Added as `AudioPlacement.Volume` (defaulted to 1.0 so every recording-derived placement is unchanged, pinned by a test) and applied in `ApplyPlacement`. **Deliberately no fade fields on `AudioTrack`** — those would work in preview and be silently dropped on export, which is the trap the fade metadata on `AudioPlacement` already documents.
- **Preview needed a SECOND `AudioPlaybackEngine`, not a bigger `Load`.** The two are seeked in different clocks: recorded audio in the primary recording's own file time (`AudioPositionForVideo`, which maps through segments), inserted audio in output time. One engine seeks every reader from a single position, so it physically cannot serve both. `LoadPlacements` stores a per-reader `AudioTimelinePlacement` and `SeekCore` switches to per-reader mapping when it is non-empty; `SyncTo(outputPosition, tolerance)` does per-reader drift correction and returns whether anything sounds there (a whole-engine drift check is meaningless when tracks start at different instants).
- **The bug that would have shipped silently: NAudio's `MixingSampleProvider` REMOVES any input whose `Read` returns 0, and `MediaFoundationResampler` latches end-of-input.** A placed track is parked at EOF wherever it is silent, so every track not placed at 00:00 would have been dropped from the mixer during the *first* buffer and never played — with no error. Fixed with `SilencePaddedSampleProvider`, which zero-fills any shortfall and always returns the full count. It must wrap the **reader**, before the resampler, or the resampler latches EOF first. `ReadFully = true` does NOT save you here: it pads the mixer's *output*, after the removal has already happened.
- **What didn't work**: nothing structural, but two mechanical traps. (1) Removing the blank line after `<!-- Canvases -->` in `TimelineControl.xaml` collapsed the comment and the next element onto one line and produced `XamlCompiler error WMC0610` / XBF `0xc00cee2f` — an opaque error that points at the line, not the cause. (2) `dotnet test --no-build` after an MSBuild build works fine; `dotnet build` still fails with the PriGen `MSB4062`, and `vswhere` is absent on this host — MSBuild is at `C:\Program Files (x86)\Microsoft Visual Studio\2022\BuildTools\MSBuild\Current\Bin\MSBuild.exe`, as the previous entry recorded.
- **Verification**: `Musio.App` and `Musio.Tests` x64 Debug build with 0 errors / 0 CS warnings (app rebuilt from a cleaned `bin`/`obj` after the XAML row change, per the playbook); suite **973 passed, 3 skipped, 0 failed** (957 baseline + 16 new covering the model's duration/volume clamps, output-anchored placement, mute/silence filtering, legacy-timeline parity, the recorded-audio volume default, and the package round trip).
- **Still open for a follow-up**: no drag-to-move or trim handles on the lane (right-click gives mute/remove only), no properties-pane volume slider, and the insert is not undoable — inserted tracks live on `Project`, while `UndoRedoManager`'s operations all act on `TimelineModel`.

## Deploying the external-audio build (ARM64 Release re-register)

- **Feature/area**: build/deploy only, no code change — `feature/external-audio-import` ARM64 Release deploy.
- **This host is ARM64.** The registered package and the running process were `bin\ARM64\Release\...\win-arm64`, even though the feature work was verified with x64 builds. Build the platform that is actually registered before deploying, or `Add-AppxPackage -Register` points at a manifest that does not match the running app.
- **`Remove-Item -Recurse -Force bin,obj -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue` silently skips locked files while the app is running** — it reported success but left the ARM64 Release output half-deleted, with the registered package's `InstallLocation` still pointing into it. The playbook's "kill the process FIRST, then delete bin/obj" is not just about MSBuild `/t:Clean` failing: `-ErrorAction SilentlyContinue` turns the same problem into a silent partial delete you only notice later. Stop the process, then re-delete, then rebuild.
- **What worked**: `Stop-Process -Id <pid>` → delete `bin\ARM64`+`obj\ARM64` → MSBuild `Musio.App.csproj /restore /t:Build /p:Configuration=Release /p:Platform=ARM64` (0 errors, 0 CS warnings) → `Remove-AppxPackage` the old full name → `Add-AppxPackage -Register <AppxManifest.xml>` → `Start-Process 'shell:AppsFolder\PratikMistri.Musio_9gph0n9984scy!App'`. Verified live: PID responding, window title "Musio", `crash.log` untouched (3 days stale).
- **What didn't work**: nothing new; the only stumble was assuming the x64 verification build was what the machine ran.

## Making inserted audio editable, and splitting it into Voice / Music lanes

- **Feature/area**: `AudioTrack` moved from `Project` to `TimelineModel.AudioTracks`; new `Musio.Core/Timeline/Operations/AudioTrackOperations.cs`; `TimelineControl` gained two lanes (rows 8/9) with full drag/trim/select gestures; `EditorPage` Delete/Split accelerators and context menu.
- **The correction that mattered: an editable timeline object must live on `TimelineModel`, not `Project`.** The first pass put `AudioTracks` on `Project` (reasoning: it is a media reference). That made move/split/trim/delete impossible to do properly, because `IEditOperation.Execute/Undo` take a `TimelineModel` and `UndoRedoManager` is constructed around one — so nothing on `Project` can ever be undoable. Moving it also came free on packaging (the timeline is already serialised into the manifest) and put it next to `CameraSegments`/`TextOverlays`, which is where a reader would look for it. **Rule of thumb: if the user can drag it, it belongs on the model the undo system owns.**
- **`AudioTrack.Id` changed `Guid` -> `string` (`Guid.NewGuid().ToString("N")`)** to match every other timeline object, so it drops straight into the control's `string?` selection plumbing (`SelectedXId`, `ClearOtherSelections`) and the operations' id parameters. A `Guid` id forced conversions at every boundary for no benefit.
- **A left-edge trim is NOT a start/duration edit.** Dragging the left edge right must reveal LATER audio, so `TrimStart` advances by exactly the same delta as `StartTime`, keeping every surviving sample pinned to the timeline instant it was already at. It is also bounded on the left by `StartTime - TrimStart` — dragging further can only uncover audio the file does not have. The right edge is bounded by `AvailableAfterTrim`. Both are pinned by tests; getting either wrong makes the audio slide under the block, which is very hard to spot by eye.
- **Split is rejected, not clamped, when out of range.** `SplitAudioTrackOperation.CanSplit` gates both the operation and the menu item's `IsEnabled`. Clamping a split to the nearest legal point cuts somewhere the user did not ask for, which is worse than not cutting; the disabled menu item keeps the precondition visible.
- **Derived state must be rebuilt on undo/redo, and undo/redo does not tell you what changed.** The inserted-audio preview engine and the lane projection are both derived from `TimelineModel.AudioTracks`, and `UndoRedoManager` only mutates the model — so `Ctrl+Z` after an audio edit kept playing and drawing the undone placement. Fixed in `OnUndoRedoStateChanged`, guarded by a flattened signature (id + path + start + trim + effective duration + effective volume) so the rebuild — which reopens every inserted WAV — is skipped for the unrelated edits that also raise `StateChanged`. **The signature must include the fields an EDIT changes, not just the track ids: a move or trim leaves the set of tracks identical while changing every placement in it.**
- **Two lanes, one per `AudioTrackKind`**, so a voice-over and a music bed that overlap in time stay independently grabbable (a single lane makes the top block unclickable wherever they overlap). Both share one set of drag-state fields and one set of handlers, keyed by `ReferenceEquals(canvas, MusicTrackCanvas)` — only one block can be dragged at a time, so per-lane state would be dead weight.
- **What didn't work**: `python` is not on PATH on this host (only the Microsoft Store alias), so a scripted extract-and-replace of a large code block failed; use the `edit` tool with a large exact `old_str`, or a PowerShell regex with `(?s)`.
- **Verification**: `Musio.App` + `Musio.Tests` x64 Debug 0 errors / 0 CS warnings; suite **986 passed, 3 skipped, 0 failed** (13 new operation tests). ARM64 Release clean-rebuilt, re-registered and relaunched; running, responding, `crash.log` untouched.

## Why inserted-audio trim handles were ungrabbable (and the fix)

- **Feature/area**: `TimelineControl` inserted-audio lanes — hit testing, handle rendering; `EditorPage` context menu.
- **Root cause 1 — the right edge was literally off the canvas, and there was nowhere to scroll to.** `TimeToX` maps against `TimelineModel.DisplayDuration`, which is `TotalSegmentsDuration` — the VIDEO's length. An inserted music bed or voice-over is routinely LONGER than the footage (a 3-minute song on a 30-second recording), so its right edge sat several canvas-widths past the visible area. Hit testing the raw coordinates meant `posX > x2` was never true, so **every** press resolved to `Body`: the block could be moved but never trimmed. Fixed with `VisibleExtent`, which clamps both edges into `[0, canvasWidth]` for BOTH drawing and hit testing, so a clamped boundary doubles as a reachable trim handle.
- **Root cause 2 — the grab zone was invisible.** Handles were drawn only on the SELECTED block, so an unselected one presented an 8px strip with no affordance; users read "no handle" as "cannot trim". Handles are now drawn on every block (brighter when selected), with two small ticks past a clamped edge meaning "continues off-screen".
- **Generalisable rule: a drag affordance whose target is derived from a time→pixel mapping must be clamped to the visible viewport, or content outside the mapping's own range becomes permanently uneditable.** The other tracks never hit this because their content is BY CONSTRUCTION within the video's duration; inserted audio is the first content that can exceed it.
- **Also added a geometry-independent escape hatch**: "Trim start/end to playhead" in the right-click menu. Dragging is imprecise at low zoom (one pixel can be worth a second) and impossible for an edge far outside the viewport; the menu passes the playhead straight into the same `TrimAudioTrackOperation`, and its enable-guard mirrors the operation's own bounds.
- **Edge width is now adaptive** (`EdgeHitWidthFor` = blockWidth/3 clamped to 3..10px): a fixed 10px zone on a 20px block would leave no body to grab, making a short block impossible to MOVE — the exact inverse of the bug being fixed.
- **Drag preview**: an edge dragged past its opposite inverts the rectangle; the preview now swaps the two so the block pins to a sliver instead of vanishing mid-gesture (the operation clamps properly on release).
- **Verification**: x64 Debug 0 errors / 0 CS warnings; suite **989 passed, 3 skipped, 0 failed** (3 new trim tests, incl. the fresh-import `Duration == null` -> materialised-length case a long music bed always starts in). ARM64 Release clean-rebuilt, re-registered, relaunched; responding, `crash.log` untouched.

## Trimmed audio waveform compressed instead of clipping

- **Feature/area**: `Musio.Core/Audio/AudioWaveformGenerator.cs` (new pure `WaveformWindow`), `TimelineControl.DrawInsertedAudioWaveform`, `EditorPage.PublishInsertedAudioLane` waveform cache.
- **Symptom**: after trimming an inserted track the block redrew the ENTIRE track's waveform squeezed into the shorter block, so the picture no longer told you which part of the audio survived — the one job it has.
- **Two compounding causes.** (1) The cached peak array was stretched across the block width (`bx = x1 + i * blockW / waveform.Length`), which is only correct when the block plays the whole array. (2) The cache was keyed by **path** but generated from the track's **trim point** (`GenerateWaveform(path, n, startSeconds: trimStart)`), so it was stale by construction the moment the trim changed, and after a split both halves shared one array built for neither.
- **Fix — the waveform is a WINDOW onto the file, never a rescaling of it.** Generate once per file from second 0, cache `(peaks, fileDurationSeconds)` by path, and at draw time select the peak-index range covering `[TrimStart, TrimStart + Duration]`, positioning each peak by its own source time. This is exactly what `DrawSegmentWaveform` already does for video segments — the precedent was there and should have been followed first time.
- **Position against the UNCLAMPED block geometry, then filter to the visible span.** Mapping onto the canvas-clamped width reintroduces the identical distortion one level up: the whole waveform gets squeezed into whatever part of the block is on screen.
- **Peak budget must scale with duration.** A fixed 400 peaks over a 3-minute bed is 0.45s per peak, so trimming to 5s left ~11 peaks — a blocky, useless picture precisely when detail matters most. Now 20 peaks/second, clamped 400..6000.
- **A ceiling on the far edge of the index range is load-bearing**: a 50ms window of a long file rounds down to zero peaks and renders a blank block after an aggressive trim.
- **Extracted the arithmetic into `WaveformWindow` in Core rather than leaving it inline in the draw handler**, because a draw handler is untestable and this exact mapping had already shipped wrong once. Seven tests now pin it, including "two equal-length windows cut from the same file cover an equal number of peaks" — which is the invariant that fails if anyone reintroduces stretching.
- **Verification**: `Musio.App` + `Musio.Tests` x64 Debug 0 errors / 0 CS warnings; suite **996 passed, 3 skipped, 0 failed**. ARM64 Release clean-rebuilt, re-registered, relaunched; responding, `crash.log` untouched.

## Trim drag PREVIEW slid and squashed (the committed result was already correct)

- **Feature/area**: `TimelineControl.DrawInsertedAudioWaveform` / `GetInsertedAudioStartX`/`EndX`; `WaveformWindow.SecondsFor`.
- **Symptom, and why it was a different bug from the previous entry**: after the whole-file-window fix, the RELEASED result was correct, but during the drag the waveform slid right (left-edge drag) and squashed (right-edge drag). The user's report distinguished the two precisely — "the correct segment shows when I release the drag, but it shows wrong preview while dragging" — which is what localised it to the preview path rather than the model or the cache.
- **Root cause**: the preview changes the block's WIDTH (the dragged edge follows the pointer) while the trim window is still the committed one. Positioning peaks as a fraction of the block width therefore rescaled the unchanged window into a changing width every frame. Correct at rest, wrong for exactly the duration of the gesture — which is why it survived the previous round's fix and its tests.
- **Fix — position every peak absolutely, with no width term at all.** `x = fileOriginX + peakSourceSeconds * pixelsPerSecond`, where `fileOriginX` is the canvas x at which the file's second 0 would sit, then clip the drawing to the block rectangle. The peaks become physically stationary and the dragged edge sweeps over them, revealing or hiding audio — which is what a trim *is*. The peak range to draw is derived from the (already previewed) rectangle rather than from the trim window, so the preview is correct by construction.
- **The anchor differs by drag kind, and this is the subtle part.** For an EDGE drag it comes from the COMMITTED start — a left trim advances `StartTime` and `TrimStart` by the same delta, so `Start - TrimStart` is invariant and the file's origin genuinely does not move. For a BODY drag the audio really is travelling, so the anchor rides the previewed block. Using the previewed start for both would reintroduce the slide on left-edge drags.
- **Also clamped the preview to the operation's own bounds** (`FileOriginTime` on the left, `FileOriginTime + SourceDuration` on the right), so the block stops where the trim really stops instead of previewing an edit that snaps back on release. `SourceDuration` had to be added to the lane item for this; a test now asserts the operation clamps to exactly those two instants, so preview and commit cannot drift apart.
- **Generalisable rule: any drag preview that changes a container's size must preview the CONTENT mapping too, or express the content in coordinates the resize does not touch.** The second is far more robust — there is no width in the arithmetic to get wrong.
- **Verification**: `Musio.App` + `Musio.Tests` x64 Debug 0 errors / 0 CS warnings; suite **998 passed, 3 skipped, 0 failed** (2 new: peak source time is window-independent; trim bounds match the preview's clamps). ARM64 Release clean-rebuilt, re-registered, relaunched; responding, `crash.log` untouched.

## "Everything glitches" on project load — diagnosing an editor-wide regression report

- **Feature/area**: `EditorPage` preview-init path and inserted-audio load; `TimelineControl.ApplyTrackVisibility`.
- **The report was that the audio work had broken the whole editor. The logs said otherwise, and reading them FIRST saved an unnecessary revert.** `diag.log` showed `[Win2DUtils] Failed to allocate transition output render target (2304x987)` with an EMPTY exception message, immediately followed by eight `shared CanvasDevice lost` lines, `COMException HR=0x88990012` inside `VideoFrameReader.Clone`, then a successful `editor graphics recovery completed`. **An empty COMException message on an allocation is the device already being gone, not VRAM exhaustion** — the loss came first and every later failure is the cascade.
- **Decisive check: is the signature pre-existing?** `diag.pre-instrumentation.log` (Aug 4, before any of this work) contains the same `shared CanvasDevice lost` / `RaiseDeviceLost` sequence. So the device loss is NOT introduced by the audio feature, and the recovery machinery handled it. **Grep the archived logs for the signature before accepting "your change broke X".**
- **But the audio code WAS making a fragile load worse, in three ways, all now fixed:**
  1. `ReloadInsertedAudioPlayer()` sat in `InitializePreviewCoreAsync` immediately after the teardown block and **unguarded** — anything it threw would abort the rebuild and leave a live but blank editor with nothing scheduled to retry it, which is exactly the failure the crash-hardening playbook exists for. Now called through `TryReloadInsertedAudioPlayer()`, which logs and continues.
  2. `AudioPlaybackEngine.LoadPlacements` opens every inserted WAV **synchronously on the UI thread**, during the same window the preview decoder, filmstrip pass and canvases are all coming up. Now built on a background thread and published back through the dispatcher, with a generation counter so a superseded build is disposed rather than installed.
  3. The whole-file waveform decode raced project load for I/O. It now waits 600ms first — the blocks draw immediately, the waveform arrives a moment later.
- **`ApplyTrackVisibility` took a non-nullable `RowDefinition` but is reachable from the `InsertedAudioTracks` setter**, which the host can assign before the XAML tree is realised — a null-deref there lands inside preview init, i.e. failure mode 1. Row is now nullable and checked, matching the label/canvas parameters that already were.
- **Also cut GPU churn during audio drags**: `PointerMoved` called `InvalidateAll()`, repainting all ten canvases (filmstrip included) on every pointer move. Now only the two audio lanes repaint; the full invalidate stays on release.
- **Known, untouched, and deliberately so**: `TransitionRenderer.Render` allocates a fresh full-size `CanvasRenderTarget` per frame of a crossfade preview (ownership passes to the caller, which does dispose it). That is worth revisiting, but it is a pre-existing design in a high-churn subsystem and rewriting its ownership model blind, at the end of a long session, is exactly how the compositor rounds in this archive went wrong.
- **Verification**: x64 Debug 0 errors / 0 CS warnings; suite **998 passed, 3 skipped, 0 failed**. ARM64 Release clean-rebuilt, re-registered, relaunched; responding. Rotated `diag.log` to `diag.before-hardening.log` first so the next session's log starts clean and is attributable.

## Audio state not restored on load, and clipped edges stealing drags

- **Feature/area**: `EditorPage.LoadAudioWaveformAsync`, `TimelineControl.SyncAudioMuteVisuals`/`ResolveTrackVisual`/`HitTestInsertedAudio`.
- **Mute state "not remembered" was not a persistence bug — the flags round-tripped fine, nothing APPLIED them.** `TimelineModel.IsSystemAudioMuted`/`IsMicAudioMuted` are plain serialized properties and restore correctly, but (1) `LoadAudioWaveformAsync` called `_audioPlayer.Load(validPaths)` with EVERY path instead of `GetUnmutedAudioPaths(project)`, so a muted track came back audible, and (2) the mute-button glyphs were only ever written inside their own click handlers, so a restored project showed an unmuted icon over a muted track. **When state "isn't remembered", check whether it is being restored and then ignored before assuming it was never saved** — the serialization was innocent both times.
- **Waveforms are `[JsonIgnore]` by design** (a regenerable render cache; persisting thousands of floats would bloat every manifest), so the load path MUST rebuild them — now pinned by a test asserting they come back null, so nobody "fixes" the blank waveform by persisting the cache.
- **The blank primary waveform had a shadowing cause**: `ResolveTrackVisual` returns a registered per-file visual in preference to the model-level one, but "which file is primary" is decided there from `TimelineModel.PrimaryVideoFilePath` while the host's `LoadAppendedTrackVisualsAsync` decides it from `Project.VideoFilePath`. When those two disagree the primary gets a per-file entry registered with EMPTY waveforms, which then permanently shadows the model-level samples — blank audio tracks for the whole session, no error. Now a waveform-less registration never shadows the primary's model-level data. **Two sources of truth for "is this the primary recording" is the underlying defect; this only defuses it.**
- **Also**: `WaveformDurationSeconds` fell back to `model.Duration`, which divides every peak index by zero if a restored project never wrote it — drawing nothing, identical in appearance to "this recording has no audio". Falls back to `DisplayDuration` now, and the whole pass logs what it resolved instead of swallowing failures in a bare `catch {}`.
- **The "end stuck to the end of the video" drag bug was the clamped edge from the previous round.** Making a clipped boundary a grab target (so an off-screen edge could be trimmed) also made it *look* like the audio ended at the timeline's end, and — worse — stole every body drag that began near that boundary, so a block longer than the video could not be moved until it had first been shortened. **A boundary is not an edge.** Only real, on-screen edges are handles now; a clipped edge draws continuation ticks and no handle, and off-screen edges are trimmed from the context menu, which needs no pixel to aim at.
- **Verification**: `Musio.App` + `Musio.Tests` x64 Debug 0 errors / 0 CS warnings; suite **1000 passed, 3 skipped, 0 failed** (2 new: mute round trip, waveform cache deliberately not persisted). ARM64 Release clean-rebuilt, re-registered, relaunched; responding.

## After Effects-style trim visuals, and why they also fixed reachability

- **Feature/area**: `TimelineControl.DrawInsertedAudioLane` (dimmed full-file extent), insert-time duration clamp in `EditorPage.ImportAudioAsync`, context-menu anchoring.
- **The user asked for an AE-style visual — full opacity for the used range, dimmed-but-visible for the trimmed head/tail — and it turned out to also solve the reachability problem three previous rounds had failed to.** Once the whole source file is drawn behind the block, the trim handles no longer need to reach the file's ends: they sit at the USED range's edges, and the dimmed region shows what can still be dragged back in. The handle and the headroom are separate visuals, which is exactly what the clamped-edge experiments were conflating.
- **The remaining case — a block longer than the ruler — is now prevented at the source rather than papered over at draw time.** Insert clamps the new track's duration to the room left on the timeline (`DisplayDuration - playhead`), so its right handle is always on screen. Nothing is lost: the rest of the file is the dimmed tail and `TrimAudioTrackOperation` already allows dragging the right edge back out to `AvailableAfterTrim`. Clamped to at least `MinDuration` so inserting at the very end of the timeline cannot produce a block too small to grab.
- **The waveform code needed no change to support this**, because the previous round had already made peaks position absolutely from `fileOriginX + sourceSeconds * pixelsPerSecond`. Drawing the dimmed layer is the same call with a wider clip rectangle and a desaturated colour. **Absolute positioning kept paying off** — a width-relative implementation would have needed a second, differently-scaled mapping for the dimmed region.
- **`MenuFlyout.ShowAt(control)` anchors to the CONTROL's bounds, not the pointer.** The lane menu opened up by the preview because it was shown at `Timeline` (a tall control) rather than at the click. Fixed by carrying the canvas and the click point on the event and using `ShowAt(target, new FlyoutShowOptions { Position = ... })`; `FlyoutShowOptions` lives in `Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Controls.Primitives`, not `Controls`. Also set `e.Handled` on the right-tap so it cannot bubble into a second menu.
- **Verification**: `Musio.App` + `Musio.Tests` x64 Debug 0 errors / 0 CS warnings; suite **1002 passed, 3 skipped, 0 failed** (2 new: the right edge can be dragged back out into the trimmed tail, and an insert-clamped block keeps the whole file reclaimable). ARM64 Release clean-rebuilt, re-registered, relaunched; responding.

## Stacking overlapping inserted-audio blocks into sub-rows

- **Feature/area**: new `Musio.Core/Audio/AudioLaneLayout.cs`; `TimelineControl` inserted-audio lane layout, hit testing and drawing.
- **Symptom**: two music beds covering the same stretch of timeline drew into one band. Both waveforms became unreadable and the lower block was effectively unclickable, since the hit test returned whichever was drawn last.
- **Fix**: greedy interval packing per lane — walk blocks in start order, drop each into the first row whose previous block has already finished — and size the lane from the resulting row count. The timeline's grid row is `Auto`, so the control just gets taller; a fixed height there would have silently clipped whichever track fell past it (a comment in `EditorPage.xaml` records that exact bug from an earlier feature).
- **Greedy is optimal here, so there is nothing to gain from anything cleverer**: it uses exactly the maximum number of blocks overlapping at any single instant, which is the lower bound (those blocks share an instant, so they cannot share a row).
- **Two details that are easy to get wrong and are now pinned by tests:** (1) touching blocks — one ending exactly where the next starts — must SHARE a row (`>=`, not `>`), or the ordinary "clips laid end to end" case doubles the lane's height for nothing; (2) at the row cap, a row holds several blocks, so a row's end must never move backwards when a shorter block is added to it, or the row looks free to a block that still overlaps.
- **Extracted to Core rather than left in the draw handler because packing decides what is CLICKABLE, not merely what is pretty** — hit testing resolves the pointer to a sub-row *before* it looks at any block, so a wrong row assignment makes a block untouchable in a band it is not drawn in. Same reasoning as `WaveformWindow` two rounds earlier.
- **Row height dropped 40 -> 30 per sub-row.** The user explicitly accepted a taller track area in exchange for legibility, so the lane grows rather than compressing its blocks.
- **Verification**: `Musio.App` + `Musio.Tests` x64 Debug 0 errors / 0 CS warnings; suite **1010 passed, 3 skipped, 0 failed** (8 new packing tests). ARM64 Release clean-rebuilt, re-registered, relaunched; responding.

## Dimmed trim headroom: selection-only, and drawn as a background layer

- **Feature/area**: `TimelineControl.DrawInsertedAudioLane`, new `InsertedAudioFileOriginX` helper.
- **Feedback**: with the dimmed full-file extent drawn for EVERY block, two clips cut from the same long file washed the lane in overlapping grey — each file's extent runs under its neighbours, so it was no longer clear which headroom belonged to which clip.
- **Fix: draw it only for the SELECTED block.** The headroom is only ever actionable on the clip whose edges you can drag, and dragging requires selection, so nothing is lost by hiding it elsewhere. This is the general principle worth keeping: **a visual that exists to support a gesture should be scoped to whatever the gesture is scoped to** — showing it everywhere is not more informative, it is noise.
- **Ordering matters as much as the condition.** Drawing the extent inline with its own block would paint headroom OVER a real clip whenever the selected block sat earlier in the same sub-row, because the extent is wider than the block. It is now a background pass over the whole lane before any block is drawn, so a real clip always wins.
- Extracted `InsertedAudioFileOriginX` because the background pass and the per-block waveform now both need the same anchor, and its drag-dependent rule (committed start for an edge drag so the peaks stay stationary; previewed start for a body drag) is subtle enough that two copies would drift.
- **Verification**: `Musio.App` + `Musio.Tests` x64 Debug 0 errors / 0 CS warnings; suite **1010 passed, 3 skipped, 0 failed** (unchanged — this round is presentation only). ARM64 Release clean-rebuilt, re-registered, relaunched; responding.

## Code review of the branch diff (master...HEAD, ~4500 lines)

- **Feature/area**: review pass over the whole `feature/external-audio-import` diff; two fixes in `SplitAudioTrackOperation` and the insert path.
- **Delegated to the `code-review` agent rather than read directly** — 4491 diff lines across 20 files does not fit a single read, and the agent is purpose-built for branch diffs. Giving it the branch's own design rationale up front (audio on `TimelineModel` for undo; output-time vs source-time semantics; absolute waveform positioning; clipped edges deliberately not being handles) mattered: those all look like mistakes without the history, and a reviewer without it would have spent its budget re-litigating settled decisions.
- **It reported no defects across disposal, threading, undo/redo, boundary math, null-handling, persistence and the throw-inside-preview-init paths.** I verified the reasoning on the parts I considered highest-risk — in particular that `LoadPlacements` running on a background thread is safe because the engine it builds is unreachable until the dispatcher publishes it, so no other thread can hold a reference while its readers are being torn down.
- **Two things it correctly classified as "not defects" were still worth fixing**, and both came from asking "what does this do at the boundary?" rather than "is this wrong?":
  1. `SplitAudioTrackOperation` minted the right half's id with `Guid.NewGuid()` **inside `Execute`**, so a redo produced a different id than the original split. Anything holding that id — the selection the editor sets immediately after splitting — silently stopped resolving after undo/redo. Now allocated once at construction, matching how `AddAudioTrackOperation` and `AddCameraSegmentOperation` fix their created object's identity up front. **A create-operation that allocates identity in `Execute` is not redo-safe**; that is worth checking on every such operation in this repo.
  2. Inserting with the playhead parked at `DisplayDuration` (pressing End, or a project that ends on a clip) produced a block starting exactly at the canvas's right edge, so `VisibleExtent` collapsed it to zero width and the lane drew **nothing at all** — and the ruler cannot scroll past `DisplayDuration` to reach it. The insert appeared to silently do nothing. The start now backs off just far enough for the clip to land somewhere visible.
- **Verification**: `Musio.App` + `Musio.Tests` x64 Debug 0 errors / 0 CS warnings; suite **1012 passed, 3 skipped, 0 failed** (2 new: the right half keeps its id across undo/redo, and repeated undo/redo cycles neither accumulate blocks nor lose the original length). ARM64 Release clean-rebuilt, re-registered, relaunched; responding.
